# Strategy v2 design log (chest X-ray), 2026-10-12

Provenance file for `STRATEGY.md` v2, `STAGE1_PLAN.md` and `PROTOCOL.md`. Raw agent outputs, lightly rendered from the workflow journals; not edited for wording. Read `HANDOFF.md` first.

Contents:
1. PART 1: v2 design workflow (run id wf_7888b6dc-9f4): the synthesized v2 strategy (from three independent drafts and three judges), the synthesis rationale, the three judges' scores, the four adversarial skeptic reports (novelty, feasibility, rigor, advisor-and-ethics) with their MUST CHANGE lists, and the draft titles. The final reviser agent overran the 64,000-token output cap twice (revise:final and revise:strategy), so no machine-written final v2 exists; the synthesis plus the skeptic must-change lists were condensed by hand into STRATEGY.md v2.
2. PART 2: supplementary tracks workflow (run id wf_3c6dcabd-073): Track A (a 10-day proposal-grade deliverable; superseded by the Stage 1 results report but reused for its feasibility-evidence and outline pieces), Track C (training-based mitigation recipes with verified code, licences and compute), and the two checks (checkA, checkC) that corrected them.
3. PART 3: Stage 1 executed-study workflow (run id wf_eec5a52e-445): the nine-day Stage 1 design (tiers, day by day, runs, items, prompts, metrics, mitigation, statistics, outline, cost, risks, deferrals, first-24-hours checklist) and its feasibility check (issues, hour recount, verdict, revised core scope, revised day by day), which STAGE1_PLAN.md follows.

Wording caution: these raw outputs predate the corrections. In particular they still say "radiologist labels" in places (the rule is "RANZCR CLiP labels (43 trained labellers)"), plan a peer audit (no peer is available), and in Track A treat the Oct 22 deliverable as a proposal (superseded: the Oct 21 deliverable is a results report).


# PART 1: v2 design workflow (wf_7888b6dc-9f4)



######## SYNTHESIZED V2 STRATEGY ########

TITLE:
LongiDev-CXR: Penalty-Scored Device-Existence Hallucination in 2-4B Open Vision-Language Models on Chest Radiographs, with an Edit-Free Same-Patient Negative Arm, Verified Synthetic Device Probes, and Bias-Controlled Grounding and Retrieval Mitigation (Strategy v2, chest X-ray re-fit, 12 Oct 2026)

THESIS:
RQ1 (perception versus the ICU co-occurrence prior, without inpainting artefacts): on public NIH ChestX-ray14 radiographs re-annotated by RANZCR CLiP labellers and mapped back to their NIH filenames, do three 2026 2B generalists (Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B), a claimed-clean medical model (MedGemma-1.5-4B) and a pixel-contaminated CXR specialist next to its own base (CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B) answer 'Is a <device> present in this chest X-ray?' from the pixels or from the prior? The negative stimulus is not an edit but the same patient's other frontal study with the device absent per radiologist label (arm B, paired with the device-present study A), beside unpaired context-matched real negatives U (same other-labelled-line profile as B), a text-only, a demographics-only and a grey-image prior row, all at one native pixel size (1024x1024 NIH PNG) with visual tokens logged. The within-patient persistence rate (correct 'yes' on A that stays 'yes' on B) is the device-sensitivity readout; FAR_B stratified by other labelled lines present is the co-occurrence-prior readout. Two bounded synthetic probes make the title's 'synthetic visual data' measurable rather than assumed: a 40-image three-remover device-removal pilot (LaMa, SD-1.5 inpainting, RadEdit) behind a pre-registered validity gate, and a 100-image CPU cut-paste device insertion into B hosts behind a mirror gate; each yields a disclosed preliminary row in report v1 and a full arm in v2 only if its gate passes. RQ2 (abstention; the Kalai and HalluLens bridge): does the verbatim confidence-target instruction at t = 0.5 and t = 0.9 move abstention, FAR_B and Effective Reliability, do rankings change under Phi_9, and how far is verbal abstention from what the hero model's first-token logits could support? RQ3 (the advisor's claim 2, image-grounded): does 'general-purpose beats medical-specialised', which Alshahrani and Behzad 2026 inherit from Kim et al. 2025 (text-only Med-HALT, 11 LLMs, 350 items), hold when a CXR-post-trained model is compared with its exact base on identical device items (CheXOne minus Qwen2.5-VL-3B, the primary test, label-clean because no model lists RANZCR device labels) and when MedGemma is compared with the 2B generalists (secondary, stated as size- and encoder-confounded)? RQ4 (the advisor's claim 3): do two training-free mitigations from the umbrella's own family, detector-gated answers (MedSigLIP or CheXzero log-odds fused into cached yes/no logits) and similar-case retrieval (kNN over a patient-disjoint radiologist-labelled pool), beat their yes-ratio-matched controls (detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias sweep, Mirage OLM; kNN-vote-alone, mismatched, label-shuffled retrieval) without destroying recall and coverage, with a region-focused agree-or-abstain pass as the v2 grounding arm? Claimed contributions: C1 LongiDev-CXR, a credential-free device-existence benchmark built from a new RANZCR-to-NIH mapping, a realised-yield table, same-patient device-present/absent pairs with radiologist labels, non-expert-verified absences with kappa, four questions per image giving within-image adversarial negatives, and a released lmms-eval task yaml with abstention scoring; C2 the first per-class x visual-token-budget device-existence numbers for 2026 2B generalists and the first instructed, penalised abstention scoring on chest radiographs; C3 the first controlled, image-grounded, matched-pair test of the advisor's general-vs-medical claim at or below 4B; C4 the first grounding-vs-retrieval comparison on existence questions with the full yes-bias control set, including the first Mirage and Rethinking-CD controls on medical images and below 7B and the first detector-alone and shuffled-expert rows in CCD-style fusion; C5 measured validity numbers for device removal and insertion on radiographs (two independent verifiers, protected-region error, 2AFC detectability, a two-reader rubric with untouched controls), which no CXR editing paper reports. Every hypothesis has one pre-registered primary test, so the report is complete whichever way each result falls: a floor on every tube class is Result 1 (a per-class x budget table no paper has published, with MedGemma as positive control); a persistence rate near 1 with high FAR_B is a prior-driven finding, near 0 a perception finding; zero verbal abstention beside informative logits is a representation-action gap; a mitigation point on the base model's constant-bias curve is a bias shift, not a gain; a failed inpainting gate is the first LaMa-on-CXR detectability measurement.

WHY_UNIQUE:
(1) Edit-free same-patient device-present/absent pairs as the negative arm of a hallucination benchmark. Nearest: Lotfinia et al. 2026 (arXiv 2606.17710; image swap, target-box occlusion, equal-size irrelevant occlusion and image removal on about 2,550 MIMIC/MS-CXR finding questions at 224 px, nine systems of 4B and above, CGR/UAR/IS metrics, MIT code), DualRead 2026 (confidence drop under a substituted real image), MedVH's wrongful-image task, RoentMod (real follow-ups only as an anatomy-preservation comparator for its generator), MC-CXR (cross-patient prior images as misleading context). The medical digest verifies that no hallucination benchmark uses longitudinal device pairs as its negative arm; this plan does so per device class, radiologist-labelled on both studies, artefact-free, and is explicit that B is a within-patient natural contrast (time, disease, positioning change), not a counterfactual, which U negatives, covariates and the stratified conditional model handle. (2) First device-class x token-budget numbers for Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B, and the first 2B-generalist device numbers of any kind. Nearest: ReXVQA Table 3 (4-option MCQ; Qwen2.5-VL 65%, Phi-3.5-vision 32%, MedGemma-4B 83.9% on tubes/lines), Lotfinia 2026 (pooled yes/no at 224 px), RAD-DINO and Sabottke and Spieler 2020 (resolution effects on classifiers, no VLM curves). (3) Instructed, penalised abstention (Kalai Section 4.2 verbatim at t = 0.5 and 0.9; Effective Reliability) on CXR device existence, crossed with image source. Nearest: CARES (NeurIPS 2024 D&B; post-hoc unpenalised 'are you sure'), Med-HALT (CoNLL 2023; -0.25 pointwise penalty, text-only), HALT-MedVQA (ICLR 2024 Tiny; NOTA option), EVADE, DualRead and CONRep (selective prediction without an instructed penalty), HalluCXR (0-100 self-reported confidence, no abstention), MC-CXR (abstention forbidden). (4) Image-grounded, matched-pair test of the advisor's claim 2 at or below 4B. Nearest: Kim et al. 2025 (arXiv 2503.05777v2; text-only), MedVH (medical LVLMs 'often more' prone), CheXpercept 2026 (no perceptual advantage for medical models at larger scale), Gosai et al. ML4H 2025 (MedGemma below GPT on localisation); none compares a CXR-post-trained model with its exact base (CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B: same encoder, LLM and chat format) on device existence and abstention, and none uses labels no model trained on. (5) Detector-gated answers with detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias (Rethinking-CD), OLM (Mirage) and selective-override controls at matched yes-ratio. Nearest: CCD (Findings of ACL 2026, arXiv 2509.23379, MIT; its expert-informed stage adds a clipped log-odds bias but reports no such controls), Expert-CFG, ChatCAD, MedRAX, FactCheXcker (ETT measurement correction); Mirage and Rethinking-CD were run only on COCO at 7B and above. (6) Similar-case retrieval on frozen 2-4B models with kNN-vote-alone, mismatched, label-shuffled, k-sweep and over-reliance controls on existence questions. Nearest: RULE (EMNLP 2024) and MMed-RAG (ICLR 2025) retrieval-only ablations (7B, report corpora, later fine-tuned), When Retrieval Hurts (Sept 2026; report generation, supplies the mismatched-retrieval and copy-rate definitions), MC-CXR (misleading context, no retrieval), V-RAG entity probing, MedPromptX, CXR-RePaiR. (7) A validated AUC for the CCD/MedSigLIP 13-class device prompts (ccd/ccd_utils.py: ET_tube, NG_tube, CVC, PICC_line, Pacemaker, ICD and others over google/medsiglip-448; the README reports no accuracy) and for CheXzero's 'support devices' template on RANZCR radiologist labels; the MedSigLIP card's 0.852 support-devices AUC is on CheXpert-test labeler labels (n = 518). (8) The RANZCR StudyInstanceUID-to-NIH-filename mapping and the realised per-class pair, stratum and single-device yields, which no paper or solution repository publishes (the CLiP descriptor gives label totals, not per-image co-occurrence or per-patient state changes). (9) Measured device-removal and device-insertion validity on radiographs: LaMa, SD-1.5 inpainting and RadEdit scored by two independent verifiers (CheXzero MIMIC-only; MedSigLIP, no RANZCR listed), the Spatially Gated Diffusion protected-region check, a Sogancioglu-style 2AFC and a two-reader rubric with untouched negative controls, plus a cut-paste insertion scored by a mirror gate and a real-vs-inserted 2AFC. Nearest: Sogancioglu 2018 (2AFC on 2018 inpainters, 64x64 holes), MaskMedPaint (ML4H 2024; LaMa as a background eraser), MedDiME 2026 (arXiv 2609.15647; pacemaker removal validated by classifier flip only), Spatially Gated Diffusion 2026 (arXiv 2610.00805; two-reader rubric, RadEdit 82.1% joint validity on findings, devices kept not removed), RoentMod (no real negative controls by the authors' admission), HEAL-MedVQA (pasted region from another image), the anatomy-guided shape-insertion paper (arXiv 2501.12022; synthesis for segmentation training, not for VLM evaluation). (10) A released lmms-eval task (cxr_device_pope) with abstention metrics: lmms-eval main today ships gemma4, gemma3, qwen3_vl, qwen2_5_vl and internvl3_5 model wrappers but no medical yes/no task and no MedGemma or CheXOne entry (verified 12 Oct 2026). (11) A replication note that Kim et al.'s printed medians (76.6 vs 51.3) do not reproduce from the per-model baselines printed in the same paper (71.2 vs 36.4), protecting the advisor's inherited claim from over-statement.

ADVISOR_ALIGNMENT:
Umbrella fit: the JRCAI umbrella asks for controlled experiments that detect, categorise and reduce hallucination in open-source LVLMs on domain-specific QA using grounding and retrieval-based techniques; this plan runs exactly that in healthcare, the lab's only domain (MedCT-VLM, XAI-CLIP ROI perturbation, PULSE, the 2026-2029 trustworthy medical segmentation project; no education work and no CXR assets): six open-weight runs, a controlled stimulus design (A/B/U plus bounded synthetic probes), taxonomy-based categorisation, and grounding (Arm 1 detector fusion; Arm 3 region crop in v2) and retrieval (Arm 2) mitigation with controls. The student title is honoured literally: devices are the objects, the real arm is NIH imagery, and the synthetic data are the verified removal and insertion probes that appear in v1 as disclosed preliminary rows and in v2 as full arms if gated in. Framing: the component is the first controlled, image-grounded empirical instantiation of Alshahrani and Behzad, arXiv 2606.13211 (11 Jun 2026; KFUPM ICS and SDAIA-KFUPM JRCAI), cited first in the introduction. Claim 2 (general-purpose beats medical-specialised) becomes H2 with the CheXOne vs Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct paired difference in hallucination-free rate (1 - FAR, the paper's own quantity) and balanced accuracy on A union B as the primary endpoint, MedGemma-1.5-4B vs each 2B generalist as the secondary endpoint explicitly labelled size- and encoder-confounded (MedSigLIP encoder swap), and the gemma-3-4b-it vs MedGemma pair appendix-only; stated two-sided because Kim et al.'s evidence is text-only and ReXVQA predicts the opposite direction on devices. Claim 3 (retrieval-augmented and grounding mitigation under regulatory constraints) becomes H5 and H6 with the controls the cited RAG and grounding literature lacks, plus a lifecycle paragraph (TRIPOD-LLM and CLAIM 2024 items, WHO LMM guidance) in the discussion. Vocabulary adopted: Brooks and Anastasio, 'A Taxonomy of Machine Hallucination in Radiology' (Radiology: Artificial Intelligence 2026;8(2):e250203; full text through the KFUPM library before quoting) for the data-driven-versus-confabulation attribution of a 'yes' on a device-absent study (a 'yes' on B or U with no referent in the image is confabulation; a 'yes' on an inpainting trace is data-driven); Med-HallMark's MediHall severity tiers (arXiv 2406.10185; 6,341 samples) as optional weights; the DREAM definition (J Nucl Med 2026;67(2):166-174, PMCID PMC12866389) only for auditing generator- or inpainter-added devices in the synthetic probes; XAI-CLIP (arXiv 2602.07017) as the lab's precedent for region-level perturbation and Alansari and Luqman (KFUPM, arXiv 2510.06265) as local related work. Wording the advisor will hold the student to: 'within-patient contrast' or 'edit-free negative arm', never 'counterfactual'; 'no other labelled line' rather than 'no other device' (ECG leads and chest tubes are unlabelled in RANZCR); 'to our knowledge' on every first; no model called 'unseen' without a card citation; MedGemma-vs-2B stated as confounded with the CheXOne/base pair carrying the test; 'not for clinical use' on every edited or inserted image; the compliance sentence reads 'the pod holds NIH pixels and prompt text (which may contain aggregate label-derived counts for the retrieval arm); raw RANZCR CSV rows, polylines and StudyInstanceUIDs never leave Kaggle and the student's machine'. Citation errors fixed in a one-page memo sent to Dr. Behzad in Week 3, before any manuscript carries his name: (a) the 76.6% vs 51.3% figure must be cited as Kim et al. 2025, arXiv 2503.05777v2 (Nov 2025), 11 text-only LLMs on 350 sampled Med-HALT items (Pal et al., CoNLL 2023), U = 27 of 28, p = 0.012, never as a VLM or imaging result, with the note that the per-model baselines printed in the same paragraph give medians 71.2 and 36.4 and that Section 4.2 of the advisor's paper repeats it as p < 0.001; (b) reference [45] should read Zuo and Jiang, 'MedHallBench: A New Benchmark for Assessing Hallucination in Medical Large Language Models', arXiv 2412.18947, AAAI-25 Bridge, unreleased; (c) 'CXR-VisHal' is a QA subset of MedHEval (Chang et al. 2025, arXiv 2503.02157; 5,587 pairs on 790 images; no grounding task) and must not be cited to [45] or described as a localisation benchmark; (d) Med-HallMark is reference [4] with 6,341 samples, not 'over 20,000'; (e) the DREAM PMCID is PMC12866389 (the printed PMC11844622 is an unrelated MRI preprint) and the report defines no SUV/uptake-pattern taxonomy. Day-1 asks to the advisor in one email: broker one radiology resident or attending at IAU King Fahd Hospital of the University (Khobar) or JHAH for a two-hour blinded web-form reading in the week of Nov 9 (two weeks of lead time); approve a $30 cloud spend; approve the ACL-short-plus-appendix report format; agree to read the citation memo; say whether a PhysioNet-hosted MIMIC replication is wanted as a post-course lab asset. Lab value: the released NIH-filename item list, pair ids, masks, task yaml and PROTOCOL.md are a durable credential-free asset for the advisor's 2026-2029 project, and Table 2 is the first number his framework's open problem ('direct comparative evaluations across vision-language tasks') can cite.

BRIDGE_TO_READING_LIST:
LLM-Check (Sriramanan et al., NeurIPS 2024): the logit-level signal is core and free because the HF runner caches first-token P(yes)/P(no) on every P0 item: per-arm risk-coverage curves, AUROC, AURC, coverage at 1/5/10% risk, ECE, a threshold fixed at 5% risk on U and transferred to B and A (and to the inserted and edited probes if gated in), and the logit-versus-verbal coverage gap at equal risk (Figure 1b); the hidden-state Hidden Score (mean log singular value of the prompt hidden-state matrix at a dev-chosen layer, image and text blocks separately, visual sink tokens removed) is a kill-dated Week-5 item on the hero so LLM-Check is load-bearing in v2 if time allows and cited as the design source either way; the attention-kernel score is deleted; ReXTrust (hidden states), RadFlag (sampling), VASE (MICCAI 2025) and 'Uncertainty Is Not a Safety Net' (Findings of EMNLP 2026) are the medical positioning. Huang et al. survey (2023): object hallucination as input-conflicting hallucination driven by co-occurrence priors; the B image is the purest co-occurrence probe available (an ICU-looking study with ECG leads and a central line but no endotracheal tube), and H1 quantifies the prior as FAR_B stratified by other labelled lines against the text-only, demographics-only and grey rows and against FAR_U. Xu et al., 'Hallucination is Inevitable' (2024): backdrop only; the consequence drawn is that calibrated abstention, not elimination, is the target, which is why P1/P2 and Phi_c are core and why the advisor's 'lifecycle not checklist' framing is used; Theorem 1 is cited once, no number is presented as a test. Kalai et al., 'Why Language Models Hallucinate' (2025; Nature 2026): (a) accuracy-only yes/no scoring rewards guessing, instantiated by HalluCXR, CheXpercept and MC-CXR, which forbid or ignore abstention; (b) the Section 4.2 instruction is quoted verbatim at t = 0.5 (penalty 1) and t = 0.9 (penalty 9) and scored as Effective Reliability with c = t/(1-t) (footnote on the paper's own 't = 0.75 (penalty 2)' slip, since the formula gives 3); (c) the sentence licensing 'text or multimodal inputs' is quoted; (d) Med-HALT's -0.25 pointwise penalty and HALT-MedVQA's NOTA option are cited as the medical ancestors. HalluLens (Meta 2025): NonExistentRefusal gets its image-grounded analogue, an existent device class with a verified-absent instance, as FAR_B and FAR_U with hit_A (positive recall) as the informativeness guard; the open-ended 'Describe the endotracheal tube in this chest X-ray' arm on B with the A control is a kill-dated extension because yes/no scoring needs no LLM judge. GitHub topic pages: NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination is the Week-1 currency check and the release target (pull request in Week 6).

DATA_PLAN:
DATASETS AND ACCESS (credential-free critical path; every link verified in the sweeps on 12 Oct 2026; terms pages re-read and screenshotted on Day 0). (1) NIH ChestX-ray14: 112,120 PNG 1024x1024 frontal images, 30,805 patients; NIH Box (https://nihcc.app.box.com/v/ChestXray-NIHCC) or the Kaggle mirror nih-chest-xrays/data (JSON-LD CC0) attached to the mapping notebook; terms 'no restrictions on the use of the NIH chest x-ray images' plus three attributions (link to the Box site, cite Wang et al. CVPR 2017, acknowledge the NIH Clinical Center); metadata Data_Entry_2017_v2020.csv (Image Index, Finding Labels, Follow-up #, Patient ID, Patient Age, Patient Gender, View Position, OriginalImage[Width,Height], OriginalImagePixelSpacing[x,y]); the 14 NLP labels are covariates only, never ground truth (Oakden-Rayner 2020; Rafferty and Rajan: expert agreement about 0.5). Pixels for every arm come from here, so the pod only ever holds NIH PNGs and prompt text. (2) RANZCR CLiP (Kaggle competition ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification; late join after accepting the rules; data 'Subject to Competition Rules'; rules text unread by every agent, so on Day 0 the student reads, screenshots and quotes the Data Access and Use and Data Security clauses into PROTOCOL.md and plans on non-commercial research use with no redistribution of labels, polylines or JPEGs): 30,083 radiographs that are NIH ChestX-ray14 images 'upscaled to original resolution before conversion to JPEG' (Tang et al., Sci Data 2021, PMC8553862), re-annotated by 43 RANZCR-affiliated labellers; train.csv (StudyInstanceUID, PatientID, 11 binary labels; 50,612 labels in total, which are label counts, not image counts), train_annotations.csv (17,999 polylines for ETT/NGT/CVC on about 9,095 images), 3,791 patients (7.9 images per patient), about 15% labeller discrepancy on re-review (the real-arm label-noise figure). Per-image co-occurrence is unpublished; the descriptor's totals imply at least 12,452 images carry no ETT/NGT/Swan-Ganz label. (3) CheXmask v1.0.0 (PhysioNet open access, CC BY 4.0): lung and heart RLE masks keyed by ChestX-ray8 Image Index (keep Dice RCA (Mean) >= 0.7); used for the insertion midline, keep masks, Arm-3 crop regions and covariates. (4) ChestX-Det (Apache-2.0; github.com/Deepwise-AILab/ChestX-Det-Dataset; zips at resource.deepwise.com; 3,578 NIH images; renumbered file names): bridge row only. (5) Object-CXR: second reserve class (above). (6) GEMeX-VQA JSONL (HF BoKelvin/GEMeX-VQA, ungated, no licence tag) and ReXErr-v1 (PhysioNet open access, ODC-By): question-wording and distractor templates only (Add Medical Device, Change Name of Device, Change Position of Device); their images need MIMIC credentials and are not used. (7) MedRAG corpora (StatPearls and textbooks; public-domain notice) only if Arm 4 is ever revived. (8) PhysioNet family (MIMIC-CXR-JPG, Chest ImaGenome tubes/lines attributes, MIMIC-CXR-VQA, VinDr-CXR, MedVH, MC-CXR, HalluCXR data): account, CITI 'Data or Specimens Only Research' and credentialing filed on Day 0 as background; June 2026 'significant delays' notice; Sept 2025 policy forbids online LLM APIs and prefers local models; images may not be shown to uncredentialed readers; no derived images released outside PhysioNet; never on the critical path. (9) ReXVQA / ReXGradient-160K (HF gated non-commercial DUA, no CITI): optional external anchor and the fallback real set if the Kaggle rules forbid the planned use. (10) CANDID-PTX is closed to non-NZ users since Feb 2024 (why chest tubes are not a class). MAPPING PROCEDURE (Day 1, one Kaggle notebook with both datasets attached, CPU): first-pass key = SHA-256 of the decoded uint8 pixel buffer for any RANZCR JPEG whose size equals an NIH original (expected rare because of JPEG re-encoding), then the dimension key: read (width, height) of all 30,083 JPEGs with PIL .size without decoding and join to OriginalImage[Width,Height] (the 'upscaled to original resolution' hypothesis, tested in the first hour); resolve multi-candidate keys with 256-px pHash and dHash (Hamming threshold chosen on 200 known pairs; SSIM on ties); 200-pair visual spot check; log unmatched and ambiguous UIDs and exclude them; if the dimension key fails outright, hash all 112,120 NIH images at 256 px (about 40 CPU-minutes) and match on hash alone; rescale polyline coordinates by (1024/W, 1024/H); record SHA-256 of every input file and the notebook version; the mapping table is itself a releasable artefact (NIH filename to an opaque pair id; UIDs withheld). SELECTION RULES (pre-registered): frontal only; presence per class = any positive among that class's RANZCR columns; absence = no column of that class positive; strata per image: S1 = class present with a polyline available (A candidates; source of the removal pilot), S2 = class absent and at least one other labelled line present (hard negatives; the co-occurrence stratum), S3 = class absent and no labelled line at all (easy negatives; insertion hosts if B placement fails), S4 = longitudinal pairs (same Patient ID with the class present in one frontal study and absent in another), S5 = the single-device stratum (exactly one of ETT/NGT/CVC/Swan-Ganz present, polyline available, frontal, NIH-matched), reported because the brief requires it. PAIRS: for each patient with both states for the class, one A (present; prefer polyline-available) and one B (absent; same NIH View Position; nearest Follow-up #), chosen at random among eligible, recording direction (device added or removed) and the follow-up gap; one pair per patient. U: class-absent images from patients outside every pair, one per patient, sampled to match B's distribution of other-labelled-line presence (S2/S3 mix). Four questions per image (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz) keyed to the RANZCR columns, the pair's class as the primary question. Dev split: 40 patients (80 images) outside the test set. Retrieval pool: all remaining mapped train images from patients outside test and dev (expected over 10,000) with labels. Targets: primary class 300 pairs (minimum 200), secondary 150 (minimum 100), CVC 100 (minimum 50), U 300 for the primary and 150 for the secondary class; about 1,550 images and 6,200 items per prompt. RELAXATION LADDER (in order, each rung pre-registered): allow view mismatch with view as a covariate; allow up to two pairs per patient with patient as the cluster; pool ETT and NGT for the primary test; switch the primary class to the one with the largest realised pair yield; unpaired A/U design (pacemaker branch). REALISED-YIELD PROCEDURE (Day 1-2, before any power claim): in pandas on the mapped train.csv compute per class: images with the class present, unique patients, polyline-available images, patients with both states, view-matched pairs, S1-S5 counts, U pool, retrieval pool; print the table per relaxation rung; hash the chosen pair list and the dev split and register them on OSF before any model output is inspected; the Week-2 pilot discordance from 35 pairs through the hero fixes N by the McNemar power formula (alpha 0.05, power 0.8; reference points 1,742 pairs for a 3-point difference at discordance 0.20, 940 pairs for 5 points at 0.30, 233 pairs for 10 points at 0.30): 300 B items per class power the primary-question contrasts for about 8-10 points, and pooling the four questions per image (about 1,200 items per arm) for about 5 points, with the design effect from patient clustering reported. CONTAMINATION MATRIX (verified from cards and papers; Appendix Table A1; never claimed absent): ChestX-ray14 pixels seen by CheXagent-2-3b and CheXOne (CheXinstruct and CheXinstruct-v2 list ChestXray14 and Object-CXR, not RANZCR or ChestX-Det), NV-Reason-CXR-3B (ChestX-ray14, MIMIC, CheXpert), Lingshu-7B, RadEdit (NIH, MIMIC, CheXpert) and Cheff/MaCheX; MedGemma 4B and 1.5: the medical digest contains two contradictory reads of the same card (one read lists ChestX-ray14 under the card's dataset-ownership section; the other finds CXR14 only in evaluation tables and the tech report's Table 1), so the student confirms by eye on Day 0, quotes the 'Dataset overview' and 'Dataset ownership' sections verbatim, and the matrix carries whichever reading the card supports; XReal is MIMIC-only; Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B are 'unknown' (web-scale crawls plausibly include the Kaggle and HF mirrors and RANZCR notebooks); RANZCR device labels are listed by no model; ChestX-Det is folded into the NIH column. Contamination-sensitive readouts: the text-only blind row (label leakage through text), the A-vs-U comparison (both real) as a null reference, and the real-minus-inserted hit-rate gap and real-minus-edited FAR gap per model if the synthetic probes pass their gates; the general-vs-medical test is label-clean for devices regardless of pixel exposure. LICENCES: NIH attribution-only; RANZCR competition rules (non-redistribution assumed until read); CheXmask CC BY 4.0; ChestX-Det Apache-2.0; Object-CXR research and non-commercial, no redistribution; GEMeX-VQA no licence tag (templates only); ReXErr-v1 ODC-By; MedGemma and MedSigLIP HAI-DEF (gated auto); CheXzero MIT (weights via the README Google Drive link); BiomedCLIP MIT; ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes MIT code with research-only weights; LaMa Apache-2.0 via IOPaint (archived Aug 2025, installable); stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-inpainting (creativeml-openrail-m, ungated; verified 12 Oct 2026); microsoft/radedit MSRLA weights with an MIT pipeline, gated click-through; CheXOne CC BY-NC on the card and CC BY-NC-ND in the paper; Qwen2.5-VL-3B Qwen Research License; Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5, Gemma 4 Apache-2.0. COMPLIANCE DESIGN: RANZCR CSVs and JPEGs stay on Kaggle and the student's machine; the pod receives NIH PNGs and prompt text (which may contain aggregate label-derived counts in the retrieval arm) only; scoring joins the labels locally; released artefacts rebuild the key from the Kaggle CSV after rule acceptance.

PROTOCOL:
These rules become PROTOCOL.md v1.0 in Week 1 and are mirrored in the OSF registration. HARNESS: one HF runner per model family (one venv per family at pinned transformers versions; Qwen3-VL and Gemma 4 on >= 4.57; CheXOne from main; MedGemma via the gemma3 wrapper or the runner) that generates greedily and caches the first-token logits of the yes/no/abstain surface forms in the same forward; a custom lmms-eval task cxr_device_pope (copy of pope.yaml; local NIH-PNG dataset; metrics accuracy, balanced accuracy, yes-ratio, abstention, Phi_c) at a pinned commit (main ships gemma4, gemma3, qwen3_vl, qwen2_5_vl and internvl3_5 wrappers, no MedGemma or CheXOne entry, verified 12 Oct 2026) validates the runner at >= 99% argmax agreement on 100 items per model before any cached number is reported and runs the SLAKE closed X-ray anchor; batch_size 1 (4 only if the Day-1 test shows identical outputs); never mix lmms-eval and VLMEvalKit numbers; the task yaml is released. PRECISION: every headline number from HF models in bf16 on the native-bf16 pod (RTX A5000 or 3090); Kaggle T4 fp32 only as a backup for the two Qwen3-backbone 2B models (fp16 is unsafe for the Gemma family and Qwen2-VL and unverified for Qwen3-VL/InternVL3.5; MedGemma 8-bit produces gibberish); NF4 on the GTX 1060 only for triage; a precision column in every table; GGUF and vendor numbers only in labelled appendix tables. IMAGES AND TOKEN BUDGET: every arm is a 1024x1024 8-bit grayscale NIH PNG replicated to RGB at load time, so pixel size is identical across A, B, U, edited and inserted images by construction; fixed family settings in the core: qwen3_vl min_pixels = max_pixels = 1,048,576 (1,024 tokens), internvl3_5 max_num = 6 with thumbnail (realised tokens logged), gemma4 visual budget 1120, medgemma fixed 896 px to 256 tokens, CheXOne and Qwen2.5-VL-3B max_pixels 1024*1024 with 512*512 as the recommended-setting sweep point; tokens per sample logged (image_grid_thw for the Qwen family, tiles x 256 plus thumbnail for InternVL, the soft-token budget for Gemma 4, 256 for MedGemma) and asserted equal across arms for the same image; resolution is a design factor only in the Day-2 pilot (three budgets; Figure 1a, H7) and the hero 2x2 (256/391/640/1,024 tokens); any 512-px RadEdit output is resampled to 1024 with one documented resampler applied identically to its untouched control. DECODING: greedy (do_sample = False, num_beams = 1, temperature 0); max_new_tokens 16 for all yes/no prompts (32 for CheXOne only if it emits a boxed answer; truncations counted), 128 for the gated open-ended arm; thinking and reasoning modes off; seeds fixed. PROMPTS: P0 'Is a {device} present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.' (Lotfinia's wording as the digest records it; the exact string is confirmed against github.com/mahshadlotfinia/causal on Day 0 and 'visible' is kept as a reliability paraphrase); P0b 'Is there evidence of a {device} in this chest X-ray?' (HalluCXR wording; hero and MedGemma); P1 '... Answer with yes, no, or cannot tell.'; P1b paraphrase with 'I don't know' and reordered options; P2 = the Kalai Section 4.2 sentence verbatim ('Answer only if you are >{t} confident, since mistakes are penalized {t/(1-t)} points, while correct answers receive 1 point, and an answer of I don't know receives 0 points.') at t = 0.5 and t = 0.9 followed by the question and 'Answer with yes, no, or I don't know.'; PN 'Is it true that there is no {device} in this chest X-ray?' with flipped labels; BLIND-text = question only (P(yes) per class per model); BLIND-demo = question plus 'This is an {AP/PA} chest radiograph of a {age}-year-old {sex}.' from NIH metadata; BLIND-grey = mid-grey 1024x1024 PNG; device names 'endotracheal tube', 'nasogastric tube', 'central venous catheter', 'Swan-Ganz catheter'; four questions per image; P0, P1 and P2-hi on every run, P2-lo, PN, P0b and BLIND-demo on the hero and MedGemma; retrieval and detector prompts as specified in the arms. PARSING: case-insensitive regex for yes / no / cannot tell / don't know / not sure / unsure / unknown; unparsable and truncated outputs counted and 100 hand-inspected; no LLM judge in the core; the gated open-ended arm uses a rule classifier validated on 200 hand labels with a capped ($2) batch cross-check only on NIH-derived text. LABELS: RANZCR radiologist labels are the labels of record (presence = any positive column of the class; absence = none; NGT incompletely imaged = present, flagged); NIH NLP labels are covariates only; non-expert verification by the student on every B and U image and every primary-class A image, disclosed on every table, with ambiguous items flagged and excluded from tests; blinded second rater on 200 items with Cohen's kappa (target >= 0.8), PABAK, adjudication and Wilson intervals on adjudicated accuracy per arm; the about-15% RANZCR re-review discrepancy cited; edited items absent-by-edit and inserted items present-by-insertion with every co-occurring label re-verified; ground truth is never a detector output. VERIFIER INDEPENDENCE: MedSigLIP (no RANZCR, no NIH listed) and CheXzero (MIMIC-only) are the only models allowed to verify edited or inserted images and to act as the Arm-1 expert; torchxrayvision -mimic_ch flags drift only and never judges device absence (it has no support-devices head); ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes (in-sample on RANZCR plus 31,448 NIH images; no fold file) may only refine masks and aid placement on the probe subsets; the Arm-1 expert is never the detector that selected an item's label. DEV SPLIT: 40 patients (80 images) outside the test set for every regex, threshold, dilation radius, beta, gamma, tau, k and layer choice; pair lists and the dev split hashed and OSF-registered before any model output is inspected. YES-BIAS: yes-ratio on every run; PN acquiescence shift; BLIND-text, BLIND-demo and BLIND-grey rows; FAR_U as the trace-free, patient-free reference and FAR_B(other labelled lines present) as the context-matched reference; in the mitigation tables greedy, OLM, constant-bias sweep, shuffled-expert, sign-randomised perturbation, kNN-vote-alone, mismatched and shuffled retrieval, random and wrong-region crops; every gain reported net of its yes-ratio-matched control. CONTAMINATION: the matrix with the Day-0 MedGemma card quotes; the text-only row as the label-leak check; the A-vs-U null reference; the real-minus-inserted and real-minus-edited gaps if the probes pass; no model called 'unseen' without a card citation; the general-vs-medical test is label-clean because no model lists RANZCR device labels. PATIENT-LEVEL CLUSTERING: the patient is the cluster for every pair and every test (one pair per patient by default; two per patient only on the pre-registered rung, still clustered); U items one per patient. INFORMATIVENESS: hit_A beside FAR_B and FAR_U, hit_inserted beside FAR_edited, coverage beside risk, Phi_c, over-reliance and copy rates, TAR beside FAR_open if the open-ended arm runs. METRICS: accuracy, balanced accuracy, precision, recall, F1, yes-ratio, hit_A, FAR_B, FAR_U, FAR_edited and FAR_irr-flip (gated), hallucination-free rate, paired flip rate A to B, persistence rate (Lotfinia CGR analogue), co-occurrence stratum, UAR via swapped images, IS via E-irr, abstention rate, risk, coverage, Phi_c = (#correct - c x #wrong)/N at c in {1, 3, 9} (Kalai's rule at c = t/(1-t); Whitehead's Effective Reliability for 0/1 items), guessing premium GP_c = c x err_P0, CARES over-confidence ratio (secondary), optional MediHall-weighted score, ordered rankings with pairwise flip counts; selection: AUROC, AURC, coverage at 1/5/10% risk, ECE, transferred-threshold risk and coverage, logit-vs-verbal coverage gap. STATISTICS: Wilson 95% interval on every proportion with n; one pre-registered primary endpoint and test per hypothesis: H0 floor gate; H1 co-occurrence prior (FAR_B with vs without other labelled lines, patient-clustered BCa bootstrap, 9,999 resamples); H2 general vs medical (primary = CheXOne minus Qwen2.5-VL-3B paired difference in hallucination-free rate and balanced accuracy on A union B; secondary = MedGemma vs each generalist, Holm across generalists, labelled confounded); H3 penalty effect (abstention and FAR_B under P2-hi vs P1 vs P0, McNemar on abstain indicators); H4 ranking change under Phi_9 vs P0 accuracy; H5 Arm 1 net gain vs matched control by arm; H6 Arm 2 net gain (relevant k = 3 vs greedy, expected null-to-negative) and harm (mismatched vs greedy); H7 resolution (monotone gain with budget for thin classes, CVC lowest); H8 (gated) synthetic probes (FAR_edited vs FAR_B vs FAR_U; hit_inserted vs hit_A); pooled mixed-effects logistic correct ~ arm x model + (1 | patient) + (1 | image) beside the per-model tests; exact McNemar as the secondary check everywhere; Holm within each hypothesis family only; bayes_evals for cells under 300 items; N fixed from the 35-pair pilot discordance by the McNemar power formula (alpha 0.05, power 0.8); design effect reported; reliability block (split-half curve, retest, P1b and P0b agreement, PN consistency). PRE-REGISTRATION: OSF registration by Oct 17 of H0-H8, the primary-class decision rule and branches, the relaxation ladder, the N rule, the verifier-independence rule, the probe gates and every extension's kill date; the floor pilot registered as exploratory; N and the realised yield added as a registered amendment after the Week-2 pilot; any deviation logged with its date. REPRODUCIBILITY: per-item JSONL with model id, precision, device, token count, prompt id and hash, image hash, seed and wall time; task yaml, runners, pinned requirements, PROTOCOL.md, OSF link, mapping code, rebuild script and token logs released; labels joined at build time from the Kaggle CSV. COMPLIANCE: the pod holds NIH pixels and prompt text (which may carry aggregate label-derived counts in the retrieval arm) only; RANZCR files never leave Kaggle and the student's machine; no PhysioNet data anywhere in the core.

ETHICS_AND_RELEASE:
Secondary use of de-identified public research data with no human-subject interaction, no PHI, no re-identification attempt and no clinical claim; the only new human data are the student's and raters' labels and, if obtained, a clinician reader's ratings, which may make the reader a research participant and is flagged to the IRB. Week 1 actions: obtain the NCBE certificate (ncbeportal.kacst.gov.sa; required for every team member by the KFUPM IRB); email IRB@kfupm.edu.sa a one-page exemption/expedited determination request citing NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Articles 10.19 (minimal risk; previously collected, non-identifying records) and 10.33 (publicly available, non-identifying data), the datasets' own IRB statements (RANZCR: IRB waived because the source data are public; NIH de-identified by the NIH Clinical Center), the 'no new human data' sentence and the reader-session plan; state the determination (or 'pending, submitted on <date>') in the report; keep the Kaggle rule acceptance screenshot, HAI-DEF acceptances and the RadEdit click-through as records; budget a week for the answer and never block on it. Reader session: brokered by the advisor at IAU King Fahd Hospital of the University (Khobar) or JHAH; two-hour blinded web-form reading on NIH images only (no DUA barrier); consent text and a one-page ethics note prepared; the reader sees only NIH pixels; no online second-opinion services. Data terms: NIH attribution trio (link to the Box site, Wang et al. CVPR 2017 citation, NIH Clinical Center acknowledgement) on every release and in the paper; RANZCR CLiP used under the Kaggle competition rules accepted on the student's own account (clauses copied into PROTOCOL.md on Day 0; JPEGs, train.csv, train_annotations.csv and StudyInstanceUIDs kept inside Kaggle and the student's machine; never redistributed, nor edited versions of the JPEGs; the report states that the real arm is public NIH imagery re-annotated by RANZCR-affiliated labellers, not Australian hospital imagery); Object-CXR, if ever used, non-commercial research only with no redistribution; CheXmask CC BY 4.0 with citation; ChestX-Det Apache-2.0; ReXErr-v1 ODC-By; GEMeX-VQA no licence tag (wording only); PhysioNet data, if ever obtained, used only on local hardware (the Sept 2025 policy forbids online LLM APIs; whether community pods satisfy the security clause is unresolved, so none are used), shown to no uncredentialed reader, reported in aggregate, no derived images released outside PhysioNet. Model terms cited in the methods: HAI-DEF (MedGemma, MedSigLIP: research use, outputs usable, no clinical use, derivatives carry the terms); MSRLA (RadEdit: research-only, no deployed use); CC BY-NC(-ND) (CheXOne; evaluation only, no redistributed derivatives); Qwen Research License (Qwen2.5-VL-3B); NVIDIA OneWay non-commercial (NV-Reason-CXR-3B if used); MIT (CheXzero, BiomedCLIP, CCD code; ianpan code with research-only weights); Apache-2.0 (Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5, Gemma 4, torchxrayvision, LaMa); creativeml-openrail-m (SD-1.5 inpainting); no model outputs redistributed as training data. Release (GitHub and an HF dataset card): NIH filenames of every item with the attribution trio, pair ids, prompts, question templates, per-item model outputs, CheXmask-derived masks and device masks keyed by NIH filename, the student's artefact and ambiguity flags, edited and inserted NIH-derived images if the Kaggle rules read on Day 0 do not forbid derived works (otherwise masks, seeds and scripts only), scripts, task yaml, PROTOCOL.md, OSF link, statistics, and a rebuild script that joins RANZCR train.csv after the user accepts the Kaggle rules; NEVER RANZCR CSV rows, polylines or StudyInstanceUIDs, Object-CXR images, PhysioNet derivatives, or model outputs repackaged as training data. Edited and inserted radiographs are labelled synthetic in filenames and metadata, marked 'not for clinical use', never presented as genuine; the DREAM-definition audit is reported. Reporting: TRIPOD-LLM modular items and CLAIM 2024 items where applicable; WHO LMM guidance for the responsible-AI framing; an explicit limitations block that ground truth is the radiologist annotation, that presence and absence of tubes and lines were verified by trained non-radiologist raters with inter-rater statistics, that B is a within-patient contrast not a counterfactual, that no diagnostic claim is made, that any finding-level row is radiologist-labelled but never edited, and that contamination is bounded by the matrix, not removed. Cost and environment: cloud spend logged against the $40 ceiling; the pod volume deleted at the end.

VENUE_PLAN:
Guaranteed: the RES201 individual paper-format report (v1 complete by Nov 8 for scenario A, v2 by Nov 22 for scenario B), with a parallel JURI (KFUPM, rolling) submission. Paper targets in order of fit: ML4H 2027 Findings (about Sep 2027; 4 pages, non-archival, mentorship; the advisor as co-author; the credible-unit template of HalluCXR, RadFlag and the ML4H 2024 single-author uncertainty study) as the primary medical venue; MIDL 2027 short paper (3 pages, spring 2027; welcomes negative and early-stage results, so a floor table, a null mitigation or a failed inpainting gate still fits); EMBC 2027 (contributed papers due Jan 24, 2027; IEEE 4+1 pages) as the first firm deadline after finals, frozen from the v2 text by Dec 10; NAACL 2027 Student Research Workshop or workshops (Feb 5, 2027 window; SRW mentorship request Nov 16; the SRW call was not published on Oct 12, so confirm) for the abstention-scoring angle; BioNLP 2027 (about Apr 2027, ACL window) as the ACL-side home. Opportunistic: EACL 2027 SRW mentorship request on Nov 6 only if report v1 exists; its direct submission (Dec 15) falls inside finals (Dec 13-24) and is used only if the manuscript is final by Dec 10. Infeasible or unverified: ISBI 2027 (Oct 26, 2026 deadline), AIIT 2027 Jeddah (Oct 20 listed deadline, calendar unverified). Positioning sentence for every submission: 'Unlike HalluCXR, MedVH, HEAL-MedVQA and Lotfinia et al., which probe presence on unmodified, swapped or box-occluded images with NLP-derived or coarse labels and no instructed abstention, we evaluate device existence on the same patient with and without the device at one pixel size, score penalised abstention, measure rather than assume the validity of synthetic device removal and insertion, and test grounding- and retrieval-based mitigation under yes-bias controls on 2-4B open models, including the first matched-pair, image-grounded test of the general-vs-medical claim.' Lab asset: a PhysioNet-hosted MIMIC replication (MedVH precedent) and a findings-level extension with a reader are post-course items for the advisor's 2026-2029 project.

OBJECT CLASSES:
  - PRIMARY CANDIDATE 1 (preferred): endotracheal tube (ETT). RANZCR CLiP image-level labels ETT normal/borderline/abnormal (7,240/1,138/79 labels; presence = any column positive) and polylines in train_annotations.csv; about 10 mm radiopaque midline stripe, about 25 px wide at 1024 px (roughly one Qwen3-VL token); a temporary ICU device, so same-patient present/absent studies should be plentiful (RANZCR averages 7.9 images per patient); highest tube DICE for the Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR specialist (0.800). Verification note: tracheostomy tubes are not ETTs and are flagged ambiguous.
  - PRIMARY CANDIDATE 2 / SECONDARY: nasogastric tube (NGT). Labels normal/borderline/abnormal/incompletely imaged (4,799/530/267/2,748); about 12 px at 1024 px; 'incompletely imaged' counted as present (tube visible, tip not) with a flag and a sensitivity analysis excluding it; the CLiP descriptor notes side holes are often invisible; also temporary, so pairs exist; lowest specialist DICE (0.782). Reported in every table, primary only under branch (ii).
  - HARD CLASS (resolution analysis only, never primary): central venous catheter (CVC) including PICC. Labels 21,323/8,460/3,198; 1-6 px wide at 512-1024 px, sub-token at 512 px for Qwen3-VL (32 px per token) and at MedGemma's fixed 896 px to 256 tokens; kept as 100 pairs (minimum 50) to produce the per-class x token-budget floor table (Figure 1a, H7) and the thin-line result; its failure is a reportable finding. Faint CVCs flagged 'ambiguous' by the student are excluded from tests.
  - ADVERSARIAL-NEGATIVE-ONLY QUESTION: Swan-Ganz catheter (830 labels; co-occurs with CVC). Asked on every image as the fourth question; almost always 'no', so it is a co-occurrence-prior probe; no pairs are built for it; its yes-rate is reported beside FAR_B.
  - RESERVE CLASS (only under branch (iv)): pacemaker/ICD generator, about 5 cm, highest contrast ('cardiac pacer and wires' in Chest ImaGenome vocabulary). Not labelled in RANZCR, so candidates are shortlisted on the mapped NIH images with the CCD/MedSigLIP 'Pacemaker'/'ICD' prompt pairs, hand-checked by the student and a peer (non-expert labels disclosed; a generator box is unmistakable), with a random 200-image sample from the non-shortlisted tail hand-checked to estimate shortlist recall; verifier-independence rule: MedSigLIP may only shortlist, the Arm-1 expert on these items must be CheXzero, and no detector output is ever a label. Longitudinal pairs are rare (pre- vs post-implant), so this branch uses the unpaired A/U design with patient clustering. Budget 1-2 days of person-time, Week 2 only.
  - SECOND RESERVE: external foreign objects (Object-CXR, JF Healthcare, MIDL 2020: 5,000 positives with boxes/ellipses/polygons by 12 radiologists plus 5,000 negatives; terms 'free to download for scientific research and non-commercial usage'; original site 404; mirror github.com/hlk-1135/object-CXR with an untested Google Drive link; Academic Torrents 13.64 GB). Tested on Day 0 and recorded; used only if both tube classes and the pacemaker branch fail; never redistributed; label-contaminated for CheXOne and CheXagent (CheXinstruct lists Object-CXR).
  - BRIDGE (gated Week-5 extension, real arm only, no edits): coarse findings from ChestX-Det (Apache-2.0; 3,578 NIH images; boxes and polygons for 13 classes by three board-certified radiologists; files are renumbered, so a pixel-hash mapping to NIH names is needed): pleural effusion and consolidation presence questions on 200-300 images for the hero, MedGemma and CheXOne, as the comparability row to HalluCXR and CheXpercept, the devices-vs-findings contrast, and a fully releasable sample; the student cannot verify findings, so radiologist polygons are the only label and no finding is ever edited. FINDINGS-LEVEL FALLBACK only if every device branch fails: the same protocol on ChestX-Det real images with the limitation stated.
  - GO / NO-GO RULE (pre-registered on OSF before the pilot runs; the primary class is registered after it). Day-2/3 pilot per model x class: 100 device-positive and 100 device-negative real NIH images (one image per patient; half of the negatives with another labelled line present) at three token budgets (Qwen3-VL-2B min_pixels = max_pixels at 196/784/1,024 tokens; InternVL3.5-2B max_num 1/4/6 tiles plus thumbnail; Gemma 4 E2B visual budget 280/560/1,120; CheXOne and Qwen2.5-VL-3B max_pixels 512*512 and 1024*1024; MedGemma-1.5-4B and medgemma-4b-it fixed at 896 px to 256 tokens) with the P0 prompt. GO for a model-class pair if balanced accuracy >= 0.70, hit rate on positives >= 0.70 and yes-rate within 0.25-0.75 at the best budget (Wilson 95% half-width about 0.07 at n = 200, so 0.70 excludes chance); CONDITIONAL at 0.60-0.70 (raise the budget or move to the next class); NO-GO below 0.60 or yes-rate below 0.10 or above 0.90 (constant-answer policy, as LLaVA-Med-7B showed in Lotfinia et al., retaining 100% of its correct answers without the image). Positive controls: MedGemma-1.5-4B and medgemma-4b-it (ReXVQA tubes/lines 83.9%, implanted devices 73.1%); classifier references on the same images: MedSigLIP zero-shot with the CCD prompt pairs (card AUC 0.852 for support devices) and CheXzero's 'support devices' template, which is also the first validation of both on radiologist device labels. If MedGemma and both classifiers fail a class, the items, not the models, are blamed and the class is dropped.
  - DECISION PROCEDURE (pre-registered branches): the primary class is the first class in the order ETT > NGT > (reserve) pacemaker that is GO for at least two of the three generalists and for MedGemma; CVC is never primary. (i) ETT GO: ETT primary, NGT secondary, CVC hard. (ii) only NGT GO: NGT primary, ETT secondary. (iii) no tube class GO for two generalists but MedGemma GO: the report becomes a floor paper plus a MedGemma-centred core (the floor table is the headline; mitigation arms run on MedGemma and on the best generalist as the hero). (iv) nothing GO anywhere: pacemaker reserve in Week 2, then Object-CXR, then the findings-level fallback. In every branch the pair design, abstention scoring, controls and statistics are unchanged. The pilot items double as the resolution figure (Figure 1a, H7): balanced accuracy and hit rate against visual tokens per class and model, with MedGemma's fixed 256-token point and CVC as the thin-line class.

PHASES:
- 1. Feasibility, mapping, floor pilot, freeze and pre-registration [Week 1 (Oct 12-18; front-loaded, about 25-30 h)]: Confirm the due date; read and quote the Kaggle rules; accept every model term; confirm MedGemma's card by eye; build the RANZCR-to-NIH mapping and the realised-yield table; set up one venv per family on the pod (A5000 or 3090, bf16) including the CheXOne/Qwen2.5-VL pair; run the floor pilot at three token budgets with MedGemma and the two classifiers as references; choose and pre-register the primary class; freeze PROTOCOL.md, hashed lists and the dev split; file ethics and reader requests
    * Written due date and scenario (A: Nov 8-12; B: later)
    * Rules screenshot and quoted clauses for RANZCR; HAI-DEF and RadEdit acceptances; MedGemma card sections quoted verbatim; RunPod, OSF, GitHub, NCBE, PhysioNet accounts
    * Mapping notebook with SHA-256 log, match rate and 200-pair spot check; realised-yield table (S1-S5, pairs, U and retrieval pools) per class and relaxation rung
    * Pod smoke test: six runs loaded, s/item, peak VRAM, tokens per image, CheXOne load result (NV-Reason-CXR-3B swapped in if it fails)
    * Floor-pilot table (model x class x token budget: balanced accuracy, hit rate, yes-rate, Wilson CIs) and the primary-class decision; MedSigLIP and CheXzero reference rows
    * PROTOCOL.md v1.0; OSF registration of H0-H8, the class decision rule and branches, the N rule, the relaxation ladder, the synthetic-probe gates and every extension's kill date
    * IRB email sent; NCBE certificate in progress; reader request sent via the advisor; peer rater booked; citation-search log
- 2. Core item set, verification, harness, detectors, retrieval index; synthetic probes built [Week 2 (Oct 19-25; mid-term break Oct 20-22)]: Build pairs A/B, negatives U and the four-question item set with RANZCR labels; non-expert verification with a blinded second rater; validate the HF runner against the lmms-eval task; SLAKE anchor; pilot discordance and N; detector AUC on the held-out pool; retrieval embeddings; on the 1060 and CPU in parallel, run the 40-image three-remover pilot (plus E-irr) and build the 100-image cut-paste insertion set
    * Dataset v0 (NIH filenames, pair ids, prompts, covariates, flags; labels joined at build time)
    * Table 1 construction statistics v0 (yield, pairs, verification kappa and PABAK, ambiguous rate)
    * cxr_device_pope lmms-eval task yaml; HF runner with 99% agreement on 100 items per model; SLAKE closed X-ray anchor rows
    * Pilot discordance and N fixed on OSF (registered amendment)
    * MedSigLIP/CheXzero per-class AUC on the held-out pool (by-product table); retrieval index (MedSigLIP; BiomedCLIP ablation)
    * Remover pilot outputs (LaMa, SD-1.5, RadEdit, E-irr) with verifier scores and protected-region errors; insertion set of 100 with mirror-gate scores; 2AFC blocks scheduled
- 3. Core evaluation on A/B/U and prior rows; probe gates; Sections 1-3 [Week 3 (Oct 26-Nov 1)]: Run P0/P1/P2-hi on all six runs and P2-lo/PN/P0b/BLIND-demo on the hero and MedGemma over every arm with logits cached; produce Table 2 and Figure 1a/1b with the pre-registered tests; apply the automatic and 2AFC gates to the removal and insertion probes by Nov 1; draft Sections 1-3; send the advisor the citation memo
    * Per-item JSONL for every run with token counts, precision, seeds and wall time
    * Table 2 (models x A/B/U x prompts) with Wilson and patient-clustered bootstrap intervals; H1-H4 results; the co-occurrence probe panel
    * Figure 1a (floor pilot as the resolution figure, H7) and Figure 1b (softmax risk-coverage per arm with verbal abstention points and the transferred threshold)
    * Gate results for the removal pilot (verifier pass, protected-region error, 2AFC) and the insertion set (mirror gate, 2AFC); go/no-go recorded (kill date Nov 1 for the automatic gates, Nov 3 for the 2AFC)
    * Sections 1-3 and the related-work table drafted; citation-correction memo sent
- 4. Mitigation arms with controls; preliminary synthetic rows; freeze; report v1 [Week 4 (Nov 2-8)]: Detector-gated answers (CPU) on all six runs and similar-case retrieval (pod) with their control sets; run the hero and MedGemma on gated removal survivors and all six runs on gated insertion survivors under P0/P1/P2-hi; freeze the headline exhibits; deliver a complete, submittable report v1
    * Table 3a (detector-gated vs controls, net gain by arm, coverage) and Table 3b (retrieval vs controls, over-reliance, copy rate, mismatched harm); accuracy-vs-yes-ratio figure with the constant-bias curve per model
    * Table 4 (preliminary synthetic probes: FAR_edited and hit_inserted beside FAR_B, FAR_U and hit_A, n stated, 2AFC numbers stated, reader audit pending) or a 'gate failed' line with the measured detectability
    * Report v1 complete in the course template by Nov 8 (scenario A submission)
    * EACL 2027 SRW mentorship request sent by Nov 6 (only if v1 exists); go/no-go recorded for every extension
- 5. Validity appendix, reader audit, gated extensions; draft v2 [Week 5 (Nov 9-15)]: Reliability block, label-noise and contamination sensitivity, hero resolution 2x2, harness divergence, UAR/IS, power memo; two-reader audit if the resident reads; full edited and inserted arms if their gates passed; Arm 3 region crop on the hero and MedGemma; other extensions only through their gates; draft v2 to the advisor by Nov 13
    * Appendix tables A-F
    * Reader-audit results (two-reader rubric, kappa) or a dated 'pending' statement with the student-plus-peer audit standing
    * Full edited and inserted arm columns in v2 if gated in; Arm 3 Table 3c; extension results or a one-line 'not run' each
    * Draft v2 to the advisor by Nov 13
- 6. Revision, release, final v2, submissions [Week 6 (Nov 16-22)]: Incorporate advisor comments; number-provenance check; release code, task yaml, mapping code, masks and the NIH item list; submit final report v2 (scenario B); NAACL SRW mentorship request; awesome-list pull request; close the cost ledger
    * Final report v2 (Nov 22)
    * Public GitHub repo and HF dataset card (NIH-releasable items, rebuild script, per-arm terms); no RANZCR rows
    * NAACL 2027 SRW mentorship request (Nov 16); pull request to NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination
    * Cost ledger under the cap; pod volume archived and deleted
- Buffer [Weeks 7-8 (Nov 23-Dec 6)]: Absorb slippage; convert the report into the 4-page SRW/EMBC/MIDL manuscript with the appendix as supplementary material; JURI submission; manuscript frozen by Dec 10
    * Reruns of failed cells
    * Manuscript frozen by Dec 10 (EACL Dec 15 only if nothing is pending; otherwise EMBC Jan 24 or NAACL SRW)
    * JURI submission
    * Hand-off note for the PhysioNet replication and the findings-level extension

CONTRIBUTION STEPS:

[1] Day-one feasibility: terms, MedGemma card check, RANZCR-to-NIH mapping, realised yield, pod smoke test (six runs), floor pilot at three token budgets, primary-class decision, protocol freeze, pre-registration, ethics and reader filings
  what: Confirm the due date; read and quote the Kaggle rules; accept HAI-DEF (medgemma-1.5-4b-it, medgemma-4b-it, medsiglip-448) and the RadEdit click-through; confirm MedGemma's CXR14 status by eye; build the mapping and the realised-yield table on Kaggle CPU; stand up one venv per family on the pod and smoke-test all six runs including the CheXOne/Qwen2.5-VL pair; run the floor pilot for three classes at three budgets with MedGemma and the two classifiers as references; apply the pre-registered rule; freeze PROTOCOL.md v1.0, hashed lists and the dev split; register H0-H8 on OSF; send the IRB, NCBE and reader requests.
  why_it_matters: Week 13 is Nov 8-12 (registrar-verified); the RANZCR pixels are NIH images with no public mapping, so the credential-free pipeline depends on a half-day join; label totals are not image counts, so N cannot be claimed before the yield is realised; 2-4B generalists have no published device numbers and may sit at the floor (Phi-3.5-vision at 32% on ReXVQA tubes/lines), so the primary class must be chosen on measured balanced accuracy; the medical digest contradicts itself on MedGemma's CXR14 exposure, so only the card settles the matrix; the reader and IRB routes need two weeks of lead time; and the pod price must be read today (the official RunPod page on 12 Oct 2026 lists RTX 3090 $0.50/h, RTX 4090 $0.89/h and RTX A5000 $0.27/h, not the $0.22-0.34 the brief carried).
  inputs: Kaggle (competition ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification joined with rules accepted; nih-chest-xrays/data attached; Data_Entry_2017_v2020.csv); HF token with HAI-DEF accepted; Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct, OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B, google/gemma-4-E2B-it (Apache-2.0), google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it and google/medgemma-4b-it (HAI-DEF), StanfordAIMI/CheXOne (CC BY-NC; transformers from main; own venv) and Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (Qwen Research License); nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B (NVIDIA OneWay non-commercial) as the drop-in if CheXOne fails; lmms-eval at a pinned commit (main ships gemma4, gemma3, qwen3_vl, qwen2_5_vl and internvl3_5 wrappers, verified 12 Oct 2026); RunPod community RTX A5000 24 GB ($0.27/h) or RTX 3090 ($0.50/h) with a 50 GB network volume; OSF, GitHub, NCBE portal (ncbeportal.kacst.gov.sa), PhysioNet and CITI (background); GTX 1060 venv on torch 2.14 cu126 fp32; CCD repository (MIT) ccd/ccd_utils.py for the MedSigLIP device prompt pairs; CheXzero (MIT) zero_shot.py templates; Lotfinia's MIT repo for prompt wording.
  method: Mon Oct 12: email the RES201 instructor (exact due date, template, rubric, whether an ACL-short body plus appendix satisfies the format); one email to the advisor with the five asks (reader, $30 spend, format, memo, PhysioNet interest); join RANZCR CLiP, screenshot the Rules tab, copy the data-access and data-security clauses into PROTOCOL.md; accept HAI-DEF and RadEdit terms; open the MedGemma 1.5 card and quote its 'Dataset overview' and dataset-ownership sections verbatim into the contamination matrix; open ccd/ccd_utils.py and record the prompt pairs; open github.com/mahshadlotfinia/causal and record the exact yes/no prompt wording (digest reading: 'Is [finding] present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.') and whether a support-devices block exists; create RunPod, OSF, GitHub; start NCBE and PhysioNet/CITI; test the Object-CXR Drive link and record the result. Tue Oct 13 (Kaggle CPU): mapping per the data plan (pixel-hash first pass, dimension key, pHash/dHash, 200-pair spot check, unmatched log); realised-yield table per class and rung (S1-S5, pairs, U, retrieval pool); pilot lists (per class 100 present + 100 absent, one image per patient, half of the negatives with another labelled line); copy the needed NIH PNGs to the pod volume. Wed Oct 14 (pod, bf16): one venv per family at pinned versions (qwen3_vl; internvl; gemma4; medgemma through the gemma3 wrapper or the HF runner; CheXOne and Qwen2.5-VL-3B through qwen2_5_vl or the HF runner); 50-item smoke test per run with s/item, VRAM and tokens per image (assert 1,024 for Qwen3-VL at 1024 px; log InternVL tiles x 256 plus thumbnail; 1,120 for Gemma 4; 256 for MedGemma; Qwen2.5-VL at 512*512 and 1024*1024); floor pilot with the P0 prompt and device names 'endotracheal tube', 'nasogastric tube', 'central venous catheter' (about 9,000 items); MedSigLIP zero-shot (CCD prompt pairs) and CheXzero ('support devices' vs 'no support devices' at 320 px) on the same images; rerun 200 items of the two Qwen3-backbone generalists in fp32 on Kaggle and count flips. Thu Oct 15: apply the decision rule and record the branch; PROTOCOL.md v1.0; OSF registration (H0 gate; H1 co-occurrence prior; H2 general vs medical with the CheXOne/base pair primary; H3 penalty; H4 ranking change; H5 Arm 1; H6 Arm 2; H7 resolution; H8 synthetic probes, gated); hash the pair lists and the 40-patient dev split; 1060 venv with MedSigLIP, CheXzero, BiomedCLIP in fp32, one timed embedding pass over 500 images, IOPaint LaMa smoke test on one image. Fri-Sat Oct 16-17: citation search log (Semantic Scholar and Google Scholar citing lists of Lotfinia 2026, HalluCXR, CCD, RULE; keywords 'chest X-ray device hallucination', 'tubes lines vision-language', 'longitudinal radiograph pair', 'abstention medical VQA'); IRB email citing NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Articles 10.19 and 10.33 and the datasets' own IRB statements; archive the Kaggle notebook with hashes; book the second rater. Decision rules written before the pilot: any run with over 1% bf16-vs-fp32 flips or any assert runs only in bf16 on the pod; Kaggle T4 fp16 is excluded for the Gemma family and Qwen2-VL; the whole headline panel runs in bf16 on the pod.
  output: Written due date and scenario; rules record; MedGemma card quotes; mapping table with match statistics; realised-yield table; smoke-test note; floor-pilot table (Table 1 draft, Figure 1a data); primary-class decision and branch; PROTOCOL.md v1.0; OSF link; hashed lists; filings sent; citation log.
  compute: Pod 3-4 h on an A5000 at $0.27/h (about $1; $2 on a 3090); Kaggle CPU 4-6 h ($0); GTX 1060 2-3 h ($0); person-time about 25-30 h (the heaviest week; the break week absorbs the overflow).
  risk: Dimension key not unique or all JPEG sizes identical; CheXOne fails to load from transformers main; Gemma 4 wrapper failure; pod unavailable on Wednesday; every generalist at the floor on every tube class; course answers late; A5000 unavailable in the console.
  fallback: Hash-only matching against all NIH images (40 CPU-minutes); NV-Reason-CXR-3B as the contaminated specialist (same Qwen2.5-VL class); google/gemma-3-4b-it if Gemma 4 fails everywhere; run the pilot without MedGemma for a day and add it Thursday; pre-registered branches (iii) and (iv); assume scenario A by Oct 16; 3090 at $0.50/h with the hour budget halved by the cut list.
  paper_section: 3.1-3.2 (mapping, yield), 5.1 (setup, Table 1 floor table, Figure 1a), Appendices A and B.
  closest_prior_work: ReXVQA Table 3 (MCQ device categories; no 2B numbers); Lotfinia et al. 2026 (prompt wording; constant-answer models; 224 px); Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR (ML4H 2025; specialist ceiling, no weights); RAD-DINO and Sabottke and Spieler 2020 (resolution); UVLM and GHOST-Q (precision as a factor, from v1); analokmaus and ianpan repositories (no mapping); MedGemma tech report arXiv 2507.05201 (CXR14 as evaluation).

[2] LongiDev-CXR core set: pairs A/B, negatives U, four-question items, non-expert verification with blinded audit, harness validation, anchors, detector AUC, retrieval index, pilot discordance and N
  what: Build the item set from the mapped RANZCR train labels: one same-patient pair per patient for the primary and secondary classes, 100 CVC pairs, context-matched U negatives, four yes/no questions per image with radiologist labels and covariates; student verification of every absence and every primary-class presence with a second rater on 200 items; the cxr_device_pope task and the per-family HF runner validated against each other; SLAKE anchor; pilot discordance and N; MedSigLIP and CheXzero AUC on the held-out pool; retrieval embeddings.
  why_it_matters: The pair is the artefact-free negative stimulus (same anatomy, habitus and view, device gone, no inpainting trace); U separates 'same patient' from 'any ICU-looking negative'; the four questions turn the RANZCR label vector into within-image adversarial negatives for free; verification with kappa answers the label-noise critique (15% re-review discrepancy) without pretending the student is a radiologist; the detector AUC is a prerequisite for Arm 1 and a publishable by-product; the harness agreement check is what makes the cached logits citable.
  inputs: Mapped train.csv and train_annotations.csv (Kaggle and local only); NIH PNGs and metadata; CheXmask ChestX-ray8 CSV; realised-yield table; a one-page reference sheet per class (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz, tracheostomy) from radiology teaching material; lmms-eval at the pinned commit (pope.yaml and slake task as templates; BoKelvin/SLAKE CC BY 4.0); per-family HF runners; google/medsiglip-448 with the CCD prompt pairs; CheXzero weights; microsoft/BiomedCLIP-PubMedBERT_256-vit_base_patch16_224 (MIT); statsmodels, scipy, scikit-learn, bayes_evals.
  method: Pairs and U exactly as the data plan specifies, applying the relaxation ladder only as far as needed and recording the rung. Items: per image four questions ('endotracheal tube', 'nasogastric tube', 'central venous catheter', 'Swan-Ganz catheter') keyed to the RANZCR columns (NGT incompletely imaged = present, flagged). Verification: the student reads every B and U image (device absent?) and every primary-class A image (device visible?) at 10-15 s per image (about 1,000 images, 3-4 h) with the reference sheet, flagging ambiguous items (faint CVC, tracheostomy vs ETT, tube partly outside the field); blinded second rater (peer or the advisor's graduate student) on a random 200-item sample across A/B/U; raw agreement, Cohen's kappa (target >= 0.8), PABAK, adjudication; agreement with the RANZCR label reported with Wilson intervals; ambiguous items excluded from tests and counted. Covariates per image: view, age, sex, other-labelled-line count, NIH NLP labels (covariate only), follow-up gap, direction, CheXmask Dice. Harness: cxr_device_pope.yaml (copy of pope.yaml; local HF dataset of NIH PNGs plus items; metrics accuracy, balanced accuracy, yes-ratio, abstention, Phi_c) validated with --limit 20 on the dev split; the HF runner per family generates greedily with max_new_tokens 16 and caches the first-token logits of the yes/no/abstain surface forms in the same forward; 99% argmax agreement on 100 items per model before any cached number is reported. Prior rows prepared: BLIND-text (question only), BLIND-demo (question plus 'This is an {AP/PA} chest radiograph of a {age}-year-old {sex}.'), BLIND-grey (mid-grey 1024x1024 PNG). Anchor: lmms-eval slake closed X-ray subset for all six runs (comparability with the MedGemma card's SLAKE 82.8). Pilot: 35 pairs through the hero under P0; discordance; N by the McNemar formula; registered amendment. Detectors (1060, fp32): MedSigLIP zero-shot per class (softmax over the CCD positive/negative prompt pair) and CheXzero 'support devices' logit difference at 320 px on at least 2,000 held-out pool images per class with RANZCR labels, patient-disjoint from test and dev; AUROC per class with bootstrap CIs; dev-split thresholds; this is also the gate calibration for step 3 (90th percentile of real class-negative scores) and step 4 (10th percentile of real positives). Retrieval index: MedSigLIP image embeddings of the whole patient-disjoint pool with labels; BiomedCLIP embeddings for the retriever ablation. Dataset v0 and Table 1 construction statistics.
  output: Dataset v0 (NIH filenames, pair ids, prompts, covariates, flags; labels joined at build time); Table 1 construction statistics; cxr_device_pope.yaml; HF runner agreement report; SLAKE anchor rows; power memo with realised N; detector AUC table; retrieval index.
  compute: GTX 1060 3-5 h (detector and embedding passes); pod 1-2 h ($0.5) for the pilot, the agreement check and the SLAKE anchor; Kaggle CPU 1-2 h; person-time 8-10 h (student), 2 h (second rater).
  risk: Primary-class pair yield below 200; verification backlog in the break week; second rater unavailable; wrappers reject a no-image prompt for the text-only row; MedSigLIP or CheXzero AUC near 0.5 on a class.
  fallback: Relaxation ladder; verify only B and U plus a 50% sample of A; advisor's graduate student or a second peer; text-only row through the HF runner only; if a detector is uninformative on a class, Arm 1 uses the better detector and reports the other as a null row.
  paper_section: 3 (LongiDev-CXR), 5.1, Appendix B (dataset card, audit) and C (power memo).
  closest_prior_work: Lotfinia et al. 2026 (prompt wording, swap and removal controls); RoentMod (real follow-ups as comparator); HalluCXR (256-item human check by non-radiologists); MedHEval (50-item check); RANZCR CLiP descriptor (15% discrepancy); VHTest and AMBER audit conventions (from v1); the lmms-eval slake task.

[3] Synthetic probe 1 (bounded, gated): 40-image three-remover device-removal pilot with the pre-registered validity gate (two independent verifiers, protected-region error, E-irr control, 2AFC detectability, two-reader rubric) and preliminary edited-arm rows
  what: Remove the primary-class device from 40 A images with each of three removers; add the E-irr control (LaMa fill of an equal-size lung region away from any device); pass survivors through the gate; if a remover passes the automatic and 2AFC gates by Nov 1-3, run the hero and MedGemma on the pilot set for a preliminary row in v1 (Table 4); if the reader audit passes by Nov 15, build the full edited arm for v2 and report the real-minus-edited FAR gap per model.
  why_it_matters: This makes the component title's 'synthetic visual data' measurable instead of assumed: LaMa on grayscale CXR is undocumented (MaskMedPaint used it as a background eraser), RadEdit 'could not remove findings convincingly' in the one precedent (cxr-grounding) and scored 82.1% joint validity on findings under the Spatially Gated Diffusion rubric, and every 2024-2026 device editor validates by classifier flip only; a measured detectability and reader-validity number is new on its own, and a passing arm gives the trace-versus-prior attribution (FAR_edited vs FAR_B vs FAR_U vs blind rows) and a contamination-free comparison.
  inputs: 40 A images with RANZCR polylines rasterised at 1024 px and dilated by a class-specific radius set on the dev split (about 1.5x the measured half-width; 8-15 px), with ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes (MIT code, research-only weights; in-sample on RANZCR plus 31,448 NIH images; no fold file) used only to refine masks on this subset, never as a presence oracle; IOPaint LaMa (Apache-2.0; fp32 on the 1060; grayscale replicated to 3 channels); stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-inpainting (creativeml-openrail-m; fp32 on the 1060; 512-px crop windows pasted back; prompt 'clear lung, no tube', negative 'tube, line, catheter'; 30 steps; seed 1234); microsoft/radedit on the pod at 512 px (edit_mask = dilated device mask, keep_mask = inverse including the CheXmask heart; microsoft/BiomedVLP-BioViL-T; stabilityai/sdxl-vae in fp32 or madebyollin/sdxl-vae-fp16-fix; 100-200 steps; outputs resampled to 1024 with one documented resampler also applied to the untouched control); CheXzero and MedSigLIP thresholds from step 2; torchxrayvision densenet121-res224-mimic_ch (Apache-2.0; MIMIC-only; no support-devices head) as a drift verifier flagging unintended opacity changes only; a peer for the 2AFC; readers for the rubric (student plus peer by default; the IAU KFHU resident if obtained).
  method: Gate (pre-registered): G1 both independent verifiers agree the device is absent (CheXzero support-devices logit and the MedSigLIP class score both below the 90th percentile of real class-negative scores from the dev-calibrated pool) AND the protected-region mean absolute error outside the dilated mask is below the 95th percentile of PNG re-encoding noise (the Spatially Gated Diffusion criterion), with symmetric pass/fail criteria applied to untouched controls; G2 2AFC edited-vs-untouched crop test (Sogancioglu 2018 design; 40 crop pairs per remover of identical size and position; two raters blinded) with accuracy not significantly above 50% by a one-sided binomial test at alpha 0.05, the number reported regardless; G3 two-reader blinded audit on 100-150 surviving images plus 30-50 untouched negative controls using the Spatially Gated Diffusion criteria (target removed, no unintended change, no structural artefact, joint validity), adjudication, Cohen's kappa and PABAK. Per remover record verifier pass rate, protected-region error, drift flags, 2AFC accuracy, seconds per image. Remover choice: highest joint G1 pass rate among removers passing G2; ties toward LaMa (fastest, 1024-px native, releasable outputs); the trace-vs-prior analysis needs at least one high-trace and one low-trace remover, so a detectable remover is kept as a labelled trace row. Co-occurring-item drift check: does removing the ETT change the CVC answer? Preliminary row (v1): hero and MedGemma on the 40 edited images and the 40 E-irr controls under P0/P1/P2-hi, 'preliminary edited arm (n = 40, reader audit pending)', FAR_edited and FAR_irr-flip beside FAR_B and FAR_U. Full arm (v2 only): all polyline-available primary-class A images edited with the passing remover, all six runs under P0/P1/P2-hi, the real-minus-edited FAR gap per model as the pixel-memorisation signal. Kill dates: automatic gates Nov 1; 2AFC Nov 3; reader audit and full arm Nov 15; afterwards the step is reported as 'not run' with its nearest prior work.
  output: Appendix D table (remover x {verifier pass, protected-region error, 2AFC, reader validity, kappa, s/image}); Table 4 preliminary rows in v1 if gated in; full edited-arm columns and the gap table in v2 if the audit passes; the first LaMa-on-CXR detectability number either way.
  compute: GTX 1060: LaMa under 10 min, SD inpainting about 40-60 min, E-irr under 30 min, verifiers 20 min; pod: RadEdit 40 images about 1 h ($0.3); preliminary rows 0.3 h; full arm if gated: 1060 LaMa 1 h plus pod panel 2-3 h ($1); person-time: pipeline 2-3 h, 2AFC 1 h per rater, rubric 2 h per reader.
  risk: No remover passes (expected high for RadEdit, unknown for LaMa; thin tubes leave fewer residues than COCO objects); reader not obtained; IOPaint install fails on torch 2.14; LaMa leaves a smooth band on lung markings.
  fallback: The pair arm already carries the paper; the failed gate is itself the LaMa-on-CXR measurement; readers = student plus peer with the limitation stated; LaMa via the advimman/lama repository directly or Kaggle CPU; a feathered wider mask compared in the ablation.
  paper_section: Appendix D and Table 4 (v1); 3.5 and 5.5 (v2) if gated in.
