  closest_prior_work: Sogancioglu et al. 2018 (2AFC); MaskMedPaint (ML4H 2024); MedDiME 2026 and Latent Drifting (classifier-flip validation); RadEdit (ECCV 2024) and Spatially Gated Diffusion 2026 (two-reader rubric, protected-region MAE, RadEdit 82.1%); RoentMod (no real negative controls); BEAF and ROHE (LaMa removals on COCO, from v1); cxr-grounding (RadEdit removal failure); Lotfinia's Irrelevant-Mask Stability (E-irr analogue).

[4] Synthetic probe 2 (bounded, gated): 100-image CPU cut-paste device insertion into same-patient B hosts along CheXmask anatomy, validated by a mirror gate and a real-vs-inserted 2AFC, giving an A / B / B-plus-synthetic triplet
  what: Paste an intensity-matched, feathered polyline crop of the primary device from a donor A image into 100 B images of the primary class (U hosts if placement fails) along the tracheal midline derived from the CheXmask lung masks; gate with the two verifiers, a 2AFC and a DREAM-definition audit; run all six runs on survivors under P0/P1/P2-hi in Week 4 so report v1 carries a disclosed 'synthetic object on real background' row; build in Week 2 because it reuses the step-3 polylines.
  why_it_matters: Device insertion is the scarce resource (XReal drops devices, RoentMod excluded them, SynthCheX-75K has no device labels, RadiT is unreleased); a cut-paste insertion is CPU-only, label-certain by construction, contamination-free for every model, and turns each pair into a triplet (real present, real absent, synthetic present), the closest executable analogue of the component title's 'synthetic visual data'; hit rate on inserted versus real A devices tests whether a pasted device reads as a device, and the real-minus-inserted hit-rate gap is a second pixel-memorisation signal.
  inputs: Primary-class polyline crops from donor A images (segmented by thresholding the bright device inside the dilated polyline); B and U hosts; CheXmask lung and heart masks for the NIH ids (midline between lungs; tip above the carina estimated from the heart-mask top; the ianpan carina heatmap as a placement aid only); NIH pixel spacing for scale; numpy/OpenCV alpha or Poisson blending; verifier thresholds from step 2 (10th percentile of real positives); the DREAM definition for the audit of implausible or extra content; a peer for the 2AFC.
  method: For each host: locate the midline from the CheXmask masks; warp the donor crop to the host's scale; match intensity by histogram of the surrounding background; feather with a 5-10 px alpha ramp; save at 1024 px PNG with identical encoding. Gate: MedSigLIP and CheXzero scores above the 10th percentile of real positives; a 50-pair synthetic-vs-real crop 2AFC by two raters (not significantly above 50%; the number reported regardless); a 50-image audit for implausible placement (DREAM-type stimulus hallucination) by the student and peer; co-occurring labels kept from the host and re-verified. Panel: all six runs on P0/P1/P2-hi for surviving items (about 100 images x 4 questions x 3 prompts x 6 runs = 7,200 items); metrics as in Table 2 with the inserted arm as a column; hit_inserted vs hit_A; FAR on the untouched co-occurring questions as a drift check. Kill date for the v1 row: Nov 5; the 20-image RadEdit masked 'add' pilot on the pod only if the pod budget is under $20 by Nov 10 (v2).
  output: Table 4 row (insertion yield, gate pass rates, 2AFC, hit_inserted vs hit_A, FAR drift) or a 'gate failed' line with the measured detectability; Appendix D details.
  compute: CPU 1-2 h; 1060 verifiers 20 min; pod panel 1 h ($0.3); person-time 3-4 h plus 1 h per rater for the 2AFC.
  risk: Pasted tubes fail the 2AFC (visible seams); midline estimate wrong on rotated films; donor and host exposure mismatch.
  fallback: Report the failure as a measurement; restrict to upright U hosts; histogram matching over a larger window; drop the row and keep the pipeline for v2.
  paper_section: Table 4 and Appendix D; 3.5 in v2 if gated in.
  closest_prior_work: HEAL-MedVQA (IJCAI 2025; pasted region from another image); the anatomy-guided shape-insertion paper (arXiv 2501.12022; foreign-object synthesis for segmentation training); RadEdit's pacemaker insertion; XReal (devices absent by construction); the DREAM definition; RadiT and RoentMod reader-test designs.

[5] Core behavioural evaluation (Table 2, Figure 1a, Figure 1b): co-occurrence prior under the edit-free negative arm, general vs medical with the matched pair, penalty dose-response, ranking change, resolution factor, softmax selection
  what: Run every core model on A, B and U under P0, P1 and P2-hi, and the hero and MedGemma additionally under P2-lo, PN, P0b and BLIND-demo, with BLIND-text and BLIND-grey rows for all; cache first-token logits in the P0 pass; score with patient-clustered statistics; draw Figure 1a from the step-1 pilot and Figure 1b from the hero's softmax; test H1-H4 and H7.
  why_it_matters: One table answers RQ1-RQ3: whether a 'yes' on a device-absent study of the same patient exceeds the unpaired and blind references (the prior) and how often a correct 'yes' persists after the device is gone (device sensitivity); whether CXR post-training changes device hallucination and abstention (the clean claim-2 test) and whether MedGemma beats or trails the generalists (confounded, secondary); whether a stated penalty moves abstention and FAR_B (no CXR paper has run Kalai's instruction); whether Effective Reliability reorders the models; and whether thin devices are sub-token at low budgets.
  inputs: Dataset v0 with hashed lists; the six runs in bf16 on the pod; the HF runners and the cxr_device_pope task; prompts from PROTOCOL.md; statsmodels (mixed-effects logistic, exact McNemar), scipy BCa bootstrap, bayes_evals, scikit-learn metrics; the pilot JSONL for Figure 1a.
  method: Pod, incremental JSONL by image id: for each run, each image in A/B/U, each of four questions, each assigned prompt; greedy, max_new_tokens 16; logits cached under P0. Metrics per model x arm x prompt with n and Wilson intervals: accuracy, balanced accuracy, precision, recall, F1, yes-ratio, hit_A (positive recall), FAR_B, FAR_U, hallucination-free rate (1 - FAR), paired flip rate A to B, persistence rate (among A-correct 'yes', the share still 'yes' on B; 1 minus this is the Lotfinia CGR analogue), co-occurrence stratum (FAR_B with vs without other labelled lines present), BLIND-text P(yes), BLIND-demo and BLIND-grey yes-rates, abstention rate, risk, coverage, Phi_c at c in {1, 3, 9}, guessing premium, CARES over-confidence ratio (secondary), optional MediHall-weighted score. Primary pre-registered tests: H1 FAR_B(other labelled lines present) minus FAR_B(none) per model, patient-clustered BCa bootstrap (9,999 resamples), with FAR_B vs FAR_U, vs BLIND-demo and vs the grey row as secondary and the persistence rate reported beside them; H2 primary = paired difference in hallucination-free rate and balanced accuracy, CheXOne minus Qwen2.5-VL-3B, on A union B (cluster bootstrap; exact McNemar secondary; also on U), secondary = MedGemma-1.5-4B vs each generalist (Holm across the three; labelled size- and encoder-confounded), with the pre-registered note that the advisor's claim predicts generalists >= medical while ReXVQA predicts the reverse on devices; H3 abstention and FAR_B under P2-hi vs P1 vs P0 per arm: McNemar on abstain indicators plus bootstrap; H4 Phi_9 vs P0-accuracy orderings with pairwise flip counts; H7 monotone gain with token budget for the thin classes and CVC lowest, from the pilot items with cluster-bootstrap trend intervals; pooled mixed-effects logistic correct ~ arm x model + (1 | patient) + (1 | image) beside the per-model tests; Holm within each family. Hero softmax signal: confidence = max(P(yes), P(no)) after summing surface forms; AUROC for error prediction, AURC, coverage at 1/5/10% risk, ECE; threshold fixed at 5% risk on U and transferred to B and A with bootstrap intervals; logit-vs-verbal coverage gap at equal risk; compared with a random gate at equal coverage. Stratify by class, pair direction, other-labelled-line count, view, follow-up gap, token budget. The real-minus-synthetic gaps from steps 3-4 are appended as Table 4 when gated in.
  output: Table 2 (models x arms x {balanced accuracy, hit_A, FAR_B, FAR_U, yes-ratio, abstain%_P1, abstain%_P2-hi, risk, Phi_1, Phi_9} plus prior rows, the co-occurrence stratum and the persistence rate) with intervals; the claim-2 panel with hallucination-free rates; Figure 1a (balanced accuracy and hit rate vs visual tokens per class and model); Figure 1b (risk-coverage per arm, verbal abstention points, transferred threshold); Appendix C.
  compute: Pod bf16: about 6,200 items per prompt; six runs x 3 prompts plus the hero and MedGemma x 4 extra prompts (BLIND-demo text-only items are fast) = about 150,000 items at 0.3-0.5 s per image item = 14-20 h on a 3090-class card, 20-28 h on an A5000 ($5.5-7.5 at $0.27/h; $7-10 on a 3090 at $0.50/h); Kaggle fp32 backup only for the two Qwen3-backbone generalists.
  risk: Models ignore or over-use the abstain option; PN acquiescence dominates (CXR-ContraBench: over 62% negated-option attraction); the mixed model fails to converge on near-zero cells; pod interruptions; CheXOne emits reasoning text despite instruct mode.
  fallback: Both abstention extremes are findings; PN reported as the acquiescence-shift row and dropped from tests if degenerate; the clustered bootstrap is the fallback primary; resume by image id and finish the Qwen3-backbone runs on Kaggle fp32; parse CheXOne's final token with max_new_tokens 32 for it only, stated.
  paper_section: 4, 5.2 (Table 2), 5.3 (Figure 1a), 5.4 (Figure 1b), 6.
  closest_prior_work: Lotfinia et al. 2026 (CGR, UAR, retention without image; nine systems at 224 px); Kalai et al.; Reliable VQA (Whitehead et al. ECCV 2022; Effective Reliability); CARES; HalluCXR (yes-bias and miscalibration at 7B); CheXpercept (response bias persists); MedVH and Kim et al. 2025 (medical vs general); CXR-ContraBench (negated options); Seeing without Looking (unpenalised unknown, from v1); RAD-DINO Appendix C (resolution ablation); HalluPrism and Anatomy of a Lie (softmax selection at 7B+, from v1).

[6] Mitigation Arm 1: detector-gated answers (CCD expert-informed stage re-implemented on cached logits) with detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias, OLM and selective-override controls (Table 3a)
  what: Fuse a clipped log-odds device score from MedSigLIP zero-shot (or CheXzero) into the cached first-token yes/no logits of every run on A/B/U (and on gated synthetic survivors); score against greedy and every yes-ratio-matched control; add an agree-or-abstain variant scored with Phi_c and coverage.
  why_it_matters: This is the umbrella's 'grounding-based technique' in its cheapest, fully training-free form and the first CCD-style fusion reported with the two controls a reviewer will ask for (does the VLM add anything beyond the detector; is the gain just a bias term), plus the first Mirage and Rethinking-CD controls on medical images and below 7B; CPU-only, so it cannot slip.
  inputs: Cached P0 logits from step 5 (all six runs); per-image MedSigLIP class scores s (CCD prompt pairs) and CheXzero support-devices logits from step 2; the dev split for beta, gamma and tau; the CCD equation and defaults (clip gamma in {2, 5, 10}); Mirage OLM and Rethinking-CD constant-bias definitions.
  method: z = log P(yes) minus log P(no) with surface forms summed; z' = z + beta x clip(log(s/(1-s)), -gamma, gamma), beta in {0.5, 1, 2}, gamma in {2, 5, 10} chosen on the dev split; answer = sign(z'); CheXzero variant as the class-agnostic second expert; on any pacemaker-branch item the expert is CheXzero only. Agree-or-abstain variant: abstain when sign(z) differs from the expert's sign and |z'| < tau (dev-tuned), scored with Phi_1/3/9 and coverage. Controls, each at the method's yes-ratio: detector-alone (threshold on s at the dev operating point), shuffled-expert (s drawn from a random other test image; 20 draws), constant-bias sweep z + b tracing the (yes-ratio, accuracy) curve with b* reported, Mirage OLM (force the majority direction when |P(yes) - P(no)| < tau), sign-randomised magnitude-matched perturbation (20 draws), selective override (apply only when |log-odds of s| exceeds a threshold; coverage reported). Net gain = method minus its matched control with a paired patient-clustered bootstrap; McNemar vs greedy and vs OLM; H5: does net gain differ by arm (A vs B vs U)? Informativeness: hit_A beside FAR_B; share of corrections that are yes-to-no vs no-to-yes. A method whose (yes-ratio, accuracy) point lies on the base model's constant-bias curve is reported as a bias shift.
  output: Table 3a and the accuracy-vs-yes-ratio figure with the constant-bias curve per model; the detector AUC table (step 2) cited beside it.
  compute: CPU only (seconds); detector passes already done on the 1060.
  risk: Detector AUC too low on the primary class to move anything (a reportable null); the shuffled-expert control matches the method (the gain is a bias term; reportable).
  fallback: Swap to the better detector per class; report the null with intervals; keep the detector-alone row as the floor of what grounding can add.
  paper_section: 5.5 (Table 3a), 6.
  closest_prior_work: CCD (Findings of ACL 2026, arXiv 2509.23379; MIT code for LLaVA-style models; no detector-alone or shuffled-expert controls); Expert-CFG, ChatCAD, MedRAX, FactCheXcker; Mirage (OLM) and Rethinking-CD (constant bias on COCO at 7-8B); 'Does Playing it Safe Count as Faithfulness' (recall cost).

[7] Mitigation Arm 2: similar-case retrieval (kNN over a patient-disjoint radiologist-labelled pool) with kNN-vote-alone, mismatched, label-shuffled, k-sweep, interleaved-image and over-reliance controls (Table 3b)
  what: Prepend the labels of the k most similar radiographs from the held-out RANZCR-labelled pool as text (or as interleaved images where the model accepts them) and measure accuracy, FAR_B, yes-ratio, over-reliance and copy rates against greedy, the retriever alone and the harmful-retrieval conditions; the three interpretability rows (relevant k = 3, kNN-vote-alone, mismatched) on all six runs, the full control set on the hero and MedGemma.
  why_it_matters: This is the umbrella's 'retrieval-based technique' in the only form a frozen 2-4B model can use without training, and the digest predicts it is null-to-negative (RULE basic RAG 75.79 to 67.35; MICCAI 2026 vision-attention ratio 0.06 under appended text; When Retrieval Hurts; MC-CXR context flips of 45.6-78.1% at 3-4B); B images are ICU-looking studies whose neighbours will mostly carry the device, so the arm is a direct test of retrieval harm on existence questions, and the pre-registered expectation is stated in advance.
  inputs: MedSigLIP (and BiomedCLIP) embeddings of the patient-disjoint pool with RANZCR labels (step 2); test images A/B/U; the six runs on the pod; prompt template 'A radiologist-labelled archive was searched for the {k} radiographs most similar to this one. {m} of {k} contain a {device}.' prepended to P0; MMed-RAG's over-reliance definition; When Retrieval Hurts copy-rate and mismatched-retrieval definitions.
  method: Retrieve k nearest by cosine similarity (patient-disjoint enforced; same-patient neighbours excluded by construction). All six runs, primary-class question: relevant k = 3 text, kNN-label-vote alone (majority of k labels, no VLM), mismatched (k nearest among pool images whose label is the opposite of the test item's truth; the When Retrieval Hurts and MC-CXR control), plus the constant-bias control at the method's yes-ratio from the step-6 curves. Hero and MedGemma additionally: k in {1, 5}, label-shuffled (labels permuted across queries), k = 3 on all four questions, BiomedCLIP retriever ablation at k = 3, P2-hi variant (does retrieval change abstention); interleaved-image variant with k = 3 neighbour images and labels for Qwen3-VL-2B and Gemma 4 E2B. Metrics: accuracy, balanced accuracy, FAR_B, FAR_U, hit_A, yes-ratio, over-reliance rate (greedy-correct items flipped to wrong), copy rate (answers equal to the kNN majority), net gain vs kNN-vote-alone and vs the matched constant bias with paired clustered bootstrap; H6: relevant k = 3 vs greedy (expected null-to-negative) and mismatched vs greedy (expected harm), with over-reliance as the mechanism readout; grounding-vs-retrieval contrast = Arm 1 net gain minus Arm 2 net gain on the hero with a cluster bootstrap.
  output: Table 3b (conditions x models) with intervals, over-reliance and copy rates; one figure of accuracy and FAR_B against k on the hero and MedGemma.
  compute: Pod bf16: 3 conditions x about 900 primary-class images x 6 runs = about 16,000 items, plus about 11,000 extra items on the hero and MedGemma and 1,800 interleaved items = about 29,000 items, 3-4 h on a 3090-class card, 4-6 h on an A5000 ($1.5); embeddings already done on the 1060.
  risk: Retrieval pool too small for the rarer class; models ignore the prepended text (copy rate near the greedy agreement); multi-image prompts exceed the token budget or the wrapper.
  fallback: Pool the two classes' negatives; a null is reportable with the copy-rate mechanism; drop the interleaved-image variant first (cut list).
  paper_section: 5.5 (Table 3b), 6.
  closest_prior_work: RULE (EMNLP 2024) and MMed-RAG (ICLR 2025) retrieval-only ablations and over-reliance rate; When Retrieval Hurts (Sept 2026; mismatched retrieval, copy rate); MC-CXR (Findings of EMNLP 2026); V-RAG entity probing; MedPromptX; CXR-RePaiR; the MICCAI 2026 feature-level RAG attention result.

[8] Validity appendix: reliability block, label-noise and contamination sensitivity, hero resolution 2x2, harness divergence, Lotfinia comparability metrics, covariate-adjusted model, power memo, reader audit
  what: Quantify how stable, clean and comparable the headline numbers are: split-half reliability and design effect, cross-session retest, P1b paraphrase and P0b agreement, PN acquiescence, sensitivity to ambiguous and disagreed labels, the contamination matrix with the text-only leak row and the synthetic gaps if gated in, a token-budget 2x2 on identical content, Lotfinia's Unrelated-Image Answer Rate and Irrelevant-Mask Stability, the conditional model stratified by patient, the realised-N power memo, and the two-reader audit results if the resident reads.
  why_it_matters: No 2025-26 CXR hallucination benchmark ships reliability numbers or a contamination table; the 2x2 separates the token-budget artefact from device size on current dynamic-resolution models; the label-noise sensitivity turns the 15% RANZCR discrepancy into a bounded effect; UAR and IS make Table 2 directly comparable with the direct precedent; reviewers at ML4H, MIDL and BioNLP attack exactly these points.
  inputs: All JSONL outputs; verification flags and second-rater labels; the contamination matrix with the Day-0 card quotes; the hero with min_pixels = max_pixels settable; 100 pairs for the 2x2; 200 swapped-image items for UAR; the E-irr images from step 3; reader forms; statsmodels.
  method: (a) 100 random patient-level split halves at 50/100/150/300 pairs; intra-patient correlation; design effect 1 + (m - 1) rho; (b) retest: P0 on 200 items in a second pod session; (c) P1b vs P1 and P0b vs P0 agreement as prompt stability; (d) PN acquiescence shift and parallel-forms consistency on the primary question; (e) results with and without ambiguous items, second-rater disagreements and NGT incompletely-imaged items; (f) contamination table with the text-only row, the A-vs-U null reference and the real-minus-inserted and real-minus-edited gaps if gated in, else an explicit 'not measurable' line; (g) hero 2x2: 100 pairs at 256/391/640/1,024 tokens (min_pixels = max_pixels), identical content, MedGemma fixed, per-class curves (appendix; Figure 1a stays the main resolution exhibit); (h) lmms-eval vs HF runner token counts and agreement; (i) UAR: the same question on another patient's opposite-label image, 200 items, hero and MedGemma; IS from the E-irr flip rate; (j) conditional logistic model stratified by patient with covariates (view, direction, other-labelled-line count, follow-up gap, age); (k) power memo on realised N with the observed discordance; (l) reader audit as pre-registered in step 3, replacing the v1 student-plus-peer numbers in v2 and stating both.
  output: Appendix tables A (setup, precision, tokens, harness), C (reliability, sensitivity, contamination, power) and D (audit), Figure B (resolution 2x2).
  compute: Pod about 2 h ($0.6) for retest, P1b, PN, UAR and the 2x2; CPU for the rest; reader 2 h plus 2 h preparation.
  risk: Time in Week 5; reader session slips past Week 5.
  fallback: Keep (a), (e), (f), (j), (k), which are CPU-only; PN and P1b to the hero only; the 2x2 to two budgets; the reader results go into the manuscript if they land after v2.
  paper_section: 5.6 (one paragraph), Appendices A, C, D.
  closest_prior_work: HQM/HQH parallel-forms reliability, RePOPE, ODE and Common-O contamination flags (from v1); Lotfinia (UAR, IS, CGR); RAD-DINO and Sabottke and Spieler (resolution); the 2026 benchmark-audit papers on harness divergence; MC-CXR's 10.8% radiologist override rate as the label-noise comparator; Spatially Gated Diffusion (audit rubric).

[9] GATED extensions with kill dates: Arm 3 region-focused agree-or-abstain, open-ended false-premise arm, LLM-Check hidden-state signal, ChestX-Det findings bridge row, MedGemma version row, VCD reference row, ReXVQA anchor, PhysioNet replication, reserve classes
  what: A priority-ordered list of cheap additions, each pre-registered with a go/no-go date, none run before Table 2, Figure 1 and Table 3 are frozen; each item that did not run gets a one-line 'not run' statement with its nearest prior work.
  why_it_matters: Person-time is the binding constraint; explicit gates stop extensions from eating the writing weeks while preserving the highest-value additions: the arm that most literally matches the umbrella's 'grounding' wording (region crop with the random-region control that ARCD and EVADE lack), the HalluLens open-ended analogue, the LLM-Check signal, and a fully releasable devices-vs-findings contrast.
  inputs: (a) Arm 3: crop the 1024-px original to the CheXmask region relevant to the device (trachea/mediastinum for ETT, left hemidiaphragm path for NGT, infraclavicular region for a pacemaker), re-ask, commit only if full-image and crop answers agree, else abstain; controls: random crop of equal size, contralateral or wrong-region crop, full-image repeat (self-consistency), random-location drawn box (VPE); the RANZCR polyline crop as the labelled oracle upper bound, never a method; MedGemma 1.5's own [y0,x0,y1,x1] box as a self-grounding variant with box absence as an abstention trigger; hero and MedGemma. (b) 'Describe the {device} in this chest X-ray.' on B (absent) and A (present control), max_new_tokens 128, a rule classifier validated on 200 hand labels (100 double-rated), FAR_open and TAR. (c) the hero with output_hidden_states: Hidden Score at a dev-chosen layer, image and text blocks separately, visual sink tokens removed; risk-coverage beside the softmax. (d) ChestX-Det bridge row: effusion and consolidation presence on 200-300 real images with the three-radiologist polygons (pixel-hash mapping to NIH names; hero, MedGemma, CheXOne; no edits). (e) the other MedGemma version on A/B/U P0. (f) VCD reference row on the hero (one noised-image pass; alpha 1, beta 0.1) against the constant-bias curve, cited against MedHEval (0.499 to 0.491), Med-VCD (75.79 to 70.89) and VGS-Decoding (-5.93%). (g) ReXVQA tubes/lines and implanted-devices MCQ (gated DUA) for the panel. (h) Chest ImaGenome tubes/lines gold subset if PhysioNet credentials arrive, local hardware only, aggregate reporting. (i) pacemaker or Object-CXR reserve class only under branch (iv), Week 2.
  method: Priority and kill dates: (a) Nov 15, about 1.5 pod hours; (b) Nov 15, 600 generations, 1 pod hour, judge cross-check capped at $2 only if classifier-human agreement is under 90% and only on NIH-derived text; (c) Nov 15, 1 pod hour plus CPU SVDs; (d) Nov 15, 1 pod hour plus a CPU mapping; (e) Nov 8, 0.5 h; (f) Nov 15, 0.5 h; (g) Nov 15, 1 h, only if the DUA is granted; (h) post-term unless credentials arrive by Nov 8; (i) Week 2 only. Results go to Appendix E with the same statistics as the core; Arm 3 is Table 3c.
  output: Appendix E results or 'not run' lines; Table 3c if (a) runs.
  compute: Pod 4-7 h total ($1.5-3.5); 1060 none; API at most $2.
  risk: Any extension starts to eat Week 5; CheXmask crops lose the tube tip; the false-premise classifier misses indirect refusals.
  fallback: Cut in reverse priority; the kill dates are in the OSF registration and the advisor sees them.
  paper_section: Appendix E; Table 3c.
  closest_prior_work: (a) EVADE (Qwen2.5-VL-7B; chain-of-thought hurt), ARCD (Phi-3.5V-Med, MIT, no random-region control), VPE-in-Radiology, HEAL-MedVQA, Gosai et al. grid prompting; (b) HalluLens NonExistentRefusal, KoNA, VOPE, HalluCXR open-description fabrication; (c) LLM-Check, HalluPrism, ReXTrust, VASE; (d) HalluCXR, CheXpercept, ProbMed, MED-HALLUCINOGEN (findings-level NIH presence); (f) VCD, MedHEval, Med-VCD, VGS-Decoding; (g) ReXVQA; (h) Chest ImaGenome, MIMIC-CXR-VQA, MedVH's PhysioNet precedent.

[10] Write-as-you-go report v1 (complete by Nov 8), v2 (Nov 22), release, advisor citation memo and submissions
  what: Draft Sections 1-3 with Table 1 by the end of Week 3; freeze Table 2, Figure 1, Table 3 and Table 4 at the end of Week 4 and assemble a complete report v1 in the course template with every extension as a kill-dated appendix item; extend to v2 with the validity appendix and gated results; release code and the NIH item list; send the citation memo in Week 3; execute the venue timeline.
  why_it_matters: Week 13 (Nov 8-12) may be the literal due date and EACL's Dec 15 is inside finals; a complete v1 at the end of Week 4 is the only schedule that guarantees the deliverable under both scenarios; the memo protects the co-authored paper from inheriting the advisor's citation errors; the release makes LongiDev-CXR reusable by the lab without credentials.
  inputs: All JSONL outputs and tables; ACL 2027 style files; the course template and rubric; the OSF registration; TRIPOD-LLM and CLAIM 2024 checklists; the awesome-list repository.
  method: Main text with one spine: three behaviours (guess, abstain, hallucinate) x three real stimuli (device present, same-patient absent, unpaired absent) plus the verified synthetic probes x two model types (general, medical, with the matched pair): Table 1 floor and construction, Table 2 headline with the claim-2 panel, Table 3 mitigation with controls, Table 4 synthetic probes, Figure 1 (resolution and risk-coverage); Discussion with the advisor's claims 2 and 3, the co-occurrence-prior paragraph in Brooks and Anastasio's vocabulary, the Kalai paragraph, the Kim et al. median note, grounding vs retrieval, and benchmark-design implications (penalty-stated abstention, edit-free negative arms, measured synthetic validity, patient-clustered intervals, token logging, detector-alone and shuffled-expert rows); Limitations (non-expert verification, RANZCR label noise, within-patient contrast not counterfactual, contamination bounded by the matrix not removed, English prompts, two Qwen-family LLM backbones plus Gemma, 2-4B scale, one device family, synthetic probes preliminary or not run, no clinical claim). Every proportion with n and a Wilson interval; every 'first' with 'to our knowledge'. Release: GitHub (mapping notebook, selection scripts, task yaml, runners, statistics, PROTOCOL.md, OSF link, pinned requirements, rebuild script that joins the Kaggle CSV after rule acceptance) and the HF dataset card with NIH filenames, pair ids, masks and per-arm terms; no RANZCR rows. Venue actions: EACL SRW mentorship request Nov 6 (only if v1 exists), NAACL SRW mentorship Nov 16, EMBC manuscript by Jan 24, JURI rolling.
  output: Report v1 (Nov 8), report v2 (Nov 22), SRW/EMBC/MIDL manuscript (Dec 10), public repository and item list, citation-correction memo (Week 3), mentorship requests, JURI submission.
  compute: Writing time about 40-50 h across Weeks 3-8; no GPU.
  risk: Course template incompatible with ACL short; advisor review late; over-claiming; Kaggle rules forbid releasing derived labels.
  fallback: Keep the ACL-short text as the core and wrap it in the course template; freeze v1 regardless; 'to our knowledge' discipline and the related-work table; release NIH filenames and masks only with the rebuild script.
  paper_section: Sections 1, 2, 6, 7, 8 and all appendices.
  closest_prior_work: HalluCXR and RadFlag as the credible-unit templates (about 500-850 images, 2-6 models, 250-550 human labels, one GPU); the ML4H 2024 single-author uncertainty study as proof that an evaluation-only student paper is publishable; MedVH's PhysioNet release precedent; TRIPOD-LLM and CLAIM 2024 for reporting.

MODEL SET:
  - Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0; 2.13B; HERO: cached logits, softmax selection, Figure 1a/1b, the resolution 2x2, interleaved-image retrieval, Arm 3; 16-px patches with 2x2 merge = one token per 32x32 px; fixed at min_pixels = max_pixels = 1024*1024 = 1,024 tokens in the core; relative 0-1000 bbox_2d grounding if needed; lmms-eval qwen3_vl wrapper verified on main; bf16 on the pod, fp32 on Kaggle for backups; contamination 'unknown').
  - OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B (Apache-2.0; 2.35B; Qwen3 LLM with InternViT-300M; 448-px tiles at 256 tokens each, max_num = 6 plus thumbnail logged; use_flash_attn = False if needed; internvl3_5 wrapper verified on main; fallback OpenGVLab/InternVL3-2B; contamination 'unknown'; first panel member on the cut list because it shares the Qwen3 LLM family with the hero).
  - google/gemma-4-E2B-it (Apache-2.0, ungated; 2.3B effective, 5.1B total; the non-Qwen backbone; visual token budgets 70/140/280/560/1120 with 1120 in the core; dtype auto; gemma4 wrapper verified on main; bf16 on the pod only, never fp16; fallback google/gemma-3-4b-it, which is also the encoder-confounded MedGemma base for an appendix row; contamination 'unknown').
  - google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (HAI-DEF terms, gated auto; 4.3B; Gemma 3 architecture with the MedSigLIP encoder; 896 px to 256 tokens fixed, architecturally coarser for thin lines; returns [y0,x0,y1,x1] boxes on a 0-1000 scale for the Arm-3 self-grounding variant; card and tech report (arXiv 2507.05201) read by eye on Day 0 to settle the contradictory CXR14 status in the digest; MEDICAL COMPARATOR for the secondary claim-2 test and positive control in the floor pilot; bf16 only (8-bit produces gibberish per issue #5; NF4 on the 1060 for triage only); loaded through the gemma3 wrapper if it accepts the checkpoint unmodified, else the HF runner with the 99% agreement check; fallback google/medgemma-4b-it).
  - google/medgemma-4b-it (HAI-DEF; v1.0.1): the ReXVQA-anchored positive control in the floor pilot (tubes/lines 83.9%, implanted devices 73.1%); pre-registered rule: 1.5 is the panel member unless it fails GO on the primary class and 4B passes; the other version is a gated appendix row.
  - StanfordAIMI/CheXOne (CC BY-NC 4.0 on the card, CC BY-NC-ND in the paper arXiv 2604.00493; 4.07B per safetensors, Qwen2.5-VL-3B architecture fully fine-tuned on CheXinstruct-v2 including ChestXray14 and Object-CXR pixels, so the PIXEL-CONTAMINATED CXR specialist; instruct mode; max_pixels 512*512 recommended and 1024*1024 also run; ReXVQA presence 0.947; MS-CXR grounding mIoU 0.608; paper evaluates no devices, hallucination or abstention; transformers from main in its own venv, qwen2_5_vl wrapper plausible but unverified; Day-3 load test; P0/P1/P2-hi only; drop-in nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B (NVIDIA OneWay non-commercial; Qwen2.5-VL-3B plus GRPO; also CXR14-contaminated) if it fails to load).
  - Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (Qwen Research License; 3.75B; the exact base of CheXOne with identical encoder, LLM and chat format: the matched pair that isolates CXR post-training for H2; same max_pixels settings as CheXOne; bf16 on the pod; P0/P1/P2-hi only).
  - NOT PANEL MEMBERS (tools): google/medsiglip-448 (HAI-DEF gated; zero-shot device detector with the CCD 13-class prompt pairs from ccd/ccd_utils.py; retrieval embedder; support-devices AUC 0.852 on CheXpert-test labeler labels; trained on MIMIC-CXR and unspecified data, never on RANZCR labels); CheXzero (MIT; MIMIC-only CLIP; 'support devices' vs 'no support devices' template at 320 px; independent verifier and second expert); microsoft/BiomedCLIP-PubMedBERT_256-vit_base_patch16_224 (MIT; retriever ablation); ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes (MIT code, research-only weights; EfficientNet-B1 U-Net plus 5-fold presence classifier, mean AUROC 0.9684, in-sample on RANZCR plus 31,448 NIH images; used ONLY to refine removal masks and the carina placement aid on the probe subsets, never to score real items or as an oracle); torchxrayvision densenet121-res224-mimic_ch (Apache-2.0; MIMIC-only; no support-devices head; drift verifier for unintended opacity changes only); CheXmask (CC BY 4.0); LaMa via IOPaint (Apache-2.0; archived Aug 2025); stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-inpainting (creativeml-openrail-m; fp32 on the 1060); microsoft/radedit (MSRLA weights, MIT pipeline, gated click-through; pod only at 512 px).
  - DROPPED, with the reason stated in the report: SENTINEL (COCO alignment is off-domain); StanfordAIMI/CheXagent-2-3b (transformers==4.40 pin, licence conflict MIT card vs CC BY-NC-ND repo); 7B models Lingshu-7B, LLaVA-Med-1.5, MAIRA-2 (pod-only, disproportionate budget, report-only or 7B); Phi-3.5-vision (at chance on tubes/lines); Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR (no public weights); report-only models (LLaVA-Rad, SRRG, SLaVA-CXR); XReal and SynthCheX-75K (device-free generators; not needed for a device benchmark).

MITIGATION ARMS:
  - ARM 1, detector-gated answers (grounding-based; CPU-only; all six runs): CCD's expert-informed stage re-implemented as one equation on the cached first-token logits: z' = z + beta x clip(log(s/(1-s)), -gamma, gamma), with s from MedSigLIP zero-shot (CCD prompt pairs, per class) or CheXzero (class-agnostic support devices), beta and gamma chosen on the dev split; plus an agree-or-abstain variant scored with Phi_c and coverage. Prerequisite (step 2): the detectors' per-class AUC on RANZCR radiologist labels over a patient-disjoint pool (never published; by-product). CONTROLS at the method's yes-ratio: detector-alone; shuffled-expert (bias from a random other image, 20 draws); Rethinking-CD constant-bias sweep z + b with b* at matched yes-ratio and the full (yes-ratio, accuracy) curve; Mirage output-layer modification forcing the majority direction when |P(yes) - P(no)| < tau; sign-randomised magnitude-matched perturbation; selective override reported with coverage. Readouts: net gain vs matched control with paired patient-clustered bootstrap intervals per arm (A/B/U and gated synthetic survivors), McNemar vs greedy and vs OLM, hit_A beside FAR_B, yes-to-no vs no-to-yes correction shares. Verifier-independence: the Arm-1 expert is never the detector that selected an item's label (CheXzero on any MedSigLIP-shortlisted pacemaker item). Nearest prior: CCD (Findings of ACL 2026; MIT; no detector-alone or shuffled-expert control), Expert-CFG, ChatCAD, MedRAX, FactCheXcker; Mirage and Rethinking-CD (COCO, 7B+ only).
  - ARM 2, similar-case retrieval (retrieval-based; pod): kNN over a patient-disjoint RANZCR-labelled pool with MedSigLIP embeddings (BiomedCLIP ablation), inserting the neighbours' radiologist labels as text ('A radiologist-labelled archive was searched for the {k} radiographs most similar to this one. {m} of {k} contain a {device}.') or as interleaved images with labels for Qwen3-VL-2B and Gemma 4 E2B. On all six runs: relevant k = 3, kNN-label-vote alone (no VLM), mismatched retrieval (neighbours chosen among opposite-label images; When Retrieval Hurts and MC-CXR), and the constant-bias control at matched yes-ratio. On the hero and MedGemma: k in {1, 5}, label-shuffled retrieval, k = 3 on all four questions, the BiomedCLIP retriever ablation, the P2-hi variant for abstention under retrieval; interleaved images on the hero and Gemma 4. Readouts: accuracy, balanced accuracy, FAR_B, FAR_U, hit_A, yes-ratio, MMed-RAG over-reliance rate (greedy-correct items flipped wrong), copy rate (answers equal to the kNN majority), net gain vs kNN-vote-alone and vs the matched constant bias. Pre-registered expectation: relevant retrieval null-to-negative on B (ICU-looking negatives retrieve device-positive neighbours), mismatched retrieval harmful; either outcome is reportable. Nearest prior: RULE and MMed-RAG retrieval-only ablations (7B, fine-tuned afterwards), When Retrieval Hurts 2026, MC-CXR, V-RAG entity probing, MedPromptX, CXR-RePaiR.
  - ARM 3 (gated to v2, kill date Nov 15; hero and MedGemma), region-focused second pass with agree-or-abstain (grounding-based; EVADE-like without native grounding): crop the 1024-px original to the CheXmask-derived region for the queried device (trachea/mediastinum for ETT, left hemidiaphragm path for NGT, infraclavicular region for a pacemaker), re-ask, commit only if full-image and crop answers agree, else abstain; scored with Effective Reliability and coverage; a MedGemma-1.5 self-grounding variant (model box to crop; box absence as an abstention trigger) and a drawn-box prompt variant. CONTROLS: random crop of equal size; contralateral or wrong-region crop; full-image repeat (self-consistency alone); random-location drawn box (VPE); the RANZCR polyline crop as the labelled oracle upper bound, never a method; recall and coverage reported. Nearest prior: EVADE (Qwen2.5-VL-7B; chain-of-thought hurt, so CoT is not an arm), ARCD (Phi-3.5V-Med, MIT, no random-region control), VPE-in-Radiology, HEAL-MedVQA, Gosai et al. grid prompting; none has a random-crop or wrong-region control.
  - REFERENCE ROWS (not arms): greedy; Mirage OLM; the Rethinking-CD constant-bias curve per model; plain VCD on the hero only as a gated reference row (one noised pass; alpha 1, beta 0.1), demoted from headline mitigation because MedHEval (0.499 to 0.491), Med-VCD (75.79 to 70.89) and VGS-Decoding (-5.93%) show it null-to-negative on CXR VQA, while the Mirage and Rethinking-CD controls have never been run on medical images or below 7B; VGS-Decoding's reweighting only if its code appears before Week 3.
  - NOT RUN THIS TERM, cited as future work with the reason (person-time) stated so reviewers see the choice: Arm 4 knowledge-grounded prompting with a StatPearls or textbook snippet from the MedRAG corpora versus an irrelevant snippet of equal length and CCD's 'Attention to:' expert prompt (expected null-to-negative per the MICCAI 2026 vision-attention ratio of 0.06 under appended text and RULE); fine-tuned RAG (RULE/MMed-RAG preference tuning, V-RAG retriever training) is cited as the upper bound not attempted. Common rules: every gain net of its yes-ratio-matched control with a paired patient-cluster bootstrap interval; informativeness (hit_A, coverage, over-reliance, copy rate, the 'playing it safe' recall cost) beside every FAR; nothing trained anywhere; budget impact about 5-8 extra pod hours.

TIMELINE WEEKS: 8

REPORT OUTLINE:
  - 1. Introduction: device-existence hallucination in small open VLMs on chest radiographs; evaluations that reward guessing (Kalai et al.; HalluCXR, CheXpercept and MC-CXR forbid or ignore abstention); the edit-free same-patient negative arm as an artefact-free probe beside measured synthetic probes; resolution and the visual-token budget as uncontrolled factors in dynamic-resolution models; the advisor's framework (Alshahrani and Behzad 2026) and its two testable claims; four questions and five contributions, each with its 'new relative to' statement (Lotfinia 2026, ReXVQA, CARES, Kim et al. 2025, CCD, RULE/MMed-RAG, Sogancioglu 2018, Spatially Gated Diffusion named).
  - 2. Related work: CXR hallucination benchmarks (HalluCXR, MedVH, MedHEval/CXR-VisHal, CARES, Med-HallMark, CheXpercept, MC-CXR, HEAL-MedVQA, MED-HALLUCINOGEN, ProbMed, ReXVQA, GEMeX); image-intervention audits (Lotfinia, DualRead, ModaLens); edited and generated CXRs (RadEdit, RoentMod, XReal, Spatially Gated Diffusion, MedDiME, Sogancioglu, MaskMedPaint); devices (FactCheXcker, Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR, RANZCR CLiP, ianpan); abstention and selective prediction (CARES, EVADE, DualRead, CONRep, Med-HALT, HALT-MedVQA, Reliable VQA, ReXTrust, RadFlag, VASE); mitigation and its critiques (CCD, Expert-CFG, FactCheXcker, ARCD, EVADE, RULE, MMed-RAG, V-RAG, When Retrieval Hurts, MedHEval, Med-VCD, VGS-Decoding, Mirage, Rethinking-CD, Playing it Safe); label noise and contamination (Oakden-Rayner, Rafferty and Rajan, the RANZCR 15% discrepancy, model cards); theory backdrop (Huang, Xu, Kalai, HalluLens); a compact table with columns: object type, source arms, counterfactual type, radiologist labels, edit validity (verifier, 2AFC, reader), abstention scored, penalty stated, intervals, sub-4B models, contamination reported.
  - 3. LongiDev-CXR: 3.1 RANZCR-to-NIH mapping and realised yield; 3.2 same-patient pairs, context-matched negatives, four-question items and covariates; 3.3 the floor pilot and the pre-registered primary class (Result 1); 3.4 verification and reliability statistics, token budget, encoding, contamination matrix and licences; 3.5 the synthetic probes (removal gate, insertion gate; preliminary in v1, full arms in v2 if gated in).
  - 4. Penalty-scored evaluation protocol: P0/P1/P2 with the verbatim Kalai instruction and the dose-response design; PN, P0b and the prior rows; hit_A, FAR_B, FAR_U, persistence rate, co-occurrence stratum; Effective Reliability and the Kalai rule; detector-gated and retrieval arms with their control sets; softmax selection; statistics, clustering and pre-registration.
  - 5. Experiments: 5.1 models, harness, precision policy, token budgets and the floor table (Table 1); 5.2 Table 2: guess, abstain or hallucinate across device-present, same-patient-absent and unpaired-absent radiographs, with the claim-2 panel (CheXOne vs base; MedGemma vs generalists, confounded); 5.3 Figure 1a: resolution as a factor; 5.4 Figure 1b: softmax risk-coverage, threshold transfer and the verbal-vs-logit gap; 5.5 Table 3: detector-gated answers and similar-case retrieval against their yes-ratio-matched controls (Table 3c region crop in v2); 5.6 Table 4 and validity in one paragraph (synthetic probes, reader audit, contamination gap, reliability).
  - 6. Discussion: the advisor's claims 2 and 3 under image-grounded, controlled conditions; the co-occurrence prior in Brooks and Anastasio's data-vs-confabulation vocabulary and MediHall severity terms; the Kalai paragraph and the Med-HALT precedent; the Kim et al. median replication note; LLM-Check signals as the cheapest abstention gate; grounding vs retrieval and retrieval as a risk under the lifecycle framing; benchmark-design implications (penalty-stated abstention, edit-free negative arms, measured synthetic validity, patient-clustered intervals, token logging, detector-alone and shuffled-expert rows); largest-effect ranking (floor and resolution vs prior vs penalty vs mitigation net gain).
  - 7. Limitations: non-expert verification; RANZCR label noise; within-patient contrast, not counterfactual; contamination bounded by the matrix, not removed (generalists unknown); two Qwen-family LLM backbones plus Gemma; 2-4B scale; English prompts; one device family; synthetic probes preliminary or not run; reader audit by one resident at most; no clinical claim.
  - 8. Conclusion and release statement (NIH item list with attribution, masks, task yaml, scripts, OSF; no RANZCR rows).
  - Appendix A: setup, precision checks, token counts, harness agreement and divergence, pod prices and ledger. Appendix B: dataset card, mapping statistics, prompts, verification and audit (kappa, PABAK), reference sheets, the contamination matrix with card quotes. Appendix C: full tables and tests, power memo, reliability block, label-noise and contamination sensitivity, resolution 2x2, UAR/IS. Appendix D: synthetic probes (three removers x verifier pass, protected-region error, 2AFC, reader rubric; insertion yield and gate) with a status line (ran / preliminary / not run) and kill date. Appendix E: gated extensions (Arm 3, open-ended false-premise arm, Hidden Score, ChestX-Det bridge row, MedGemma version row, VCD reference row, ReXVQA anchor, PhysioNet replication) with results or a one-line 'not run' plus nearest prior work. Appendix F: the citation-correction note for the advisor's framework.

TOP RISKS:
  - Floor effect: every 2B generalist at or near chance on every tube class. Mitigation: the Day-2 pilot with the pre-registered GO rule and branches (iii) floor paper with a MedGemma-centred core and (iv) pacemaker reserve, each still a complete paper; the floor table is a first.
  - Realised pair yield below 200 for the primary class (label totals are not image counts; device states may not change within patient). Mitigation: the Day-1 yield query before any power claim; the relaxation ladder (view mismatch, two pairs per patient with clustering, pooled ETT+NGT, class switch, unpaired A/U design).
  - Mapping failure (dimension key not unique; JPEG sizes uninformative). Mitigation: pixel-hash first pass, pHash over all 112,120 NIH images (about 40 CPU-minutes), 200-pair visual spot check, unmatched items excluded and counted.
  - Kaggle rules forbid an assumed use (derived works, off-platform processing, label-derived prompt text). Mitigation: rules read and quoted on Day 0; labels never leave Kaggle and the student's machine; the pod sees NIH pixels and prompt text only; release by rebuild script; ReXVQA/ReXGradient (gated DUA, no CITI) as the fallback real set.
  - Same-patient pairs confounded by changes other than the device (other lines, pathology, positioning). Mitigation: never called a counterfactual; U negatives matched on other-labelled-line presence; covariates (view, direction, other-line count, follow-up gap, NIH labels as covariates); the co-occurrence stratification as the hypothesis; the conditional model stratified by patient; the persistence rate as the device-sensitivity readout.
  - Person-time (the plan with grafts is about 95-110 h before Nov 8 against roughly 80-100 available with the break week): six-run panel but only three prompts on four of them, two mitigation arms with Arm 1 CPU-only, probes capped at 40 and 100 images with kill dates, write-as-you-go from Week 3, a named second rater, the ordered cut list applied from the first slipped day.
  - Due date literally Week 13 (Nov 8-12). Mitigation: Day-0 written confirmation; report v1 complete at the end of Week 4; extensions are appendix items with kill dates.
  - Pod price and availability (official page now $0.27 A5000, $0.50 3090, $0.89 4090; community availability fluctuates). Mitigation: A5000 default, 3090 fallback, $30 pod cap, incremental JSONL with resume by image id, network volume, Kaggle fp32 backup for the Qwen3-backbone models, the cut list halves hours if prices double.
  - Precision and wrappers: fp16 unsafe for Gemma and MedGemma; MedGemma 8-bit gibberish; CheXOne needs transformers from main; no MedGemma or CheXOne lmms-eval entry. Mitigation: uniform bf16 on the pod, one venv per family, the HF runner as the primary path with the 99% agreement check, NV-Reason-CXR-3B and gemma-3-4b-it as drop-ins.
  - Synthetic probes fail their gates or no reader is obtained. Mitigation: the pair arm carries the paper; a failed gate is itself the first LaMa/SD/RadEdit device-removal detectability measurement on CXR; readers default to student plus peer with the limitation stated; the reader request goes out in Week 1 with a Nov 15 kill date.
  - Contamination confounds the general-vs-medical comparison (NIH pixels seen by every specialist; generalists unknown; MedGemma's card read inconsistently). Mitigation: the Day-0 card check and verbatim quotes; RANZCR device labels listed by no model, so the test is label-clean; the matrix, the text-only leak row, the A-vs-U null reference and the synthetic gaps if gated in; no 'unseen' claims without a card citation.
  - Verifier circularity (ianpan in-sample on RANZCR; detectors labelling items; MedSigLIP both selector and expert on reserve items). Mitigation: radiologist labels only; ianpan restricted to masks and placement on the probe subsets; MedSigLIP and CheXzero as the only verifiers and experts with their AUC validated on held-out labels first; the expert swap rule on shortlisted items.
  - Abstention extremes, chance-level softmax signals, null or negative mitigation effects, PN acquiescence. Mitigation: all pre-registered as reportable outcomes with intervals; controls make a null interpretable.
  - IRB processing time or an unexpected full-review requirement. Mitigation: request sent in Week 1 citing Articles 10.19 and 10.33; public de-identified data only; the determination or its pending status stated in the report; the reader session is the only deferrable element.
  - Novelty over-claims (Lotfinia's support-devices block and the CCD detector's accuracy could not be fully verified by the sweeps). Mitigation: confirm both from the repositories on Day 0; cite Lotfinia for wording and the image-removal control; validate the MedSigLIP device prompts on RANZCR labels before citing them as a precedent; 'to our knowledge' discipline; the Week-1 search log.

WHAT TO CUT IF BEHIND:
  - 1. Every Step-9 extension in reverse priority (PhysioNet replication, ReXVQA anchor, VCD reference row, MedGemma version row, ChestX-Det bridge row, Hidden Score, open-ended false-premise arm, Arm 3 region crop), then the full edited and inserted arms beyond the 40-image and 100-image probes.
  - 2. Arm 2's extra conditions on the hero and MedGemma (k sweep, label-shuffled, BiomedCLIP ablation, interleaved images, P2-hi variant); keep relevant k = 3, kNN-vote-alone and mismatched on every run.
  - 3. P2-lo, PN, P0b and BLIND-demo to the hero only; keep P0, P1 and P2-hi for every run on every arm.
  - 4. Secondary classes: NGT pairs to 100 and CVC pairs to 50, or CVC reported only through the floor table; U to 200 per class.
  - 5. The SLAKE anchor, the P1b paraphrase row, UAR and the hero resolution 2x2 (keep the floor table as the resolution evidence).
  - 6. The insertion probe to 50 hosts and the removal probe to LaMa plus E-irr only (RadEdit and SD-1.5 dropped), each reported with its gate numbers.
  - 7. The panel to five runs (drop InternVL3.5-2B first, since it shares the Qwen3 LLM family with the hero), then to four (drop Gemma 4 E2B if it fails the pod path and state 'one LLM backbone family' as the first limitation); never drop the CheXOne/Qwen2.5-VL-3B pair or MedGemma, which carry H2.
  - NEVER CUT: the mapping and realised-yield table; the floor table and the pre-registered class decision; same-patient pairs A/B with radiologist labels and U negatives; non-expert verification with the 200-item second-rater audit and kappa; P0/P1/P2-hi with Phi_c scoring and the blind rows; hit_A and the persistence rate beside FAR_B and FAR_U; Arm 1 with the detector-alone and shuffled-expert controls; Arm 2 relevant k = 3 with the kNN-vote-alone and mismatched controls; the 40-image removal probe with its two-verifier and 2AFC gate (even if it fails, the number is reported); patient-clustered tests with Wilson intervals; token logging; OSF pre-registration; the contamination matrix with card quotes; the dataset card; the citation-correction memo; report v1 complete at the end of Week 4.


######## SYNTHESIS RATIONALE ########
Spine and why. All three judges ranked minimum-risk-nov8 (LongiDev-CXR) first on the only criterion that decides whether anything exists on Nov 8: person-time. Its never-cut core (mapping and yield, floor pilot, A/B/U pairs with radiologist labels, 200-item audit, P0/P1/P2-hi with Phi_c, CPU-only Arm 1 with controls, Arm 2 at k = 3 with controls, patient-clustered tests, OSF, v1) is about 80-95 h, while device-triplet-core and findings-release-first each need 160-210 h before v1. The device-triplet draft is therefore used as the graft bank and as the v2/paper roadmap (its E and S arms become this plan's gated probes), and the findings draft is used only for release and verification tooling plus one gated comparability row, because (a) the medical digest rates no ChestX-Det class as safely non-expert-verifiable, (b) finding removal has no working precedent and the student cannot judge a re-synthesised lung base, (c) findings-level NIH presence is the most-covered CXR hallucination setting and its NLP labels sit in every specialist's training mix, and (d) the brief's domain verdict and the student's 'object hallucination' title point to devices. Grafts adopted (all three judges agreed on the first two, and the rest cost under 2 pod hours each): the CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B matched pair promoted into the core on P0/P1/P2-hi behind a Day-3 load test (the only label-clean, encoder-matched test of the advisor's claim 2; NV-Reason-CXR-3B as the drop-in); the CPU cut-paste insertion promoted to a capped 100-host probe so v1 carries a disclosed synthetic row; the 40-image three-remover pilot moved from Week 3 to Week 2 with the full gate (two independent verifiers, the Spatially Gated Diffusion protected-region check from the findings draft, E-irr, 2AFC, two-reader rubric) and a preliminary hero/MedGemma row in v1; the S1-S5 strata and the probe-table layout as the Table 2 co-occurrence panel; the floor pilot reported as Figure 1a under a pre-registered H7 (items already run); Arm 3 region crop with random-crop and wrong-region controls and the oracle polyline crop as a Nov-15 v2 item; BLIND-demo, P0b (HalluCXR wording), 'hallucination-free rate' as the advisor's quantity, SHA-256 pixel-buffer mapping key, torchxrayvision -mimic_ch as a drift-only verifier, the HF dataset card with a rebuild script, Lotfinia's UAR/IS rows, and the pacemaker verifier-independence rule. Contradictions resolved. (1) Arm 2 scope: the advisor wanted the full control set on all six runs, the reviewer and engineer the k = 3 core; resolution: the three rows that make retrieval interpretable (relevant k = 3, kNN-vote-alone, mismatched) on every run, everything else on the hero and MedGemma, which keeps Arm 2 at 4-6 A5000 hours. (2) Arm 3 timing: advisor and engineer put it in Week 5, the reviewer hero-only; resolution: hero first, MedGemma second, Nov 15 kill date, v2 only. (3) Insertion hosts: the engineer pasted into B images, the advisor into U hosts; resolution: B hosts first so each pair becomes an A / B / B-plus-synthetic triplet, U hosts as the fallback. (4) Removal-pilot timing: Week 2 (engineer) rather than Week 3 (winner, reviewer), because it reuses the rasterised polylines the insertion needs anyway, runs on the 1060 overnight while the pod runs the core, and lets the automatic gates close by Nov 1. (5) Wording: all judges rejected 'counterfactual'; the title and every section now say edit-free same-patient negative arm or within-patient contrast, the compliance sentence admits aggregate label-derived prompt text, and 'no other device' became 'no other labelled line'. Corrections made against the brief and the candidates, each verified today: RunPod's official pricing page lists RTX 3090 $0.50/h, RTX 4090 $0.89/h and RTX A5000 $0.27/h, not $0.22-0.34, so the budget is rebuilt around the A5000 (24 GB, bf16) at 35-50 hours with the 3090 as fallback and still lands at $12-28 under the $40 ceiling; lmms-eval main does ship gemma4, gemma3, qwen3_vl, qwen2_5_vl and internvl3_5 wrappers but no MedGemma or CheXOne entry, so the HF runner is primary only for those two and the task yaml stays a single file; the SD-1.5 inpainting checkpoint resolves to stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-inpainting (creativeml-openrail-m, ungated); the medical digest records Lotfinia's prompt as 'Is [finding] present ...' rather than the brief's 'visible', so P0 uses 'present' pending a Day-0 check of the MIT repo, with 'visible' kept as a reliability paraphrase; the digest contains two contradictory reads of MedGemma 1.5's CXR14 status, so the contamination matrix carries whichever the card supports after a by-eye check and the claim-2 test is designed to be label-clean regardless (no model lists RANZCR device labels); the CCD device-prompt file (ccd/ccd_utils.py, 13 classes plus No_devices over medsiglip-448) is verified by one digest item but reported unlocatable by another, so the Day-0 checklist opens the file before Arm 1 depends on it; torchxrayvision has no support-devices head, so the findings draft's claim is not propagated. Publishable whichever way the numbers fall: a floor on every tube class is Result 1 with MedGemma as positive control; a persistence rate near 1 is a prior-driven finding and near 0 a perception finding; zero abstention beside informative logits is a representation-action gap; a mitigation point on the constant-bias curve is a bias shift; a failed inpainting gate is the first measured LaMa/SD/RadEdit device-removal detectability on radiographs; null-to-negative retrieval is pre-registered. Honest accounting: with the grafts the pre-Nov-8 workload is about 95-110 h against roughly 80-100 available including the mid-term break, so Week 1 is written at 25-30 h, the two probes are hard-capped at 40 and 100 images with kill dates, four of the six runs get only three prompts, and the ordered cut list is to be applied from the first slipped day, not 'if behind' at the end.


######## JUDGES ########

=== advisor === winner: minimum-risk-nov8
   device-triplet-core: total=53 nov=9 feas=4 rig=9 paper=8 fit=9 tang=8 rob=6
      Rank 2. As a design document this is the best of the three: devices as the objects (the brief's verdict, and the digest's own note that thin radiopaque lines are the one structure LaMa can plausibly remove on a CXR), matched R/E/L/S arms with the edit-free L arm as a hedge, the CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B pair as the clean test of my claim 2, four grounding/retrieval arms with the controls CCD/RULE/ARCD omit, patient-clustered statistics and OSF pre-registration. It tests my claims in my vocabulary and the citation-fix memo is exactly what I want before co-authoring. The problem is purely execution: the plan's own person-time figure (100-120 h) is not consistent with its content.       Scientifically the richest plan and the best title/advisor fit (E and S in the core, four mitigation arms, the CheXOne/base pair, resolution as a factor, three publishable by-products), with v1's protocol discipline intact. But it repeats the exact failure the v1 feasibility review flagged: person-time. Its own Week 1 (RANZCR-to-NIH mapping and yield, five venvs and a six-model smoke test on the pod, a 10,800-14,400-item floor pilot at three resolutions, MedSigLIP/CheXzero validation, a three-remover pilot with RadEdit on the pod and SD-1.5 crops on the 1060, a 2AFC, PROTOCOL.md, OSF, filings, citation search) is 45-55 h for a 15-20 h/week student; Weeks 2-4 add E/E-sd/E-radedit/E-irr/L/S construction with by-eye verification of every image, seven task yamls, about 210k core items over six models, a 30k-item resolution sweep, four mitigation arms with roughly 15 controls (about 150k items), Hidden Score curves and a complete v1. My estimate is 170-210 h before Nov 8 against 60-80 available; even the 'never cut' list is 100-120 h. Three candidate primary classes (one needing about 800 hand-labelled pacemaker reads, one needing a fragile Object-CXR download) mean the class decision itself eats the first week. GPU budget is fine (35-50 pod hours, about $12-25 at aggregator prices; note RunPod's official page lists the 3090 at $0.50/h and the 4090 at $0.89/h, so check the console or use the $0.27 A5000). Harness risk is lower than the draft fears: lmms-eval main today has gemma4.py, gemma3.py, qwen3_vl.py, qwen2_5_vl.py and internvl3_5.py (no medgemma/chexone entry), so the HF-script fallback matters only for MedGemma and CheXOne (CheXOne needs transformers from main, hence its own venv). Rigor on paper is excellent (two independent verifiers, 2AFC, untouched negative controls, patient clustering, pre-registered class rule), but rigor that cannot be executed by Nov 8 does not count; if the cut list is applied from Day 1 the plan converges on Draft 3 plus an E arm, which is why its parts are the main graft source.
   findings-release-first: total=42 nov=7 feas=3 rig=6 paper=7 fit=7 tang=8 rob=4
      The strongest release story of the three (NIH pixels plus Apache-2.0 ChestX-Det polygons by three board-certified radiologists plus CC BY CheXmask plus Apache-2.0 SynthCheX, so R, LaMa-E, masks, mapping and items can ship on HF), better-validated verifiers than the device drafts (CheXzero and torchxrayvision-mimic_ch carry effusion heads; no open model carries a per-device head), a lower floor risk for large-area findings, and a correct catch that ChestX-Det files are renumbered (confirmed today: 36200.png-style names in ChestX_Det_train.json), so a pixel-hash mapping is needed for patient clustering and CheXmask. But it overturns the brief's verdict (devices as the objects; findings only as a bridge with expert verification) and puts the hardest possible edit on the critical path: erasing a 2-25%-of-frame basal effusion means re-synthesising a costophrenic angle and diaphragm contour, which LaMa and SD-1.5 (natural-image priors) cannot do convincingly and which RadEdit failed at in the one precedent; a non-expert 2AFC on 512-px crops will almost certainly detect every remover, and the draft's own fallback concedes that E then becomes a trace-confounded probe. The student cannot judge whether a 'cleared' lung base is anatomically plausible, so the gate collapses to classifier flips, the circularity the brief warned against. Paired S depends on XReal (Python 3.8, taming-transformers, SharePoint weights, 256 px, MIMIC AP style, effusion never demonstrated), a hidden-engineering sink; SynthCheX is unpaired and device-free. Person-time is as overloaded as Draft 1 (six runs x four classes, three blind rows, PN, a false-premise arm with a 200-label classifier, attention-kernel signals, four arms on three models, Google-form adjudicated labels, CTR checks). PhysioNet is correctly off the path and the lmms-eval/HF-runner split is sensible. A good v2 or follow-up design once a radiologist reader is secured; not the plan most likely to yield a defensible v1 by Nov 8.
   minimum-risk-nov8: total=51 nov=7 feas=7 rig=8 paper=6 fit=7 tang=7 rob=9
      Closest to the minimal core the v1 feasibility reviewer prescribed (four runs, real arms first, P0/P1/P2, blind rows, one-model logit curves, audit, statistics, everything else behind dated gates) and the only draft whose 'never cut' list (mapping and realised yield, floor pilot, A/B/U pairs with radiologist labels, 200-item second-rater audit with kappa, P0/P1/P2-hi with Phi_c, CPU-only Arm 1 with detector-alone and shuffled-expert controls, Arm 2 at k=3 with kNN-vote-alone and mismatched controls, patient-clustered tests, OSF, v1) fits in roughly 80-95 h, reachable with its listed cuts (its Week-1 '14 h' is optimistic; 25-30 h is realistic for a first-time venv matrix plus mapping). The edit-free longitudinal negative arm is the medical digest's own recommended fallback and a verified gap: artefact-free and radiologist-labelled, at the cost of being a natural experiment rather than a counterfactual, which the context-matched U negatives, covariates, direction/follow-up-gap fields and the co-occurrence stratification handle honestly. Pod use is modest (about 112k core plus 56k retrieval items, 20-30 h), the pod only ever receives NIH pixels and question text (clean under the Kaggle rules whatever they say about off-platform transfer), the HF runner is the primary path with lmms-eval as the 99% agreement check and SLAKE anchor (the right call: no MedGemma/CheXOne wrapper exists), and the ianpan/CheXzero/MedSigLIP roles are leak-free by construction. Weaknesses: the title's 'synthetic visual data' is only a gated extension, so v1 could contain no synthetic image at all; H2 (MedGemma 1.5 vs 2B generalists) is confounded by scale and the MedSigLIP encoder while the clean CheXOne/base pair sits in Step 9a; two mitigation arms instead of four; the realised pair yield is unverified until Day 2 (the relaxation ladder covers it). All of these are fixed by the grafts below, after which the paper upside approaches Draft 1's at a fraction of the schedule risk.
   GRAFTS:
     - From device-triplet-core and findings-release-first: promote the CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B matched pair from Step 9a into the core panel (six runs, or five by dropping InternVL3.5-2B, which shares the Qwen3 LLM family with the hero), gated only by a Day-3 load test in its own venv on transformers main, with nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B as the drop-in if CheXOne fails (both are standard Qwen2_5_VL classes). About 3-4 extra pod hours; it is the only clean test of the advisor's claim 2 at or below 4B, whereas MedGemma-vs-2B is confounded by scale and the MedSigLIP encoder.
     - From device-triplet-core: move the 40-image three-remover pilot (LaMa via IOPaint fp32 on the 1060, SD-1.5 inpainting crops, RadEdit at 512 px on the pod) from Week 3 to Week 2 with Draft 1's full gate (CheXzero and MedSigLIP both below the 90th percentile of real class-negative scores, a 2AFC not significantly above 50%, an E-irr irrelevant-region fill) and run the hero and MedGemma on the gated survivors under P0/P1/P2-hi, so that report v1 always carries a labelled 'preliminary edited arm (n = 40, reader audit pending)' table. This closes the title's 'synthetic visual data' gap without putting E on the critical path.
     - From device-triplet-core: build the CPU-only cut-paste insertion pipeline (polyline crop from a donor A image, intensity-matched, feathered 5-10 px, placed along the CheXmask tracheal midline) in Week 2 alongside the remover pilot, since both reuse the same rasterised polylines; gate 100 inserted items with the mirror criterion (verifier scores above the 10th percentile of real positives) and a 50-pair real-vs-inserted 2AFC; report hit rate on inserted vs real A as the 'synthetic present' counterpart of the pair (a Week-4 row if the gate passes, otherwise Appendix D).
     - From device-triplet-core: the E-irr control and Lotfinia's three comparability metrics (CGR analogue, Unrelated-Image Answer Rate on 200 swapped-image items, Irrelevant-Mask Stability) as appendix rows; UAR costs about 400 pod items and makes Table 2 directly comparable with the direct precedent (arXiv 2606.17710).
     - From findings-release-first: add the Spatially Gated Diffusion protected-region check (mean absolute error outside the dilated mask below the 95th percentile of PNG re-encoding noise) and symmetric pass/fail verifier criteria for real and synthetic items to the edit and insertion gates; a few lines of numpy that answer the 'unintended change' reviewer question with a number instead of a sentence.
     - From findings-release-first: a BLIND-demo prior row (question plus 'This is an {AP/PA} chest radiograph of a {age}-year-old {sex}' from NIH metadata) next to BLIND-text and BLIND-grey; a 1,400-item text-only run on metadata Draft 3 already joins, testing whether ICU-film demographics alone drive 'yes'.
     - From device-triplet-core and findings-release-first: Arm 3, the region-focused second pass (crop to the CheXmask region relevant to the device, re-ask, commit on agreement else abstain, scored with Phi_c and coverage) with its random-crop and wrong-region controls, on the hero only, as a Week-5 gated extension of about 2 pod hours. Draft 3 drops it for person-time, but it is the arm that most literally matches the umbrella's 'grounding' wording and the random-region control is the verified gap in ARCD/EVADE.
     - From findings-release-first: a fully releasable findings-level bridge row (R only, no edits) on 300-450 ChestX-Det images for effusion, consolidation, cardiomegaly and pneumothorax with the three-radiologist polygons as labels, as a Week-5 gated extension (about 7k items, 1 pod hour, plus the pixel-hash mapping to NIH names because ChestX-Det files are renumbered). It gives a devices-vs-findings contrast, a comparability row to HalluCXR and CheXpercept, and a sub-benchmark whose images can ship on HF.
   FATAL FLAWS:
     - [device-triplet-core] Person-time is 2.5-3x over before Nov 8: Week 1 alone needs 45-55 h (mapping and yield, five venvs and a six-model pod smoke test, a 10,800-14,400-item floor pilot at three resolutions, detector validation, a three-remover pilot including RadEdit and SD-1.5 crops, a 2AFC, PROTOCOL.md, OSF, filings, citation search) against 15-20 h/week, and the 'never cut' core (E with its gate, R/E/L at four prompts on six runs, S, the removed-device probe, contamination gap, patient-clustered tests, v1) is 100-120 h on its own. As written the student reaches Nov 8 with runs in flight and no report, the exact failure mode the v1 feasibility review identified; the plan is only viable if its own cut list is applied from Day 1.
     - [device-triplet-core] The primary-class decision is itself a week of work: three candidates (ETT, pacemaker, foreign objects) where pacemaker needs about 800 hand-labelled reads with non-expert labels and foreign objects need a fragile Object-CXR download (original site 404, Drive link untested, 13.6 GB torrent), all decided by a Day-3 pilot that also has to run six models at three resolutions. Hidden engineering on top: seven lmms-eval task yamls, a cut-paste insertion pipeline, three removers, four mitigation arms with about 15 controls and 150k items, and Hidden Score extraction, all before a complete v1.
     - [findings-release-first] Finding removal by inpainting is the hardest edit in the plan family and sits on the critical path: a 2-25%-of-frame basal effusion needs anatomical re-synthesis (costophrenic angle, diaphragm contour, lung markings) that LaMa and SD-1.5 (natural-image priors) cannot supply and that RadEdit failed at in the one precedent; the non-expert 2AFC will detect every remover, and the draft's own fallback concedes that E then becomes a trace-confounded probe. A model answering 'yes' to a smeared lung base is not a measured hallucination.
     - [findings-release-first] The student cannot verify a findings-level counterfactual (the brief states expert verification is needed for findings), so the edit gate reduces to classifier flips plus a non-expert artefact read, which is the circularity the brief warned against; and the draft overturns the brief's object verdict (devices as objects, findings only as a bridge), so 'object hallucination' in the component title and the seven-agent consensus are not honoured. Paired S additionally depends on XReal (Python 3.8 stack, taming-transformers, SharePoint weights, 256 px, MIMIC AP style, effusion never demonstrated), a hidden-engineering sink; the fallback is an unpaired, device-free SynthCheX stratum. Person-time is as overloaded as Draft 1.
     - [minimum-risk-nov8] Not fatal but must be fixed by grafts 1-3: the title's 'synthetic visual data' exists only as gated extensions (remover pilot in Week 3, inserted arm by Nov 15), so v1 may contain no synthetic image; and the core test of the advisor's claim 2 (MedGemma 1.5 vs three 2B generalists) is confounded by scale and the MedSigLIP encoder while the clean CheXOne/Qwen2.5-VL-3B pair is deferred to Step 9a.
     - [minimum-risk-nov8] Not fatal: same-patient pairs are natural experiments, not counterfactuals (positioning, pathology and other devices change between studies), the realised number of view-matched present/absent pairs per class is unknown until the Day-2 yield query, and the Week-1 person-time ('about 14 h') is understated by roughly 2x; the relaxation ladder, U negatives, covariates and co-occurrence stratification cover the first two, and the cut list covers the third.
   ADVICE: Adopt minimum-risk-nov8 (LongiDev-CXR) as the plan of record and treat device-triplet-core as the graft bank, not the plan. The decisive fact is person-time, not GPU-time or money: every draft fits the $40 ceiling (20-50 pod hours; verify the RunPod console, since the official page lists the 3090 at $0.50/h and the 4090 at $0.89/h, and the $0.27 A5000 is the safe 24 GB bf16 choice), but only Draft 3's never-cut core fits the 60-80 h a 15-20 h/week student has before Nov 8. Draft 1 and Draft 2 each need 160-210 h before v1 and would reach Week 13 with runs in flight. Make four changes to Draft 3 on Day 1: (1) promote the CheXOne/Qwen2.5-VL-3B pair into the core behind a Day-3 load test (NV-Reason-CXR-3B as the drop-in), so the advisor's claim 2 is tested on a matched pair rather than MedGemma-vs-2B; (2) pull the 40-image three-remover pilot and the CPU cut-paste insertion pipeline into Week 2 with Draft 1's two-verifier, 2AFC and E-irr gate, and run the hero and MedGemma on whatever survives, so v1 always carries a labelled preliminary synthetic exhibit that honours the title; keep the full E and S arms as v2 items with the Nov 1 and Nov 15 kill dates; (3) keep the HF runner as the primary path (lmms-eval main today has gemma4, gemma3, qwen3_vl, qwen2_5_vl and internvl3_5 wrappers but no MedGemma or CheXOne entry; use it only for the 99% agreement check and the SLAKE anchor, one task yaml, not seven); (4) rewrite Week 1 at 25-30 h, not 14, and freeze the four-plus-two-run core by Oct 18. The first real go/no-go is the Day-2 realised pair yield per class (RANZCR PatientID plus the mapping; print the table per relaxation rung before any power claim), the second is the floor pilot with MedGemma as positive control. Keep the hidden-engineering sinks out of the critical path: XReal, RadEdit as a core remover, pacemaker hand-labelling, Object-CXR, attention-kernel signals, four mitigation arms before Nov 8, and anything PhysioNet (file CITI on Day 1, report only in aggregate if it ever arrives). Mitigation stays at Arm 1 (CPU-only detector fusion with detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias and OLM controls, which cannot slip) and Arm 2 (retrieval at k=3 with kNN-vote-alone and mismatched controls, pre-registered as null-to-negative), with Arm 3 as a Week-5 hero-only extension. Write Sections 1-3 from Oct 26, send the advisor the citation-fix memo in Week 3, and ship v1 at the end of Week 4 whichever scenario the course confirms; a complete report with an honest longitudinal device benchmark, penalised abstention, controlled mitigation and a preliminary edited/inserted appendix is a credible ML4H Findings or MIDL short paper, and the v2 E/S arms are the upgrade path, not a precondition.


######## SKEPTIC REPORTS ########

=== novelty === verdict: The spine survives but several headline 'firsts' do not. Confirmed new after an adversarial sweep: the edit-free same-patient device-present/absent negative arm (no temporal CXR benchmark includes support devices; CheXTemporal excludes them by design), instructed penalty-stated abstention with Effective Reliability on CXR device items, the bias-controlled detector-fusion arm (detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias, OLM rows), the validity gate for device removal and insertion (2AFC, two-reader rubric, protected-region error, two independent verifiers), validation of MedSigLIP device prompts and CheXzero's support-devices template on RANZCR labels, and open 2B-generalist device-class numbers with the token budget logged. Refuted or substantially pre-empted: C3's 'first matched-pair test of general vs medical at <= 4B' (McLaughlin et al., Apr 2026, ran Gemma-3-4B vs MedGemma-4B on CheXpert with refusals), the claim that devices are absent from VLM evaluations (Güzel et al., Diagnostics May 2026, ran the same four devices on RANZCR; TAIX-VQA, ML4H 2025, is a public device yes/no set incl. pacemaker and chest tube), Arm 3's 'no random-crop control' (EVADE Table 3 has one and finds it changes nothing), the 'no reliability numbers on CXR' claim (PSF-Med, Consistent but Dangerous), the 'one failed precedent' for RadEdit removal (Xiong et al. 2026 at scale with a background-edit control; RadCF device removal with code), and the insertion method (Frid-Adar MICCAI 2019 and two 2018-2021 catheter-synthesis papers). Two data facts also change the design: CVC is present in about 98% of RANZCR images, so the 'no labelled line' stratum and CVC pairs are near-empty, and radiologists themselves agree on CVC presence only at kappa 0.47, so non-expert CVC verification should be dropped. With the firsts re-cut, the direct precedents added as a related-work block, the retest and paraphrase rows moved into the core (published instability exceeds the hypothesised effects), and TAIX-VQA adopted as the external anchor, the plan remains a publishable, defensible contribution; without these changes its novelty section would be rejected by any reviewer who has read the May-Sept 2026 CXR literature.
MUST CHANGE:
  - Cite Güzel, Özenbaş, Saravi (Diagnostics 2026;16(11):1602, PMC13257059) as the direct precedent for four-device yes/no on RANZCR; import their closed-model balanced accuracies as a Table 1 reference row; re-cut C2 to open 2-4B models, token-logged resolution, instructed abstention and a device-absent set of n >= 300 (theirs is n = 3).
  - Re-state C3 as the first encoder-matched (CheXOne vs its exact base Qwen2.5-VL-3B), label-clean device-existence test with instructed abstention; cite McLaughlin et al. 2026 (arXiv 2604.09841) as the matched-pair precedent and relabel the Gemma-3-4B/MedGemma row as a replication.
  - Fix the Arm 3 claim: EVADE (arXiv 2608.18833, Table 3) already has random-crop and center-crop ablations; keep only wrong-region/contralateral and oracle-polyline crops as new, and pre-register EVADE's null as the prior.
  - Delete 'no CXR benchmark ships reliability numbers'; adopt PSF-Med's flip-rate protocol (arXiv 2602.21428) and the consistency x image-reliance quadrants (arXiv 2603.20985); move a 200-item retest and the P0b paraphrase row into the core for every run.
  - Add TAIX-VQA (HF jomoll/TAIX-VQA; Moll et al. ML4H 2025, arXiv 2510.11196) as the device-specific external anchor and cite it; rewrite the 'devices nearly absent' gap to name exactly which benchmarks lack device classes (HalluCXR, MedHEval, CheXpercept, Lotfinia, verified) and which have them (TAIX-VQA, Güzel).
  - Cite Xiong et al. 2026 (arXiv 2605.20158) for RadEdit removal with a background-edit control on 3-4B models and RadCF (arXiv 2609.24879, code) for device removal; add RadCF as a fourth remover candidate with an NIH-14 contamination flag; narrow C5 to the validity gate and the LaMa/SD-1.5 numbers.
  - Cite Frid-Adar et al. MICCAI 2019 (arXiv 1908.07170), Applied Sciences 2021 (doi 10.3390/app11041638) and Yi et al. 2018 (arXiv 1806.00921) as the synthetic tube-insertion prior art; claim only the gated evaluation use.
  - Compute the realised CVC-absent and no-labelled-line counts on Day 1 (expect about 2% of RANZCR); redefine H1's co-occurrence contrast around ETT-absent/CVC-present vs ETT-absent/NGT-present; exclude CVC from human verification and from the kappa audit (radiologist kappa for CVC presence is 0.47 in Güzel).
