  - Drop the RANZCR-to-NIH mapping from the contribution list (the 1st-place Kaggle solution already hashed RANZCR images against NIH); keep the yield table and NIH-filename item list as the artefacts.
  - Rewrite the GAPS list, why_unique and the submission positioning sentence after adding a 'direct precedents' block (Güzel, TAIX-VQA, McLaughlin, Xiong, PSF-Med, RadCF, Byun 2026, Ask4VG, Theocharidou 2026, VILA-M3, Intent-RAG, Prevalence calibration, BCEA, CAST) with the exact delta against each.
ISSUES:
  [major] step 1: The plan treats device-existence numbers for VLMs on RANZCR as unpublished ('no published numbers on device existence', 'devices nearly absent'). A May 2026 journal paper already ran the same four devices (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz) as yes/no presence on 4,813 RANZCR CLiP radiographs with balanced accuracy, a hallucination readout on device-absent cases, prompt-variant and repeat-inference instability, a 377-case blinded radiologist reader study and PadChest external validation. The floor pilot and the four-question item design are a re-run of that protocol with smaller open models.
      evidence: Güzel, Özenbaş, Saravi, 'Performance of Multimodal Large Language Models in Detection and Position Assessment of Thoracic Devices on Chest Radiographs', Diagnostics 2026;16(11):1602, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13257059/ (doi 10.3390/diagnostics16111602). GPT-4o, Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite, Claude Sonnet 4.6; forced-binary JSON ('Analyze the provided chest X-ray and classify the presence and position of the following devices... Return ONLY valid JSON'), no 'uncertain' option, native JPEGs unresized; best balanced accuracy for presence: ETT 0.900, NGT 0.712, CVC 0.467, Swan-Ganz 0.538; 'hallucination' = any device predicted on gold device-absent cases, but only n = 3 such cases existed (CVC prevalence 97.8%); prompt variants swung GPT-4o ETT sensitivity 0.32 to 0.80 and NGT 0.24 to 0.85; repeat-inference kappa 0.15 to 0.92 (GPT-4o ETT 0.30, NGT 0.20); failures concentrate in multi-device cases (p < 0.0001); readers beat LLMs 42/42; no open-source or small models, no mention of the NIH origin or contamination.
      fix: Cite it as the direct precedent in Section 1 and the related-work table. Re-cut C2 to 'first open-weight 2-4B, resolution-controlled (token-logged) device-class existence numbers with instructed abstention and a device-absent set of n >= 300 instead of n = 3'. Import their closed-model balanced accuracies as a reference row in Table 1. Because their repeat kappa and prompt swings are larger than the plan's hypothesised 5-10 point effects, move the P0b paraphrase row and a 200-item retest from the Week-5 appendix into the core Table 2 for every run.
  [minor] step 1: The RANZCR-to-NIH identification by image hashing is presented as new ('a new RANZCR-to-NIH mapping ... which no paper or solution repository publishes'). Competition participants already de-duplicated the 30k RANZCR images inside NIH ChestX-ray14 by perceptual hashing and linked patient IDs to build folds.
      evidence: RANZCR CLiP 1st-place write-up 'All data are ext', https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification/writeups/all-data-are-ext-1st-place-solution (discussion 226633): used imagehash to de-duplicate the 30k RANZCR train images already present in NIH and linked patient IDs so the same patients fell in the same fold. The page is JavaScript-rendered and could not be fetched in full; the search index snippet carries the statement, so verify on Day 0.
      fix: Drop the mapping from the contribution list; cite the write-up and any released code as the precedent; keep the realised per-class pair/stratum yield table and the released NIH-filename item list as the artefacts, which remain unpublished.
  [major] step 2: A public, credential-free ICU device-existence VQA set with the exact question form ('Is an endotracheal tube present in this image? (yes or no)') already exists, has pacemaker and chest-tube questions the plan reserves, includes target-region occlusion perturbations, and has been run on MedGemma-4b-it. The plan neither cites it nor uses it, and its 'devices are nearly absent from hallucination benchmarks' gap statement is wrong as written.
      evidence: Moll et al., 'Evaluating Reasoning Faithfulness in Medical Vision-Language Models using Multimodal Perturbations', ML4H 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11196; dataset https://huggingface.co/datasets/jomoll/TAIX-VQA (634 ICU radiographs, 32,000 QA pairs, device templates for endotracheal tube, gastric tube, central venous catheter, chest tube, cardiac pacemaker, plus position variants); perturbations include occlusion (VO-BB) and bias cues; models Gemini 2.5 Pro/Flash/Flash-Lite, LlamaV-o1, MedGemma-4b-it, HealthGPT-M3; code github.com/jomoll/cot-eval. Source images come from TAIX-Ray (Nature Sci Data 2026, https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-026-07271-7; HF TLAIM/TAIX-Ray, CC BY 4.0, 215,381 bedside CXRs from 47,724 ICU patients, multiple studies per patient, image widths 382-512 px); the public structured report covers eight findings and has no device fields.
      fix: Cite Moll et al. and TAIX-VQA in Sections 1-2 and the related-work table; use TAIX-VQA as the external anchor instead of (or beside) SLAKE, since it is device-specific; state explicitly that TAIX-Ray's public labels carry no device fields and its images are 382-512 px, which is why RANZCR radiologist labels at 1024 px are still required; rewrite the gap as 'device classes are absent from HalluCXR, MedHEval, CheXpercept and Lotfinia et al. (verified) and appear only in TAIX-VQA (634 images, 4B+) and Güzel et al. (closed models)'.
  [major] step 2: Two design claims do not survive the RANZCR facts now on record: (a) 'four questions per image giving within-image adversarial negatives' is exactly Güzel's per-image four-device JSON; (b) the S3 'no labelled line' stratum and any CVC-absent cell are near-empty because CVC is present in about 98% of RANZCR images (the set was pre-selected by a catheter detector), so H1's 'FAR_B with vs without other labelled lines' contrast and the CVC pairs are underpowered by construction; (c) non-expert verification of CVC absence is unsafe: two board-certified radiologists reached only kappa 0.471 on CVC presence.
      evidence: Güzel et al. 2026 (PMC13257059): CVC prevalence 97.8% in a 4,813-image random RANZCR sample; specificity for CVC 0.085-0.283 on RANZCR vs 0.635-0.702 on PadChest at 49% prevalence; inter-reader kappa for CVC presence 0.471 vs ETT 0.878 and NGT 0.818; failures concentrated in multi-device cases. RANZCR descriptor (Tang et al., Sci Data 2021, https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-021-01066-8): radiographs were selected by an algorithm developed on MIMIC-CXR to contain catheters; CVC labels total 32,981 over 30,083 images.
      fix: Drop 'four questions per image' as a first and cite Güzel for the multi-device failure prior. Redefine the co-occurrence contrast as ETT-absent/CVC-present vs ETT-absent/NGT-present vs the rare no-line cell reported descriptively; compute the realised CVC-absent count on Day 1 before promising 100 CVC pairs (expect about 600 CVC-absent images in total). Exclude CVC from every human-verified claim and from the 200-item kappa audit; keep it as a label-only floor class. Record the radiologist kappa values as the ceiling for the student-plus-peer audit.
  [major] step 3: The plan states RadEdit removal has 'one precedent' that failed and that device-removal editors have no code. A May 2026 paper already uses RadEdit removal at scale on three CXR datasets with a background-edit control (the plan's E-irr) on Qwen2.5-VL-3B and MedGemma-4B/1.5-4B, and a Sept 2026 paper releases a device-removal counterfactual model with code. Reader detectability of AI-edited CXRs has also been measured in 2026. The 'measured validity numbers ... which no CXR editing paper reports' claim must be narrowed.
      evidence: (1) Xiong, Jin, Sinha, Lu, Zhang, 'Rethinking Visual Attribution for Chest X-ray Reasoning in Large Vision Language Models', https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20158 (May 2026): RadEdit inpainting to remove annotated attributes on Chest ImaGenome, VinDr-CXR and PadChest-GR; samples kept only if the answer flips after foreground editing and stays stable after background editing; models Qwen2.5-VL 3B/7B, Gemma3 4B/12B, MedGemma 4B and 1.5-4B; prompt 'Answer directly with yes or no without any explanation'; code github.com/gzxiong/medfocus; no reader or 2AFC validation. (2) RadCF, Xing et al. (GSK), 'Generating Chest X-Ray Counterfactuals by Specialising Foundation Image Models', https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24879 (Sept 2026), code GSK-AI/RadCF: device-removal counterfactuals do(d) on BRAX with target accuracy 0.729, NIH-14 as primary target domain, validated by pseudo-oracle classifiers, LPIPS and KID only. (3) 'Detectability and healthcare implications of generative AI-synthesized chest radiographs: a blinded radiologist reader study', 2026, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42528564/ (PMC13414275): 320 real frontal CXRs, four radiologists, image-conditioned edits detected 34.0% vs 56.1% for text-only generation. (4) RadEdit itself (https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.12865) added pacemakers and preserved chest drains via keep masks, validated by a BioViL-T score threshold; trained on MIMIC-CXR, ChestX-ray8 and CheXpert.
      fix: Cite all four. Add RadCF as a fourth remover candidate in the 40-image pilot (flag NIH-14 training as a contamination risk for the edited arm). Credit Xiong et al. as the precedent for the background-edit control and for RadEdit-removal-based VLM evaluation at 3-4B. Narrow C5 to 'first device-removal validity reported with two independent verifiers, a protected-region error, a 2AFC and a two-reader rubric with untouched controls, and the first LaMa/SD-1.5 inpainting numbers on radiographs'.
  [major] step 4: Pasting or synthesising tubes into real chest radiographs is 2018-2021 prior art that the step's nearest-prior list omits; the insertion method itself carries no novelty.
      evidence: Frid-Adar, Amer, Greenspan, 'Endotracheal Tube Detection and Segmentation in Chest Radiographs using Synthetic Data', MICCAI 2019, https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.07170 (synthetic ET tubes rendered into real X-rays, trained first on synthetic then fine-tuned on real, tested on 477 real public radiographs, AUC 0.99); 'Synthesize and Segment: Towards Improved Catheter Segmentation via Adversarial Augmentation', Applied Sciences 2021, https://doi.org/10.3390/app11041638; Yi et al., 'Automatic catheter detection in pediatric X-ray images using a scale-recurrent network and synthetic data', https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.00921 (B-spline catheters with projection profiles); RadEdit (ECCV 2024) pacemaker addition on MIMIC-Seg; the anatomy-guided shape insertion paper the plan already cites (arXiv 2501.12022).
      fix: Cite the 2018-2021 line; reuse Frid-Adar's rendering recipe (intensity profile along a spline) rather than inventing a blend; claim only the use of insertion as a gated evaluation stimulus (mirror verifier gate, real-vs-inserted 2AFC, DREAM audit) and the real-minus-inserted hit-rate gap.
  [major] step 5: C3 ('the first controlled, image-grounded, matched-pair test of the advisor's general-vs-medical claim at or below 4B') is refuted: an April 2026 paper ran Gemma-3-4B vs MedGemma-4B and LLaVA-1.6 vs LLaVA-Med on binary presence tasks including CheXpert chest X-rays, with six prompt variants and refusals scored, and concluded that medical fine-tuning gives no consistent benefit.
      evidence: McLaughlin, Shubin, Eickhoff, Singh, Rudman, Golovanevsky, 'Is There Knowledge Left to Extract? Evidence of Fragility in Medically Fine-Tuned Vision-Language Models', https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09841 (Apr 2026): 19,200 responses; CheXpert pneumonia with one-word constraint: Gemma-3-4B 45% accuracy / 54% F1all vs MedGemma-4B 70% / 59%; LLaVA-1.6 56% / 35% vs LLaVA-Med 50% / 44%; refusal 84% (Gemma) vs 1% (MedGemma) on 'Pneumonia present?'; F1 with and without refusals; 'Medical fine-tuning only provides gains on tasks requiring limited clinical knowledge, yet produces random performance on challenging diagnostic tasks'; minor wording changes caused 20-point swings.
      fix: Re-state C3 as 'the first encoder-matched pair (CheXOne vs its exact base Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct; same vision tower, LLM and chat format) on device-existence items no model trained on, scored with instructed abstention'. Cite McLaughlin as the matched-pair precedent in the H2 preamble, relabel the Gemma-3-4B/MedGemma appendix row as a device-level replication of McLaughlin, and state the two-sided prediction using their refusal asymmetry (base models refuse far more) as the prior for the abstention contrast.
  [minor] step 5: Three of the step's probes are already in print at 2B or in general LVLMs and are not cited: blank-image and mismatched-image probing of 'prior-driven answers' at 2B, penalty-style prompting at 2B on medical VQA, and the verbal-confidence-versus-internal-state disconnect that the 'representation-action gap' reading relies on.
      evidence: Ask4VG, https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01044 (May 2026): Qwen2-VL-2B on VQA-RAD with original, perturbed, blank and mismatched images (the plan's BLIND-grey and UAR rows); Byun, Park, Corbeil, Ben Abacha, 'Overconfidence and Calibration in Medical VQA', https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02543 (Sept 2026): Qwen3-VL-2B 0.562 and InternVL3-2B 0.554 closed-ended accuracy on VQA-RAD/SLAKE/VQA-Med, a 'Punish' penalty prompt variant (Ni et al. 2024, not Kalai) that did not improve calibration, HAC with VASE perturbations, yes/no logit margin; 'The Mirage of Calibrated Confidence', EMNLP 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18453 (verbalized confidence is trajectory-independent); 'Honesty over Accuracy: Reinforced Hesitation', https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11500 (text-only, +1/0/-lambda rewards incl. MedQA; frontier models almost never abstain under stated penalties).
      fix: Cite all four; present P2 as the first verbatim Kalai confidence-target instruction on image-grounded CXR items; pre-register H3's expected null from Byun and from the zero-abstention text result; frame the logit-vs-verbal gap as a device-level CXR instance of the Mirage-of-Calibrated-Confidence finding.
  [minor] step 6: Two medical precedents for expert-signal injection and for 'the gain is a bias shift' are missing; the latter changes how a matched constant-bias control should be interpreted.
      evidence: VILA-M3, CVPR 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12915: TorchXRayVision expert probabilities fed back into the VLM prompt, expert-feedback vs none on ChestX-ray14 (F1 51.3 vs GPT-4o 33.1) and CheXpert, no shuffled-expert control. Kina and Petersen, 'Prevalence calibration as shortcut mitigation', MLMI 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07922: post-hoc group-wise recalibration (a per-group bias) raises misaligned-group AUROC from 0.23 to 0.73 on chest-drain/pneumothorax, i.e., a constant-bias correction can be a legitimate fix, not only a confound. CCD (https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23379) VQA is scored by text-match recall/F1 on Medical-CXR-VQA, so a cached yes/no logit fusion with detector-alone and shuffled-expert rows remains unreported (confirmed).
      fix: Cite VILA-M3 as the text-injection ancestor and Prevalence calibration in the interpretation of the constant-bias curve: a method on the curve is 'equivalent to prevalence recalibration', which is reportable as a positive finding about cheap calibration rather than only a negative about the detector.
  [minor] step 7: The retrieval controls are partially anticipated in 2026 medical RAG work (corrupted-retrieval robustness, answer-leakage rate, nearest-label copying failure, inconsistent retrieval predictions), and a reviewer has already named the label-shuffle control as the missing experiment in medical RAG; none is cited.
      evidence: Li, Ye, Xia, 'Efficient and Faithful Retrieval-Augmented Medical VQA via Intention-Guided On-Policy Self-Distillation', MICCAI 2026 MLMI, https://papers.miccai.org/miccai-2026-sat/MLMI_080.html (IU-Xray, MIMIC-CXR; introduces 'Retrieval Answer Leakage Rate' and 'Retrieval Robustness under corrupted retrieval'); 'Option-Aware Retrieval and Task-Specific VLM Adaptation for Medical VQA', https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15530 (quantifies nearest-label copying failure, 20.0% to 57.5%); Mazor and Hope, https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17394 ('inconsistent retrieval predictions'); review of 'Retrieval-Augmented VLMs for Multimodal Melanoma Diagnosis', https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08338, flags the missing label-shuffle/removal control.
      fix: Cite them; define copy rate and mismatched retrieval relative to the leakage-rate and corrupted-retrieval definitions; claim 'first on existence questions with frozen 2-4B models, with kNN-vote-alone, label-shuffled and mismatched rows together'.
  [major] step 8: 'No 2025-26 CXR hallucination benchmark ships reliability numbers' is false, and the reliability block (paraphrase flips, text-only agreement, retest) replicates two published CXR designs that already include MedGemma-1.5-4B and CheXOne; the plan also underestimates measured instability, which is larger than its hypothesised effects.
      evidence: PSF-Med, Sadanandan and Behzadan, https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21428 (Apr 2026): 26,850 CXR questions (80.6% presence), 92,856 paraphrases on MIMIC-CXR, PadChest, VinDr-CXR; nine VLMs incl. MedGemma 4B/27B/1.5-4B, LLaVA-Rad, CheXOne, RadFM; flip rates 3-37%; negation-adjacent paraphrases 25-35%; text-only baselines (MedGemma-27B 85% text-only agreement); benchmark released. 'Consistent but Dangerous', https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20985 (Mar 2026): consistency x image-reliance quadrants on MedGemma-4B-IT and LLaVA-Rad with an NGT example; r = -0.89 between flip rate and 'Dangerous' fraction. Güzel 2026: repeat kappa 0.15-0.92, prompt swings 2.5-3.5x; McLaughlin 2026: 20-point swings.
      fix: Delete the claim; cite both papers and adopt PSF-Med's flip-rate definition and the four-quadrant classification for the device items; move a 200-item retest and the P0b paraphrase into the core for every run and report them beside every H1-H4 effect; present the block as 'device-level numbers under the PSF-Med protocol', not as a first.
  [major] step 9: Arm 3's novelty statement ('none has a random-crop or wrong-region control'; 'ARCD and EVADE lack the random-region control') is false for EVADE, whose ablation table already contains center-crop and random-crop variants and reports that they barely move the metrics; a conformal 'answer/abstain/acquire evidence by zoom or crop' method on object existence and an occlusion-selected-region decoder also exist.
      evidence: EVADE, https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18833 (Aug 2026), Table 3 on Qwen2.5-VL-7B: full EVADE AURC 0.202 (VQA-RAD), 0.216 (SLAKE), 0.242 (PathVQA) vs random crop 0.199, 0.207, 0.244 and center crop 0.198, 0.205, 0.247; text: substituting the self-proposed region for center or random crops 'barely moves the downstream metrics'. 'Look Again Before You Abstain: Budgeted Conformal Evidence Acquisition', https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16667 (June 2026): three-way answer/abstain/acquire by zooming or cropping on a balanced object-existence benchmark with conformal guarantees. CAST, MICCAI 2026, https://papers.miccai.org/miccai-2026/0209-Paper4832.html: MedSAM3 candidate masks, region chosen by occlusion log-likelihood drop, baselines VCD/ARCD/DoLA/OPERA, Phi-3.5V-Med on SLAKE and MIMIC-CXR.
      fix: Correct the claim: keep only the wrong-region/contralateral crop and the oracle RANZCR-polyline crop as new controls; cite EVADE's random-crop result as the pre-registered prior (region specificity adds little) and BCEA/CAST as nearest prior; if Arm 3 cannot beat the wrong-region crop it is reported as confirming EVADE on CXR devices.
  [minor] step 9: The hidden-state extension and the blind-image idea have 2026 precedents in medical and general LVLMs that are not cited.
      evidence: BICR, 'Grounded or Guessing? LVLM Confidence Estimation via Blind-Image Contrastive Ranking', https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10893 (hidden states with the image blacked out vs present, probe trained with ranking loss, evaluated on medical imaging among other domains); MedProb, https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04336 (probing internal representations for medical QA).
      fix: Cite both as nearest prior; keep the LLM-Check Hidden Score only as the training-free variant and state that BICR is the trained upper bound not attempted.
  [minor] step 2: MedGemma 1.5 is cast as the positive control, but a 2026 ICU study found it near floor on CVC assessment with dominant-response behaviour; the plan should not assume MedGemma passes GO on CVC or that closed models would.
      evidence: Theocharidou, Tsinaris et al., 'Diagnostic Performance of Multimodal Large Language Models for Central Venous Catheter Assessment on Chest Radiographs in the ICU', Medical Sciences (Basel) 2026, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13302893/: 183 portable AP radiographs at 2828x2320 px, all CVC-positive; MedGemma 1.5 access-site accuracy 0.339 (Gemini 3 Flash 0.874); all models classified 97.8-100% of tips as appropriate; no 'uncertain' option; no device-absent images.
      fix: Cite in the CVC paragraph; pre-register that MedGemma-1.5-4B may fail GO on CVC and that the medgemma-4b-it fallback is then the positive control; note that no published device study includes a sizeable device-absent set, which is the plan's opening.
  [major] step 0: The 'GAPS (verified absent)' list and the positioning set contain at least four statements that are now false or misleading, and omit roughly a dozen 2025-2026 works that sit directly on the plan's claims; a reviewer who knows any of them will read the 'first' sentences as careless.
      evidence: False or misleading: 'devices are nearly absent from hallucination benchmarks' (TAIX-VQA device yes/no incl. pacemaker and chest tube, ML4H 2025; Güzel 2026 on RANZCR); 'no random-region controls in ARCD/EVADE' (EVADE Table 3); no CXR benchmark ships reliability numbers (PSF-Med; Consistent but Dangerous); 'the one precedent' for RadEdit removal (Xiong 2026 at scale; RadCF with code). Missing works: Güzel 2026 (PMC13257059), Moll/TAIX-VQA (2510.11196), McLaughlin 2026 (2604.09841), Xiong 2026 (2605.20158), RadCF (2609.24879), PSF-Med (2602.21428), Consistent but Dangerous (2603.20985), Byun 2026 (2604.02543), Ask4VG (2606.01044), Theocharidou 2026 (PMC13302893), Frid-Adar 2019 (1908.07170), VILA-M3 (2411.12915), Intent-RAG (MICCAI 2026 MLMI_080), Prevalence calibration (2609.07922), the 2026 blinded detectability reader study (PubMed 42528564), BCEA (2606.16667), CAST (MICCAI 2026). Verified absent and still supporting the plan: Lotfinia et al. (2606.17710) has eight findings, no support-device block, no 2-3B model, 224 px with a 512 probe; HalluCXR (2605.20469) has 12 CheXpert findings, 6-7B+ models, no abstention; CheXTemporal (2605.11304) explicitly excludes support devices; MI-CXR (2605.15574) and LoMeVQA (2607.27806) carry no device events; the CheXOne paper (2604.00493) reports neither its Qwen2.5-VL-3B base nor any device, hallucination or abstention evaluation.
      fix: Rewrite the GAPS list with the verified-absent items only; add a 'direct precedents' row block to the related-work table (Güzel, TAIX-VQA, McLaughlin, Xiong, PSF-Med) with the exact delta of this work against each; re-cut every 'first' in why_unique accordingly; record the Lotfinia Day-0 check as already done (no support-devices block; prompt 'Is [display] present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.').
  [minor] step 10: The positioning sentence for submissions names only HalluCXR, MedVH, HEAL-MedVQA and Lotfinia; after the corrections above it must position against the device-specific and matched-pair precedents or it will be read as unaware of them.
      evidence: Güzel 2026 (same four devices on RANZCR), TAIX-VQA (device yes/no on ICU images with occlusion), McLaughlin 2026 (matched 4B pairs with refusals), PSF-Med (CXR reliability with CheXOne and MedGemma-1.5-4B), Xiong 2026 (RadEdit removal with background control on 3-4B).
      fix: Rewrite the sentence: 'Unlike Güzel et al. (closed models, forced binary, three device-absent cases) and TAIX-VQA (634 low-resolution ICU images, no abstention), we evaluate open 2-4B models on radiologist-labelled device existence at 1024 px with same-patient device-present/absent pairs, instructed penalised abstention, an encoder-matched base comparison (cf. McLaughlin et al.), reliability rows under the PSF-Med protocol, and mitigation under yes-bias controls.'
STRENGTHS:
  - Edit-free same-patient device-present/absent pairs as the negative arm of a hallucination evaluation have no precedent: CheXTemporal (arXiv 2605.11304) explicitly excludes support devices as 'not temporally meaningful'; MI-CXR (arXiv 2605.15574), LoMeVQA (arXiv 2607.27806) and MS-CXR-T carry no device events; RoentMod uses real follow-ups only as a generator comparator.
  - Instructed, penalty-stated abstention scored with Effective Reliability on chest-radiograph device items is unreported: Güzel 2026 forced binary JSON with no uncertainty option; HalluCXR has no abstention; TAIX-VQA is yes/no only; Byun 2026's 'Punish' prompt offers no abstain option; 'Honesty over Accuracy' (arXiv 2511.11500) is text-only.
  - Detector-fusion with detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias and OLM controls at matched yes-ratio is not in CCD (its baselines are VCD/OPERA/ICD/DeCo/Attn-Lens and stage ablations; VQA scored by text-match F1), VILA-M3 (expert vs none only) or any medical paper found; Mirage and Rethinking-CD controls remain unrun on medical images or below 7B.
  - A validity gate for device removal and insertion combining two independent verifiers, a protected-region error, a Sogancioglu-style 2AFC and a two-reader rubric with untouched controls is unreported: RadCF and Xiong 2026 validate by classifier flip or flip-filtering only; MedDiME by classifier flip; the 2026 blinded detectability study covers whole-image generative edits, not device removal; no LaMa or SD-1.5 inpainting numbers on radiographs exist.
  - Validation of the CCD/MedSigLIP device prompt pairs and CheXzero's 'support devices' template against RANZCR radiologist labels is absent from the literature (the MedGemma technical report evaluates MedSigLIP on CheXpert findings; 'Beyond Benchmark Scores' audits MedSigLIP on TB only).
  - Open 2026 2B generalists (Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B) have no published device-class existence numbers: Byun 2026 reports Qwen3-VL-2B/InternVL3-2B only on VQA-RAD/SLAKE/VQA-Med; TAIX-VQA reports MedGemma-4b-it, HealthGPT-M3 and LlamaV-o1 in aggregate; Güzel reports closed models; Lotfinia's smallest model is MedGemma-1.5-4B at 224 px.
  - The Lotfinia Day-0 risk is resolved in the plan's favour: the paper evaluates eight findings only, no support-device question, no 2-3B model, 224 px with a 512 probe, prompt 'Is [display] present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.'; the device-level blind, swap and occlusion rows are therefore new relative to the direct precedent.
  - The CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B pair is genuinely unreported: the CheXOne paper (arXiv 2604.00493) reports neither its base model as a baseline nor any device, hallucination, abstention or calibration evaluation, and CheXinstruct-v2 lists ChestXray14 and Object-CXR but not RANZCR labels, so the comparison is label-clean as the plan claims.

=== feasibility === verdict: The data and compute path is sound and cheap: every dataset on the critical path is credential-free, the mapping fits one Kaggle CPU session, all seven checkpoints fit a 24 GB bf16 pod individually, the 1060 workloads (MedSigLIP, CheXzero, BiomedCLIP, LaMa) are fp32-safe, the lmms-eval wrappers and the CCD/MedSigLIP device prompts exist, and the realistic cloud spend is $20-35 against the $40 ceiling. The schedule is not sound: the pre-Nov-8 workload is about 170-260 h against 70-95 h of student time, roughly twice the plan's own 95-110 h estimate, so 'report v1 complete by Nov 8' is unattainable as specified. Four concrete arithmetic errors compound it: the 50 GB network volume cannot hold ~52 GB of bf16 weights plus venvs (and network volumes are Secure-Cloud-only, so the plan's community-pod wording is impossible); the pod throughput is ~2x optimistic (30-50 A5000-hours for the core, not 20-28); verification is 1,300 images at 20-30 s, not 1,000 at 10-15 s; and the Week-1 and Week-2 workstreams are each 2-3x their allotted hours. The mapping also has an undetected failure mode (sibling-film mis-assignment within patient) that the planned random spot check cannot see. With the minimal core defined now (four runs, ETT primary with CVC through the floor table, P0/P1/P2-hi, CPU Arm 1 with controls, minimal Arm 2, LaMa-only removal probe, HF runner only, clustered bootstrap statistics) v1 on Nov 8 is feasible at ~90-100 h, and everything else moves cleanly into v2 and the buffer without changing the design.
MUST CHANGE:
  - Register the Nov 8 scope as the minimal core now: four runs (Qwen3-VL-2B, MedGemma-1.5-4B, CheXOne, Qwen2.5-VL-3B), ETT primary with CVC only via the floor table, P0/P1/P2-hi plus BLIND-text/grey, Arm 1 on all four runs with detector-alone and shuffled-expert, Arm 2 as relevant k=3 / kNN-vote / mismatched on the hero and MedGemma, LaMa-plus-E-irr removal probe with automatic gate and 2AFC, Wilson plus patient-clustered bootstrap plus McNemar; move InternVL3.5, Gemma 4 E2B, NGT, SD-1.5, RadEdit, the insertion set, lmms-eval task, SLAKE anchor, mixed-effects, Figure 1b, PN/P0b/P2-lo and the reader rubric to v2/buffer.
  - Fix the pod plan on Day 0: either Secure Cloud A5000 at $0.27/h with a 100 GB network volume (~$14 for two months) or Community Cloud 3090 at $0.22/h with weights on the container disk and JSONL synced every session; correct the price text (4090 is $0.74 secure / $0.34 community; the brief's community prices still hold; 50 GB storage is $7 over two months).
  - Add a margin rule to the mapping (best pHash distance <=2 and >=4 bits ahead of the second-best, 128-px cross-correlation tie-break), report the ambiguous within-patient fraction, and treat label-image disagreements on A/B as mapping errors first.
  - Re-budget verification: primary-class absence on B and U only (~1,000 images at ~20 s), 25% of A, non-primary questions labelled 'RANZCR label only' unless verified inside the 200-item audit; book the peer for 5-6 h and use non-authors as the 2AFC raters.
  - Start the P0 core for the four runs in Week 2 as soon as Dataset v0 exists; batch the four questions per image after the identical-output check; run two models concurrently on the 24 GB card; measure s/item on Day 3 and rebuild the hour budget from it.
  - Rewrite Week 1 to two venvs, Kaggle T4 smoke tests for the 2B models, a pilot at one budget per model with the three-budget sweep on the hero only, and a minimal OSF registration (decision rule, H1-H6 primary endpoints, N rule) by Oct 17 with PROTOCOL.md completed in the break week; use max_num in {1,4,9} for InternVL if it is kept.
ISSUES:
  [fatal] step 0: Person-time before Nov 8 is roughly double the plan's own count and about 2.5x the student's capacity, so 'report v1 complete by Nov 8' as specified cannot happen; the plan's 'apply the cut list from the first slipped day' defers a decision that must be made now.
      evidence: Plan admits 95-110 h against 80-100 h available. Re-count per step at realistic unit times for a student doing each pipeline for the first time: Week 1 (accounts/terms 3 h; mapping notebook with pHash over 112k PNGs, margin rule, spot check 8-12 h; yield table and pilot lists 3-4 h; five pod venvs incl. CheXOne-from-main and Gemma 4 plus six smoke tests 8-16 h; floor pilot runs, two classifier references, analysis 6-8 h; PROTOCOL.md v1.0 plus OSF H0-H8/branches/ladder/gates/kill dates 6-10 h; 1060 venv and LaMa smoke 3-5 h; citation log 3-4 h; NCBE course 2-4 h; IRB letter 1-2 h) = 43-69 h vs the plan's 25-30 h in a class week with 15-20 h. Week 2 (pairs/U/items 5-8; reference sheet 2; verification of 1,300 images 7-11; rater logistics/adjudication 2-3; HF runner with cached logits for five families plus BLIND rows 8-12; lmms-eval task plus 99% agreement on six runs 6-10; SLAKE 2; detector AUC on >=2,000 pool images per class 4-6; retrieval index 2-3; pilot discordance 1-2; removal pilot with three removers, E-irr, protected-region MAE, verifier gate, 2AFC form 10-16; insertion set 8-12) = 60-90 h vs ~25-35 h available even with the Oct 20-22 break. Week 3 (statistics code with clustered bootstrap, mixed-effects, Phi_c, McNemar 8-12; Table 2/Figure 1 4-6; probe gates and 2AFC 3-4; Sections 1-3 10-15; memo 2-3; pod babysitting 4) = 30-45 h vs 15-20. Week 4 (Arm 1 6-10; Arm 2 6-10; synthetic rows 3-4; Sections 4-8, tables, Appendix A-B 20-30) = 35-55 h vs 15-20. Total 170-260 h vs 70-95 h.
      fix: Define the Nov 8 deliverable now as the minimal core (~90-100 h): four runs (Qwen3-VL-2B hero, MedGemma-1.5-4B, CheXOne, Qwen2.5-VL-3B; keeps H2 and the general-vs-medical row), ETT primary with CVC only through the floor table (NGT to v2), 200-300 pairs plus 200 U, P0/P1/P2-hi plus BLIND-text/grey, Arm 1 (CPU) on all four runs with detector-alone and shuffled-expert, Arm 2 only as relevant k=3 / kNN-vote / mismatched on the hero and MedGemma, removal probe LaMa plus E-irr on <=40 images with the automatic gate and 2AFC, statistics = Wilson plus patient-clustered bootstrap plus exact McNemar. Move InternVL3.5, Gemma 4 E2B, NGT pairs, SD-1.5 and RadEdit, the insertion set, the lmms-eval task and SLAKE anchor, mixed-effects and bayes_evals, Figure 1b, PN/P0b/P2-lo and the second-rater rubric to v2 (Nov 22) and the buffer. Write this cut into PROTOCOL.md and OSF as the registered v1 scope, not as a contingency.
  [major] step 1: The Week-1 schedule (mapping Tue, five venvs plus six-run smoke test plus 9,000-item floor pilot plus two classifier references Wed, decision and full OSF registration Thu, all by Oct 17) is not executable by a part-time student in a class week.
      evidence: The plan's own Step 1 method lists eleven distinct Day-1 workstreams; CheXOne's card requires transformers built from source, Gemma 4's card says only 'latest Transformers', InternVL3.5 needs trust_remote_code on whichever version the others pin, and lmms-eval must be installed per venv at a pinned commit; the pilot alone is 600 images x three budgets x four to five runs plus MedGemma at ~0.7-1.2 s per item (1.5-3 h pod) before any analysis; PROTOCOL.md v1.0 is effectively the plan's 1,500-word protocol block plus branches and gates. Week 1 has no break and 15-20 h.
      fix: Two venvs, not five: one on the newest transformers release for Qwen3-VL, MedGemma (Gemma 3 class), Qwen2.5-VL and CheXOne (its 'build from source' note dates from Jan-Apr 2026; try the release first), a second only if CheXOne or InternVL3.5 breaks; smoke-test the 2B generalists on Kaggle T4 (free) while the pod is set up once; run the pilot at one budget per model (its maximum) with the three-budget sweep on the hero only (~4,000 items); register on OSF by Oct 17 only the class decision rule, H1-H6 primary endpoints and the N rule (2-3 h) and finish PROTOCOL.md in the break week; push the citation log and NCBE course to Oct 20-22.
  [major] step 1: The pod storage and pricing plan is internally inconsistent: a 50 GB network volume cannot hold the panel, and RunPod network volumes exist only on Secure Cloud, so 'community pod with a network volume' is impossible; the plan's 'corrected' prices mix clouds.
      evidence: RunPod docs (fetched today): 'Network volumes are only available for Pods in the Secure Cloud.' Pricing page dated Sep 27, 2026: RTX A5000 $0.16 community / $0.27 secure, RTX 3090 $0.22 / $0.50, RTX 4090 $0.34 / $0.74 (the plan's $0.89 matches nothing; the brief's $0.22-0.34 are the current community prices, so the plan's statement that they 'no longer hold' is false); network storage $0.07/GB/month under 1 TB, so 50 GB over two months is $7, not $3-4. bf16 weights alone: Gemma 4 E2B ~10 GB (5.1B total params) + medgemma-1.5-4b ~8.6 + medgemma-4b-it ~8.6 + CheXOne ~8.1 (4.07B) + Qwen2.5-VL-3B ~7.5 + Qwen3-VL-2B ~4.4 + InternVL3.5-2B ~4.7 = ~52 GB, plus five venvs with torch/CUDA (~5-6 GB each, ~8-10 GB with a shared uv cache), RadEdit stack ~2.5 GB, ~12k NIH PNGs ~4.5 GB and outputs: 70-90 GB.
      fix: Pick one of two configurations on Day 0 and write the price into the ledger: (a) Secure Cloud RTX A5000 at $0.27/h with a 100 GB network volume (~$14 for two months), HF_HOME on the volume, medgemma-4b-it deleted after the pilot, shared package cache across venvs; or (b) Community Cloud RTX 3090 at $0.22/h with no network volume, weights re-downloaded to the container disk each session (~5-10 min at RunPod bandwidth), and per-item JSONL pushed to GitHub/HF at the end of every session. Either lands at $20-35 total; (a) is safer against community interruptions during the 30-50 h core run.
  [major] step 1: The RANZCR-to-NIH mapping as specified (dimension key, then pHash at 256 px with a Hamming threshold, then a random 200-pair visual spot check) will silently mis-assign some JPEGs to a sibling film of the same patient, and those errors land exactly in the longitudinal A/B pairs.
      evidence: NIH OriginalImage[Width,Height] values cluster on a few detector formats (2500x2048 and a handful of others cover most images), so the dimension key leaves hundreds to thousands of candidates and pHash does the real matching; RANZCR averages 7.9 films per patient and NIH patients have up to 100+ follow-ups, and consecutive AP ICU films of the same patient are near-duplicates whose 64-bit pHash distances are often within any practical threshold; a visual spot check of 200 random pairs cannot distinguish a film from its sibling taken days later, so the planned check cannot detect this error mode; a sibling swap converts a radiologist-labelled 'ETT present' into an image where the ETT is absent (or vice versa), corrupting the pair arm and the verification kappa.
      fix: Make the match rule a margin rule: accept only if the best candidate's pHash distance is <=2 and the second-best is >=4 bits farther; break ties with 128-px normalised cross-correlation (or SSIM) against the resized JPEG; log and exclude ambiguous UIDs and report their count and their within-patient share; when the student's verification disagrees with the RANZCR label on an A or B image, treat it first as a mapping error (re-run the match for that patient) and only then as label noise. Cost: about one extra hour of Kaggle CPU and 2 h of code.
  [major] step 2: Non-expert verification is under-counted by 2-3x, and the four-question design relies on unverified RANZCR labels for three of the four questions per image.
      evidence: The plan's targets give B = 300 + 150 + 100 = 550, U = 300 + 150 = 450, primary-class A = 300: 1,300 images, not 'about 1,000'; 10-15 s per image is unrealistic on ICU films where the hard cases are faint CVCs, tracheostomy-vs-ETT and tubes partly out of field (20-30 s each with flagging) = 7-11 h, not 3-4 h; Table 2 scores all four questions keyed to RANZCR columns, but only the primary class is verified, while the CLiP descriptor reports ~15% discrepancies on re-review of 5,100 consensus labels; the second rater's load (200-item audit 1-1.5 h, removal 2AFC ~1 h, insertion 2AFC ~0.5 h, rubric reading of 150-200 images x four criteria 2-3 h) is 5-6 h, not 2 h.
      fix: Verify only the primary-class absence on B and U (1,000 x ~20 s ~ 5.5 h) and a 25% sample of A; label the three non-primary questions 'RANZCR label only' in Table 2 (or verify all four classes only inside the 200-item audit sample so kappa covers them); book the peer for 5-6 h across Weeks 2-5 and use a second peer or the advisor's graduate student for the 2AFC blocks, since the student who made the edits is not blind to them.
  [major] step 5: Pod throughput is about 2x optimistic, so the core run is a 30-50 h unattended job on an A5000 rather than 20-28 h, and nothing in the plan starts it before Week 3.
      evidence: Item count: 1,550 images x 4 questions = 6,200 per prompt; six runs x 3 prompts = 111,600; hero and MedGemma x (P2-lo, PN, P0b) = 37,200; ~148,800 image items. FLOP check at batch 1: Qwen2.5-VL-3B/CheXOne at 1024x1024 give 36x36 = 1,296 visual tokens, prefill ~2 x 3.75B x 1,400 = 10.5 TFLOPs plus a ~0.67B ViT over ~5,200 14-px patches (~7 TFLOPs); at a realistic 30-35 TFLOPS on a 3090 that is ~0.5 s plus 50-100 ms PNG decode/resize plus generate overhead: 0.6-0.8 s per item, and an A5000 is roughly half a 3090: 1.0-1.3 s. Qwen3-VL-2B (1,024 tokens, 4,096 ViT patches) and InternVL3.5 (1,280 realised tokens) are similar; only MedGemma (256 tokens) is fast. Core: 30-50 h A5000 / 20-33 h 3090; Arm 2's ~29,000 items 6-10 h; total pod 45-75 A5000-hours = $12-20 secure, so dollars hold but Week 3's wall-clock does not if the run starts Oct 26 and tables are due Nov 1.
      fix: Start the P0 pass for the hero, MedGemma, CheXOne and Qwen2.5-VL-3B in Week 2 as soon as Dataset v0 exists (items later excluded by verification are dropped at scoring; nothing is inspected); batch the four questions per image (identical pixel_values, left-padded text) after the pre-registered identical-output check, which removes three of four vision-encoder passes; run two models concurrently on the 24 GB card (e.g., 4.4 + 8.6 GB weights); if still behind, drop P1 on the non-hero runs (-25k items). Measure s/item on Day 3 and rewrite the hour budget from the measurement.
  [major] step 2: The harness is built twice for v1: an HF runner per family plus a custom lmms-eval task plus a 99% agreement check on six runs plus the SLAKE anchor is 8-12 h of validation that no v1 number needs.
      evidence: lmms-eval main does ship gemma4.py, gemma3.py, qwen3_vl.py, qwen2_5_vl.py and internvl3_5.py in lmms_eval/models/simple (verified today) and no MedGemma or CheXOne entry, so the agreement check requires a local HF dataset, a copied pope.yaml, and six wrapper runs in matching venvs; the HF runner already caches logits with output_scores=True in the same generate call; the SLAKE anchor adds a seventh dataset and another run per model.
      fix: For v1 use the HF runner only, with a 100-item hand check of parsed answers per model and the parsing regex on the dev split; ship cxr_device_pope.yaml and the SLAKE anchor as v2 release artefacts (they are citable either way and change no headline number).
  [major] step 3: Both synthetic probes are scheduled into Week 2 next to the item set, verification and the runner; together they are 18-28 h of pipeline work on tools the plan itself flags as undocumented on CXR.
      evidence: Removal: rasterise and rescale 17,999-polyline masks by (1024/W, 1024/H), dilate per class, run LaMa (IOPaint is archived since Aug 13, 2025 and pins torch==2.1.2, so pip install into the torch-2.14 cu126 venv may fail or downgrade), SD-1.5 inpainting in fp32 on the 1060 (UNet 3.4 GB + VAE + text encoder ~4.3 GB plus 512-px attention ~1 GB = 5-5.5 GB, OOM-prone on a 6 GB card that also drives a display), RadEdit on the pod (512 px, 200 editing steps per the card), E-irr, the protected-region MAE, two verifier thresholds and a 2AFC form: 10-16 h. Insertion: decode CheXmask RLE for NIH ids from ChestX-Ray8.csv (2.0 GB, present only in the OriginalResolution folder; the Preprocessed folder has no ChestX-Ray8 file), derive a midline, extract and warp donor crops, histogram-match, feather, build 100 hosts and a mirror gate: 8-12 h.
      fix: Week 2 runs LaMa plus E-irr only (<=6 h) via simple-lama-inpainting or the advimman/lama repo (JIT model, torch-agnostic) or on Kaggle CPU, with the automatic gate and the 2AFC; SD-1.5 goes to Week 5 on Kaggle T4 in fp16 (SD-1.5's VAE is fp16-safe; the fp16 problem is SDXL-VAE's), RadEdit to Week 5 on the pod; the insertion set moves to Week 5 with a 30-host cap; v1's Table 4 carries the LaMa row and explicit 'not run' lines for the rest, which the plan already allows.
  [minor] step 1: The InternVL3.5 token-budget sweep is mis-specified: max_num = 6 cannot yield six tiles on a square 1024x1024 image.
      evidence: InternVL's dynamic tiler picks the grid whose aspect ratio is closest to the image's among grids with at most max_num tiles; for a 1:1 image with max_num = 6 the closest grids are 1x1 and 2x2, and 2x2 wins on area, giving 4 tiles plus thumbnail = 5 x 256 = 1,280 tokens; six tiles is unreachable, so the 'third budget' silently equals the second.
      fix: Use max_num in {1, 4, 9} (256+256, 1,280, 2,560 tokens) and log the realised count, as the plan already does for the assertion.
  [minor] step 1: Download routing is left implicit, and the brief's sizes (NIH 42-45 GB, RANZCR ~13 GB, Object-CXR 13.6 GB) would be a day of home bandwidth if downloaded; the HF NIH mirror is not per-file.
      evidence: Kaggle mapping needs no download (12 h CPU sessions, 4 cores, 30 GB RAM, 20 GB /kaggle/working; 112,120 PNG decodes at ~20-30 ms are 30-60 min on 4 cores); the HF mirror alkzar90/NIH-Chest-X-ray-dataset stores images as 12 zip shards of 2-4.2 GB (45.1 GB total), so single PNGs cannot be fetched by path; the student's machine needs only ~12k PNGs (~4.5 GB), CheXmask ChestX-Ray8.csv (2.0 GB) and ~15 GB of tool weights; the RANZCR JPEGs are never needed off Kaggle (only train.csv and train_annotations.csv).
      fix: State the route in PROTOCOL.md: mapping and selection on Kaggle; export the item-list PNGs (<20 GB cap) to a private HF dataset from the Kaggle notebook (NIH terms allow it) and pull them on the pod and the 1060; never download the full NIH, RANZCR or Object-CXR archives at home; download the CheXzero checkpoints (Google Drive folder, gdown quota failures are common) on Day 0.
  [minor] step 1: The Kaggle T4 fp32 backup for the Qwen3-backbone models is budgeted at 2-6 GPU-hours but would take 10-13 h per model for three prompts.
      evidence: Qwen3-VL-2B fp32 is ~8.8 GB (fits a T4) but a T4's ~8 TFLOPS fp32 gives ~2-2.5 s per 1,024-token item; 18,600 items per model = 10-13 h; the quota is 30 h/week, so only one model's backup fits in a week.
      fix: Keep the backup, but budget it at 10-13 h per model and use it for one model per week at most; prefer resuming on a second pod.
  [minor] step 1: MedGemma-1.5 is described in the thesis as 'claimed-clean'; the card does not support that wording.
      evidence: The model card's Dataset ownership and Data citation sections list ChestX-ray14 (NIH Clinical Center) next to MIMIC-CXR and Chest ImaGenome; the training paragraph names only encoder pre-training domains; the digest's second read and the tech report place CXR14 in evaluation ('OOD'). The by-eye check will not resolve more than this.
      fix: Use 'ChestX-ray14 not listed as training data; used for evaluation per the tech report; MIMIC-CXR used for training' everywhere, and keep the contamination matrix cell as 'evaluation-only (card ambiguous)'. No schedule cost.
  [minor] step 3: The ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes description is outdated and the student's two 2AFC raters are not both blind.
      evidence: The HF card states 'Five patient-disjoint folds with balanced labels; versioned fold-selection manifest included in release' and 9,095 polyline images, contradicting 'no fold file'; the ensemble is still in-sample for every RANZCR image, so the restriction to mask refinement stands. The student built the edits and is one of the two 2AFC raters.
      fix: Cite the card correctly; use the peer plus a second peer (or the advisor's graduate student) as the 2AFC raters and keep the student as adjudicator only.
  [minor] step 1: Transformers version pins are stale for Gemma 4 and over-strict for CheXOne.
      evidence: The Gemma 4 E2B card says only 'latest version of Transformers' (>=4.57 was the Qwen3-VL floor in Sep 2025); the CheXOne card's 'build from source' predates releases that include Qwen2_5_VLForConditionalGeneration.
      fix: On Day 0 pin the newest release that loads Gemma4ForConditionalGeneration and test CheXOne on the same release before creating a separate venv.
STRENGTHS:
  - lmms-eval main ships gemma4.py, gemma3.py, qwen3_vl.py, qwen2_5_vl.py and internvl3_5.py (verified in lmms_eval/models/simple today), exactly as the plan states, with no MedGemma or CheXOne entry.
  - RunPod pricing page (Sep 27, 2026): A5000 $0.27/h and 3090 $0.50/h on Secure Cloud match the plan's budget figures, and Community Cloud is cheaper still ($0.16 / $0.22), so the $40 ceiling holds even at 2x the planned pod hours ($20-35 all-in).
  - The RANZCR-to-NIH mapping is executable inside one Kaggle CPU session with no home download: competition data and the NIH mirror attach as read-only inputs, 112,120 PNG decodes take ~30-60 min on 4 cores, and only train.csv/train_annotations.csv ever need to leave Kaggle.
  - CheXmask ChestX-Ray8 masks exist (OriginalResolution/ChestX-Ray8.csv, 2.0 GB, CC BY 4.0, open access), so the keep-masks, crop regions and insertion midline are obtainable for NIH ids.
  - CheXOne is ungated, CC BY-NC-4.0, a Qwen2.5-VL-3B post-training with a recommended max_pixels of 512*512, so the CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B matched pair is loadable with a standard release and fits a 24 GB card in bf16 (~8.1 GB).
  - The CCD code is real (github.com/X-iZhang/CCD) and its project page confirms MedSigLIP zero-shot device detection over 13 device types, so Arm 1's prompt source and the removal-gate verifier exist as described.
  - The ianpan card confirms 9,095 RANZCR images with device polylines and NIH 1024x1024 inputs, consistent with the plan's mask-refinement use and its 'never a verifier' rule.
  - Gemma 4 E2B-it is ungated Apache-2.0 with 70/140/280/560/1120 visual-token budgets; MedGemma-1.5 is gated-auto, bf16, 896 px to 256 tokens; RadEdit is a click-through gate at 512 px with 200 editing steps; all seven checkpoints fit a 24 GB bf16 pod individually and the 1060 tool set (MedSigLIP, CheXzero, BiomedCLIP, LaMa, torchxrayvision) is fp32-safe and small.
  - Every result is pre-registered as reportable (floor, prior-driven, perception, representation-action gap, bias shift, failed gate), the relaxation ladder and cut list are ordered, and IRB/PhysioNet/reader requests are kept off the critical path, so cutting to the minimal core loses no claim that v1 depends on.

=== rigor === verdict: The plan is unusually well-controlled for a student study (pre-registered branches, patient clustering, yes-ratio-matched controls, detector-alone and shuffled-expert rows, kill dates) and its core real-image design (A/B/U with radiologist labels, P0/P1/P2 scoring) will yield an interpretable report whichever way the numbers fall. It is not yet rigorous in three places that a referee will find in minutes: the synthetic-probe gate (the 2AFC as written compares a device-present crop with a device-absent one, the verifier threshold admits a large share of real positives, and the verifiers are class-agnostic or sub-pixel for the objects being removed), the readouts of the edit-free negative arm (no same-state same-patient control, persistence unconditioned on the base yes-rate, and an H1 stratum that is really ICU-vs-outpatient), and the control logic in the mitigation arms (Arm 1 scored on items the same detector selected; relevant and mismatched retrieval coincide on B). Two assumptions also need Day-0 verification before they are claimed: that CheXinstruct-v2 does not contain RANZCR CLiP, and that the question wording matches RANZCR's nasoenteric and CVC-including-PICC label definitions. Every fix above is cheap (zero to two hours, under two pod-hours in total) because the pair design already supplies the missing controls; with them the study is publishable at MIDL/ML4H scale. Without them the Table 4 probe rows and the H1/H6 tests should be labelled exploratory.
MUST CHANGE:
  - Rewrite gate G2: pair each edited A crop with a device-free real crop at the registered location (the same patient's B crop, or B after a LaMa fill under A's transferred mask), pool raters to 80 trials, and judge detectability by an equivalence bound (upper Wilson bound < 0.65), not by non-significance.
  - Add same-state same-patient control pairs (150 B-B' and 150 A-A' per primary class) and redefine the device-sensitivity readouts as flip(A->B) minus flip(B->B') and P(yes_B|yes_A) minus P(yes_B).
  - Set gate G1's threshold from the verifiers' own ROC on real A vs B (edit must fall below the 5th percentile of real positives and the median of real negatives), verify locally on the 448-px polyline crop, use CheXzero only as a whole-image check on single-device images, and report gate sensitivity/specificity.
  - Drop the 'pixel-memorisation' label for the device-item gaps; add the E-irr-B trace-only control (LaMa fill on B under A's transferred mask) and report FAR(E-irr-B) minus FAR_B as the trace effect.
  - Align P0 wording with RANZCR's label definitions ('nasogastric or other enteric feeding tube'; 'central venous catheter, including a PICC line') after reading the CLiP labeler instructions on Day 0.
  - Report Arm 1 and Arm 2 on A/B/U only, or on all probe images with the gate decision as a covariate; never on gate survivors selected by the same detector.
  - Redefine the retrieval controls by prompt content (label-concordant, label-discordant, neutral) and report the realised m/k distribution of natural retrieval per arm; test H6 as discordant vs concordant at matched yes-ratio.
  - Make H1 a covariate-adjusted conditional model within View Position (adding an ECG-leads covariate), and state the raw 'other labelled line' stratum contrast as descriptive.
  - Verify CheXOne's full CheXinstruct-v2 dataset list (arXiv 2604.00493) on Day 0; if RANZCR CLiP is present, move the H2 primary to another matched pair and mark CheXOne in-sample.
  - Hold pilot patients out of the test pool, register the GO rule on the Wilson lower bound at a pre-fixed budget, label Figure 1a exploratory and make the hero 2x2 the confirmatory H7 test; fix one primary endpoint per hypothesis, compute pilot discordance per paired contrast, pool only ETT+NGT questions, and report the realised design effect.
ISSUES:
  [fatal] step 3: Gate G2 (the 2AFC 'edited-vs-untouched crop' detectability test) is ill-posed for device removal as written: if the 'untouched' crop is the original A crop at the same position, it still contains the tube, so a rater identifies the edited crop by the absence of the device, not by inpainting artefacts. The number it produces is detectability of the device, not of the edit, and the 'pass' it licenses is meaningless.
      evidence: Plan step 3: '2AFC edited-vs-untouched crop test (Sogancioglu 2018 design; 40 crop pairs per remover of identical size and position)'. Sogancioglu's design (digest med_synthetic [13]) paired inpainted healthy patches with real healthy patches, i.e. both without the target; the plan's pairing has the target in one member. Also n = 40 per remover: a one-sided binomial test needs >= 26/40 to reject 50%, so a 65%-detectable edit passes with roughly 50% probability.
      fix: Pair each edited A crop with a device-free real crop at the registered anatomical location: the same patient's B crop (free from the pair design) or, better, the B crop after a LaMa fill under A's polyline mask transferred to B, so the only difference is the fill signature. Pool both raters (80 trials per remover) and replace 'not significantly above 50%' with an equivalence bound (upper 95% Wilson bound < 0.65), reporting the point estimate and CI regardless. Cost: zero extra images, about 30 min more rater time, 10 min of LaMa on the 1060.
  [major] step 5: The A-to-B flip rate and the 'persistence rate' have no reference: the plan never measures how often a model changes its answer between two device-ABSENT (or two device-present) studies of the same patient. Without that same-state baseline, a flip after the device disappears cannot be attributed to the device rather than to ordinary between-study variation (exposure, rotation, inspiration, lines). Persistence is also uninterpretable without conditioning on the model's base yes-rate: a model that says yes to 90% of negatives has persistence about 0.9 whatever it perceives.
      evidence: Plan thesis: 'within-patient persistence rate (correct yes on A that stays yes on B) is the device-sensitivity readout'; step 5 'paired flip rate A to B'. RANZCR averages 7.9 images per patient (CLiP descriptor, PMC8553862), so same-state pairs are free. Lotfinia et al. 2026 define CGR relative to the model's answers under an irrelevant occlusion (IS) for exactly this reason.
      fix: Add 150 B-B' and 150 A-A' same-patient, same-view, same-state pairs per primary class from the same patients (one extra image per pair), run under P0 on all six runs (about 1,200 items per run, roughly 1 pod-hour, about $0.3). Report device sensitivity as flip(A->B) minus flip(B->B') with a clustered bootstrap, and persistence as P(yes_B | yes_A) minus P(yes_B) (or the within-pair odds ratio) rather than the raw conditional rate. Pre-register both on OSF.
  [major] step 3: Gate G1 is lenient and its verifiers are near-blind to the objects being removed. 'Below the 90th percentile of real class-negative scores' means an edited image only has to look at least as negative as the top decile of real negatives; with AUC about 0.85, a large share of real device-POSITIVE images already satisfy that. CheXzero scores 'support devices' class-agnostically at 320 px, so on the common images where a CVC remains after ETT removal its score cannot fall, and at 320 px a CVC is 0.3-2 px wide. MedSigLIP's 0.852 AUC is for generic support devices on 518 CheXpert-test items; its per-class device prompts (ccd_utils.py) have no published accuracy. Both verifiers are MIMIC-trained CLIP-style models, so their errors are correlated, not independent.
      evidence: Digest med_synthetic/fill: 'CheXzero ... template pair ('{}', 'no {}') at 320x320'; 'MedSigLIP's 0.852 Support Devices AUC is on 518 unnamed ELIXR CXR evaluation examples'; 'the CCD device prompts have no published validation'. CVC label totals (21,323+8,460+3,198 = 32,981) exceed the 30,083 images, so most class-negative images for ETT still carry a CVC.
      fix: (1) Pre-register the gate's own operating point from its ROC on real A vs B images (step 2 already computes the AUC): an edit passes only if its score falls below the 5th percentile of real positives AND below the median of real negatives; report gate sensitivity (share of real A images that would fail) and specificity. (2) Verify locally: run MedSigLIP on the 448-px crop centred on the polyline before and after editing (the ETT then spans about 25 px of a 448 crop) and require the after-score to fall inside the distribution of the same patient's B crop at that location; validate crop-level AUC on A-vs-B crops first. (3) Use CheXzero only as a whole-image sanity check on single-device (S5) images. Cost: about 1 h on the 1060 and CPU; no new data.
  [major] step 3: The 'real-minus-edited FAR gap' and 'real-minus-inserted hit-rate gap' are labelled pixel-memorisation signals, but by the plan's own contamination argument no model has seen RANZCR device labels, and memorised NIH pixels carry no device label; the gaps therefore measure residual trace plus context plus insertion realism, not memorisation. The E-irr control does not isolate trace either: it fills a lung region, while the ETT mask sits in the mediastinum over the tracheal air column, where LaMa must hallucinate anatomy from above/below context.
      evidence: Plan step 3: 'the real-minus-edited FAR gap per model as the pixel-memorisation signal'; data plan: 'the general-vs-medical test is label-clean because no model lists RANZCR device labels'. Plan step 3 E-irr: 'LaMa fill of an equal-size lung region away from any device'. A dilated ETT mask (25 px stripe + 8-15 px radius at 1024 px) covers roughly the whole tracheal lucency.
      fix: Drop the memorisation wording for device items (keep it only for the ChestX-Det findings row, where NIH NLP labels were trained on). Add the trace-only control E-irr-B: LaMa applied to the same patient's B image under A's polyline mask registered to B (device-free image, identical fill signature, same anatomy); FAR(E-irr-B) minus FAR_B is the pure trace effect, and FAR_edited(LaMa) minus FAR_edited(RadEdit) on the same A images is the remover-specific trace. Cost: 40-300 LaMa fills (< 10 min on the 1060) and about 0.3 pod-hour.
  [major] step 2: Question wording does not match the label definitions for two of the three classes. RANZCR's 'NGT' category is nasoenteric (NG, OG, feeding and nasojejunal tubes) and its 'CVC' category includes PICC and dialysis lines; P0 asks for 'a nasogastric tube' and 'a central venous catheter'. A model correctly answering 'no nasogastric tube' on an orogastric tube, or 'no CVC' on a PICC, is scored as a miss, biasing hit rate and FAR for NGT (secondary class) and CVC (hard class) and the four-question pooled analyses.
      evidence: CLiP descriptor as digested: 'polylines for ETT, nasoenteric tubes and CVCs'; CCD's device vocabulary separates NG_tube, OG_tube, Feeding_tube, Enteric_tube, CVC, Central_line, PICC_line (ccd_utils.py); the plan's own class text says 'central venous catheter (CVC) including PICC'.
      fix: Align wording to the labeler instructions in the CLiP descriptor: 'a nasogastric or other enteric feeding tube' and 'a central venous catheter (including a PICC line)'; keep the bare wording as a paraphrase row (P0-bare) on the hero to measure the wording effect. Confirm the labeler definitions from the descriptor's methods section on Day 0. Cost: zero.
  [major] step 5: H1's co-occurrence contrast (FAR_B with vs without another labelled line) is confounded with acquisition and acuity. Because CVC labels outnumber images, 'another labelled line present' is essentially 'CVC present', i.e. an ICU, AP/supine, often opacified film, while 'no labelled line' is a small, distinct population of upright outpatient films. The contrast therefore mixes the device prior with view, positioning and disease, and the plan's view matching applies only within pairs, not across the H1 strata.
      evidence: CLiP Table 2: CVC labels 32,981 on 30,083 images; the brief's own count that only about 12,452 images carry no ETT/NGT/Swan-Ganz label; plan H1: 'FAR_B(other labelled lines present) minus FAR_B(none) per model, patient-clustered BCa bootstrap'.
      fix: (1) Report H1 within View Position strata and the stratum-by-view cross-table; (2) add a cheap unlabelled ICU-context covariate the student can mark in 2-3 s per image (ECG electrodes/leads present) so prior-with-no-labelled-line can be probed; (3) make the primary H1 test the conditional logistic model with view, other-line count, ECG leads and NIH labels as covariates, and state the raw stratum difference as descriptive. Cost: 1-2 h of labelling, CPU.
  [major] step 7: On the arm that matters (B, device absent), 'relevant' and 'mismatched' retrieval collapse into the same stimulus: ICU-looking B images retrieve device-positive neighbours, so relevant k=3 yields 'm of 3 contain an ETT' with m near 3, which is exactly what 'neighbours among opposite-label images' also yields. H6's 'harm' control then adds nothing on B, and the relevant-vs-mismatched contrast on A measures something different from the one on B.
      evidence: Plan step 7: 'mismatched (k nearest among pool images whose label is the opposite of the test item's truth)' and the pre-registered expectation 'ICU-looking negatives retrieve device-positive neighbours'. When Retrieval Hurts (arXiv 2609.31733) found normal images retrieved abnormal precedents in 24/28 cases, the same collapse.
      fix: Define retrieval conditions by prompt content, not by neighbour selection: label-concordant (m/k agrees with truth), label-discordant (m/k opposes truth) and neutral (m = k/2 or k even), each with the same k, plus natural retrieval reported with its realised m/k distribution per arm. Test H6 as discordant-vs-concordant at matched yes-ratio and report over-reliance and copy rate per condition. Cost: zero extra VLM passes (prompts are text), one more condition on all six runs (about 0.7 pod-hour).
  [major] step 6: Arm 1 on the synthetic probes is circular. Edited survivors are selected by low MedSigLIP and CheXzero device scores and inserted survivors by high scores; fusing those same scores into the logits guarantees a 'no' push on edited items and a 'yes' push on inserted items, so any Arm 1 gain on the probes is manufactured by the gate. The verifier-independence rule in the protocol covers only the pacemaker branch.
      evidence: Plan step 6: 'Fuse a clipped log-odds device score from MedSigLIP zero-shot (or CheXzero) into the cached first-token yes/no logits of every run on A/B/U (and on gated synthetic survivors)'; step 3 gate G1 uses the same two models; protocol: 'the Arm-1 expert is never the detector that selected an item's label' applied only to shortlisted pacemaker items.
      fix: Report Arm 1 (and Arm 2 with MedSigLIP embeddings) on A/B/U only, where labels are radiologist-derived; on the probes report Arm 1 over ALL edited/inserted images rather than survivors, with the gate decision as a covariate, or split the verifiers (V1 gates, V2 is the expert) and state their score correlation. Cost: zero.
  [major] step 1: The floor pilot is used twice: its items feed the GO decision (choice of class and 'best budget' per model-class pair) and then become the confirmatory H7 figure, and the pilot patients are not excluded from the A/B/U test pool. Picking the best of three budgets on the same 200 items that are reported inflates Figure 1a, and 54 model x class x budget looks against a 0.70 point-estimate threshold give roughly a 10% chance that a true 0.60 pair is declared GO.
      evidence: Plan: 'The pilot items double as the resolution figure (Figure 1a, H7)'; 'GO for a model-class pair if balanced accuracy >= 0.70 ... at the best budget (Wilson 95% half-width about 0.07 at n = 200)'; the dev split (40 patients) is the only hold-out named.
      fix: Hold the pilot patients out of A/B/U (they are about 300 of 3,791 patients); register the GO rule on the Wilson lower bound (LB >= 0.60) at a budget fixed in advance per family, with the budget sweep exploratory; make the hero 2x2 on 100 fresh pairs the confirmatory H7 test and label Figure 1a exploratory. Cost: zero compute, a slightly smaller pair pool.
  [major] step 0: The 'label-clean' claim that carries H2 and C3 rests on CheXOne's 30-dataset training list, which the digest only reproduces as an 'incl.' excerpt. RANZCR CLiP is a public Kaggle dataset of device labels and polylines on NIH images, a natural inclusion for a CXR foundation model; if it is in CheXinstruct-v2, CheXOne has trained on the exact test images with device labels and the primary general-vs-medical test is contaminated at the label level.
      evidence: Digest med_models [3]: CheXOne trained on '14.7M instruction/reasoning samples from 30 public datasets incl. MIMIC-CXR, CheXpert Plus, ReXGradient, ... Object-CXR, PadChest, ChestXray14, RSNA, SIIM, RadGraph (official test splits held out)'; the brief's 'RANZCR device labels are listed by none' was checked against cards and papers, not the full table.
      fix: Day-0: read the dataset table of arXiv 2604.00493 (and CheXinstruct v1, arXiv 2401.12208) and record the full list in the contamination matrix. If RANZCR CLiP appears, move the H2 primary to NV-Reason-CXR-3B vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B (check its card the same way) or MedGemma-1.5 vs gemma-3-4b-it with the encoder confound stated, and keep CheXOne as a labelled in-sample row. Cost: 1 h.
  [major] step 5: Power and multiplicity are mis-specified. H1 is an unpaired two-proportion contrast on roughly 180 vs 120 B items (detects only about 15-point differences at 80% power); H2 names two primary endpoints (hallucination-free rate and balanced accuracy); the pilot discordance from '35 pairs through the hero' is the within-model A-to-B flip rate, not the discordance of the paired contrasts actually tested (model vs model in H2, prompt vs prompt in H3); and the pooled 'about 1,200 items per arm' includes the near-degenerate Swan-Ganz question and ignores a design effect that, with four questions per image and two images per patient, can reach 2-3.
      evidence: General digest statistics item: unpaired 85 vs 80 needs 906 per group; McNemar 5-point difference needs 312/469/626/940 pairs at discordance 0.10/0.15/0.20/0.30; plan step 2: 'Pilot: 35 pairs through the hero under P0; discordance; N by the McNemar formula'; plan H2: 'paired difference in hallucination-free rate and balanced accuracy'.
      fix: One primary endpoint per hypothesis (balanced accuracy for H2, abstention rate for H3, FAR_B for H1); run the 35-pair pilot through CheXOne, its base and the three prompts so each paired contrast gets its own discordance; pool only the ETT and NGT questions; report the realised design effect and effective N in the power memo; Holm across the six models within each hypothesis family. Cost: +0.2 pod-hour in the pilot.
  [major] step 2: Only the primary class is verified on B/U images; the other three questions per image ride on raw RANZCR labels, and the noisiest of those is 'CVC absent' (faint CVCs are the known labeler weak spot, and CVCs are present in most images), so the 'within-image adversarial negatives' and the four-question pooled analyses inherit the 15% discrepancy exactly where it is worst.
      evidence: Plan step 2: 'the student reads every B and U image (device absent?) and every primary-class A image'; CLiP descriptor: 5,100 consensus labels re-reviewed with about 15% discrepancies; digest med_data [1]: 'faint or short CVCs and Swan-Ganz catheters are harder and should be flagged'.
      fix: Verify all four labels on B and U (about 2 extra hours at 5 s per extra label), flag faint CVCs ambiguous, and over-sample CVC-absent and NGT-incompletely-imaged items in the 200-item second-rater audit; until then report the non-primary questions as label-noise-sensitive secondary rows with the sensitivity analysis (e). Cost: 2 h student time.
  [minor] step 6: Arm 1 hyperparameters (beta in {0.5,1,2}, gamma in {2,5,10}, tau) and every threshold, dilation radius, k and layer are chosen on a dev split of 40 patients (80 images, about 40 device-negative), which is far too small to select 9+ combinations per model without overfitting the reported gain.
      evidence: Plan protocol: 'DEV SPLIT: 40 patients (80 images) outside the test set for every regex, threshold, dilation radius, beta, gamma, tau, k and layer choice'; CCD defaults are gamma in {2,5,10} with a single beta.
      fix: Pre-register CCD's defaults (beta = 1, gamma = 5) as the primary Arm 1 setting and report the sweep as a sensitivity table; for tau use cross-fitting on two patient halves of the test set. Cost: zero.
  [minor] step 3: RadEdit was trained on the NIH-CXR images themselves (112,120 images with labels converted to text), so DDPM inversion of an NIH image is in-sample and may reconstruct memorised content inside the edit mask; and its 512-px output is resampled to 1024 while A/B/U stay native, so FAR_edited(RadEdit) vs FAR_B confounds remover with a global blur that the 'untouched control' (not part of A/B/U) does not remove from the comparison.
      evidence: Digest fill item on RadEdit: 'trained on ... NIH-CXR (112,120 images with labels converted to text)'; plan: 'any 512-px RadEdit output is resampled to 1024 with one documented resampler applied identically to its untouched control'.
      fix: Add A-roundtrip and B-roundtrip rows (real images passed through the same 512->1024 resampler) for the hero and MedGemma (about 0.3 pod-hour); run the pHash near-duplicate check on RadEdit outputs against the source; state RadEdit's in-sample status in Appendix D.
  [minor] step 4: The insertion gate is lenient in the mirror direction ('above the 10th percentile of real positives' admits many real negatives), the donor crop is obtained by 'thresholding the bright device inside the dilated polyline', which fails over the mediastinum where the ETT stripe barely exceeds the background, and the plan does not fix tip placement relative to the carina, so DREAM-type implausible placements are likely and hit_inserted conflates realism with sensitivity.
      evidence: Plan step 4 gate and method; ianpan carina heatmap is allowed as a placement aid; RANZCR's ETT 'normal' class is defined by tip position above the carina.
      fix: Use the polyline rasterised at the measured half-width as the alpha matte and composite a high-pass 'device layer' (donor minus its median-filtered background) additively onto the host instead of cut-paste; place the tip 3-5 cm above the ianpan carina estimate; set the gate at the 95th percentile of real negatives AND above the median of real positives; report hit_inserted separately for 2AFC-detected and undetected items. Cost: 1-2 h coding.
  [minor] step 1: The mapping's first pass (SHA-256 of decoded pixels when sizes match) can never succeed: the CLiP descriptor states the Kaggle JPEGs were upscaled to original resolution and JPEG re-encoded, so no RANZCR file is 1024x1024 or bit-identical to an NIH PNG. The dimension key is also many-to-many (NIH has only a few hundred distinct OriginalImage sizes), so the pHash pass over all 112,120 NIH images is the real method, not the fallback.
      evidence: CLiP descriptor verbatim: 'All images were upscaled to original resolution before conversion to JPEG'; NIH metadata OriginalImage[Width,Height] values such as 2682x2749 recur across thousands of images.
      fix: Skip the pixel-hash pass; compute 256-px pHash/dHash for all NIH images (about 40 CPU-minutes) and match within dimension-key buckets, with the 200-pair visual spot check; the same pass settles whether labelers saw only 1024-px content (downsample a RANZCR JPEG to 1024 and check SSIM against the NIH PNG). Cost: zero.
  [minor] step 5: Figure 1b's threshold 'fixed at 5% risk on U and transferred to B and A' is undefined for a yes-biased 2B model whose risk on U never reaches 5% at any coverage; and the 'logit-vs-verbal coverage gap' compares P0 logits with P2 verbal abstention, which differ in prompt as well as mechanism.
      evidence: Lotfinia 2026: LLaVA-Med-7B answers yes to everything; HalluCXR: explicit yes-bias in Gemini and LLaVA; plan step 5 softmax block.
      fix: Pre-register fallback risk levels (10%, 20%) and report AURC and coverage at each; compute the gap on P2-hi items using the P2-hi first-token distribution over yes/no/abstain forms, with the P0 logits as a second row. Cost: zero.
  [minor] step 5: H2 is interpretable only if the base model is above the floor; the GO rule never looks at Qwen2.5-VL-3B, so the primary claim-2 test may compare a near-ceiling specialist (CheXOne ReXVQA presence 0.947) with a floor base, which answers 'does post-training lift a floor model' rather than the advisor's claim.
      evidence: Plan decision procedure: GO is defined on the three generalists and MedGemma; ReXVQA Table 3 gives Qwen2.5-VL 65% on 4-option tubes/lines and CheXOne 0.947 presence.
      fix: Include Qwen2.5-VL-3B in the pilot GO table and pre-register the H2 reading per branch: if the base is NO-GO, the primary H2 endpoint becomes FAR_B at matched hit_A (or the Phi_9 and abstention contrast) and the balanced-accuracy difference is reported as a floor-to-ceiling lift. Cost: zero (the pilot already runs the pair).
  [minor] step 1: InternVL3.5's tiling cannot give 6 tiles for a square image: with max_num = 6 the closest aspect-ratio grid for 1:1 is 2x2, so the realised budget is 4 tiles plus thumbnail (1,280 tokens at an effective 896 px), not '>= 6 tiles'; the brief's figure would mis-state the token column.
      evidence: InternVL dynamic preprocessing picks the grid closest to the image aspect ratio among grids with at most max_num tiles; digest fill: 'InternVL3.5-2B ... tiles at 448 px, up to 12 tiles, 256 tokens per tile'.
      fix: Assert the realised tile count in the smoke test and put the measured token count in every table; if a 6-tile budget is wanted, pad the square NIH image to 3:2 (documented, applied identically to all arms) or use max_num = 9 (3x3). Cost: zero.
  [minor] step 0: Person-time: the plan itself puts the pre-Nov-8 workload at 95-110 h against 80-100 available, with Week 1 at 25-30 h for a student working 15-20 h per week, and the OSF registration of H1-H8 happens after pilot outputs have been seen on the same models.
      evidence: Plan design_rationale 'Honest accounting' paragraph; step 1 timeline (pilot Wed Oct 14, OSF Thu Oct 15).
      fix: Apply the cut list pre-emptively from Day 1 (InternVL3.5-2B and the Arm 2 extras first), register H1-H8 wording on Oct 13 before the pilot with the class decision as the only post-pilot amendment, and state in the registration that pilot yes-rates were seen before the class choice. Cost: zero.
STRENGTHS:
  - Pre-registered decision procedure with branches (i)-(iv) that each yield a complete paper, so a floor result on every tube class is still Result 1 with MedGemma, MedSigLIP and CheXzero as positive controls.
  - The edit-free same-patient negative arm (B) with radiologist labels on both studies is genuinely novel in CXR hallucination benchmarking (verified absent in HalluCXR, MedVH, HEAL-MedVQA, Lotfinia 2026, MC-CXR) and avoids every inpainting-trace confound for the headline table.
  - Honest wording discipline: 'within-patient contrast' not 'counterfactual', 'no other labelled line' not 'no other device', 'to our knowledge' on every first, no model called 'unseen' without a card citation.
  - Patient-level clustering as the default unit (pairs, U one per patient, cluster bootstrap, mixed model with patient and image effects) and Wilson intervals with n on every proportion.
  - Mitigation arms carry the controls the cited literature lacks: detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias sweep at matched yes-ratio, Mirage OLM, kNN-vote-alone, over-reliance and copy rate, random and wrong-region crops; every gain is reported net of a yes-ratio-matched control.
  - Verifier-independence rules exist in principle (ianpan restricted to masks and placement; detectors never ground truth; expert swap on shortlisted items) and the per-class detector AUC on RANZCR labels is a real by-product.
  - Identical 1024-px NIH pixels across arms with per-model visual-token logging and assertion, a uniform bf16 precision policy, one venv per family and a 99% runner-vs-harness agreement check.
  - The CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B matched pair is the right instrument for the advisor's claim 2 at this scale, with the MedGemma comparisons correctly labelled size- and encoder-confounded.
  - Kill dates and an ordered cut list protect report v1 by Nov 8; the citation-correction memo to the advisor is specific and verifiable (Kim et al. medians, MedHallBench reference, CXR-VisHal provenance, Med-HallMark count, DREAM PMCID).
  - Compliance design keeps RANZCR rows, polylines and UIDs off the pod, releases NIH filenames with a rebuild script, labels edited images as synthetic, and files NCBE/IRB and reader requests in Week 1 without putting them on the critical path.
