=== advisor-and-ethics === verdict: Conditional go. Read as the JRCAI project manager: the plan does analyze, detect and reduce hallucination in open-source LVLMs on healthcare QA with a retrieval arm and an expert-fusion arm under controls the RAG and CCD literature lacks, frames itself correctly as the first image-grounded test of the advisor's claims 2 and 3, and states the Kim et al. provenance (text-only Med-HALT, 11 LLMs, 350 items) and all five citation corrections in agreement with the primary sources; but 'categorize' has no exhibit and literal visual grounding is deferred to v2, so v1 must gain a categorisation table and a minimal region-grounding arm. Read as the KFUPM IRB: the content of the ethics plan is right (public de-identified data, NCBE, Articles 10.19/10.33, no PhysioNet data, no online APIs, 'not for clinical use', TRIPOD-LLM/CLAIM), but it is mis-sequenced (pilot on Oct 14, IRB email Oct 16-17; 'before starting the study' is the rule), under-counts NCBE for raters, never asks whether the umbrella project's approval covers the student, and leaves the clinician reader's status open. Data terms are respected on the critical path (NIH attribution trio, CheXmask CC BY, HAI-DEF outputs, MSRLA, Qwen Research, CC BY-NC) except that the release plan would ship rasterised RANZCR polylines and per-image labels while promising not to, and label-organised files reach the pod. Overclaims are few but systematic: 'radiologist labels' (43 labellers including trainees and medical officers), 'verified' synthetic probes in the title before any gate, and three over-broad firsts. The Nov 8 report v1 is feasible only if the two synthetic probes shrink to one CPU insertion arm and the cut list is applied from Day 1, because the plan's own 95-110 h estimate omits the term-week 10-12 exams; the 4-page paper is a Dec 10 target and must be collapsed to three RQs and three exhibits. With the nine must-changes below the plan is sound, honest and advisor-aligned.
MUST CHANGE:
  - Release plan: withhold device masks, pair/arm structure, the 30k mapping table and edited/inserted images unless the Kaggle Rules text (read and quoted on Day 0) explicitly permits derivative distribution; release NIH filenames with opaque item ids, CheXmask masks, outputs, statistics, code and a rebuild script that regenerates everything RANZCR-derived after rule acceptance.
  - Ethics today (Oct 12): send the IRB determination request before any data processing, cc the advisor; ask the advisor whether the JRCAI umbrella project holds a KFUPM IRB number to add the student to; start the NCBE course for the student and every named rater; hold peer and clinician rating sessions until a determination or the umbrella approval covers them; cite Article 10.19 for expedited review and 10.33 only for follow-up relief.
  - Replace every 'radiologist label/radiologist-labelled' for RANZCR (C1, protocol, retrieval prompt, Step 7) with the descriptor's '43 trained labellers: radiologists, radiology trainees and hospital medical officers' and add the labeller mix to Table 1.
  - Add a zero-compute categorisation exhibit (Table 2b mapping each measured failure mode to Brooks and Anastasio and MediHall tiers) and promote a minimal hero-only Arm 3 (CheXmask crop vs random crop, agree-or-abstain, under one pod hour) into Week 4 so v1 meets the umbrella's 'categorize' and 'grounding' literally.
  - Re-plan Weeks 2-4 around the term-week 10-12 exams: write exam dates in as zero-hour days, apply cut-list items 1-3 and 6 from Day 1, run only the CPU cut-paste insertion probe before Nov 8 (2AFC as covariate, not kill gate), move the three-remover pilot to Week 5, and retitle 'gated' rather than 'verified' synthetic probes.
  - Fix pre-registration: H7 exploratory on the pilot and confirmatory on the fresh hero 2x2; flag pilot-image overlap with the A/B/U lists; word the OSF H2 text neutrally and embargo the registration until the advisor has reviewed it.
  - Advisor-paper handling: verify each of the five citation issues by eye in both PDFs, raise the Kim et al. provenance verbally in the Week-1 meeting before the OSF text is frozen, send the written notes in Week 2 framed as notes for his v2, and move the Kim-median replication from the contribution list to a discussion footnote.
  - Collapse the 4-page manuscript to one thesis sentence, three RQs and three main exhibits (Table 2, merged Table 3, Figure 1) with Table 1 and Table 4 in the appendix; shorten the title; narrow the three over-broad 'first' claims and relabel 1 - FAR as an analogue of Kim et al.'s hallucination-free rate.
  - Keep RANZCR-derived labels off the pod: opaque item ids and a flat item list on the pod, arms and labels joined locally, retrieval prompts carrying only aggregate 'm of k' counts, interleaved-image variant aggregated or dropped; add the OpenRAIL-M and Gemma Terms citations and the 'research stimuli, not medical interpretation' sentence to the methods.
ISSUES:
  [major] step 10: The release plan contradicts itself and would redistribute RANZCR Competition Data in derived form. It releases 'device masks keyed by NIH filename' (rasterised, dilated train_annotations.csv polylines), pair ids and arm membership (A = device present, B = absent, i.e. the train.csv label per image), the full 30k mapping table, and conditionally edited/inserted images whose diff against the NIH original recovers the polyline, while also promising 'NEVER RANZCR CSV rows, polylines or StudyInstanceUIDs'.
      evidence: Plan ethics_and_release: 'CheXmask-derived masks and device masks keyed by NIH filename ... edited and inserted NIH-derived images if the Kaggle rules ... do not forbid derived works'; data_plan: 'the mapping table is itself a releasable artefact'. Kaggle's competition endpoint returns license 'Subject to Competition Rules', rulesRequired true; the Rules page is a JavaScript shell (re-fetched 12 Oct 2026), so the standard 'Data Security' clause ('not to transmit, duplicate, publish, redistribute or otherwise provide or make available the Competition Data to any party not participating') is still unread. A mask rasterised from a polyline is the polyline; arm membership is the label.
      fix: Release only: NIH filenames with opaque item ids (no arm, label or pair columns), CheXmask masks (CC BY 4.0), prompts, per-item model outputs, statistics, the mapping code (not the 30k table) and a rebuild script that regenerates the mapping, pairs, arms, device masks and edits from train.csv/train_annotations.csv after the user accepts the Rules. Release device masks, pair structure or edited/inserted images only if the Rules text quoted in the dataset card explicitly permits derivative distribution. State this in Section 8 and the dataset card, and prefer LaMa (Apache-2.0) outputs over RadEdit outputs if any edited image is ever released.
  [major] step 1: IRB sequencing is backwards and the team-member requirement is under-counted. KFUPM requires approval 'before starting the study' and lists 'use of personal or sensitive data' in scope, but the plan runs the mapping and the floor pilot on Oct 13-14 and emails IRB@kfupm.edu.sa on Oct 16-17. The NCBE certificate is required for every team member, yet only the student obtains one while a peer (and possibly the advisor's graduate student) will rate. The Day-1 advisor email never asks whether the JRCAI umbrella project already holds an IRB approval the student can be added to, which is the fastest lawful route.
      evidence: KFUPM IRB page (fetched 12 Oct 2026): 'Any faculty or researcher staff, or student conducting research involving human subjects must obtain IRB approval before starting the study'; scope includes 'the use of personal or sensitive data'; required documents include 'NCBE certificate for all team members'; no processing time stated. Plan Step 1: 'Wed Oct 14 (pod, bf16): ... floor pilot'; 'Fri-Sat Oct 16-17: ... IRB email'; the advisor email lists five asks, none about an existing approval. Digest fit_and_ethics [13]: no NCBE article exempts public de-identified secondary data from review altogether.
      fix: On Oct 12: (a) email IRB@kfupm.edu.sa the one-page determination request, cc the advisor, stating that only public de-identified images are analysed, quoting the CLiP descriptor's 'As both datasets are publicly available IRB approval is waived' and NIH's de-identification, and asking which category applies; (b) add to the advisor email: 'does the umbrella project hold a KFUPM IRB number; can I be added as personnel?'; (c) start the NCBE course today for the student and the named second rater, and for any later rater before they rate; (d) proceed with pipeline building and model inference on public data, but hold every session that generates new human-rater data (peer 2AFC/audit, clinician reading) until the determination or the umbrella approval covers them; (e) record the submission and determination dates in PROTOCOL.md and the report.
  [major] step 2: 'Radiologist labels' is an overclaim repeated throughout: C1 ('same-patient device-present/absent pairs with radiologist labels'), the protocol ('RANZCR radiologist labels are the labels of record'), the retrieval prompt ('A radiologist-labelled archive was searched'), Step 7 inputs ('patient-disjoint radiologist-labelled pool'). The CLiP labellers were 43 people including radiologists, radiology trainees and hospital medical officers, about 30% double-read, with about 15% discrepancy on re-review.
      evidence: CLiP descriptor (PMC8553862, fetched 12 Oct 2026): 'There were a total of 43 labelers including radiologists, radiology trainees and hospital medical officers'; 'Approximately 30% of the radiographs were double-labeled and 10% were triple-labeled'; '5100 consensus labels were randomly reviewed by experienced labellers, with approximately 15% discrepancies demonstrated'.
      fix: Replace every 'radiologist label/radiologist-labelled' for RANZCR with 'RANZCR CLiP labels (43 trained labellers: radiologists, radiology trainees and hospital medical officers)' in captions, protocol, limitations and the OSF text; change the Arm 2 prompt to 'An expert-labelled archive was searched ...'; reserve 'radiologist' for ChestX-Det (three board-certified radiologists) and Object-CXR (12 radiologists); add the labeller mix and the 30%/10% double/triple-read figures to Table 1 beside the 15% discrepancy.
  [major] step 0: The umbrella's three verbs are not all exhibits. 'Categorize' has no table: the Brooks and Anastasio and MediHall vocabulary is used only in the discussion and MediHall weights are 'optional'. 'Grounding' is literal only in Arm 3, which is gated to v2 with a Nov 15 kill date; Arm 1 is detector/expert logit fusion (CCD's own term is 'expert-informed'). Report v1 could therefore reach the JRCAI manager with retrieval as the only recognisable umbrella technique and no categorisation exhibit.
      evidence: Umbrella: 'detect, categorize, and reduce hallucinations using grounding and retrieval-based techniques'. Plan mitigation_arms: 'ARM 3 (gated to v2, kill date Nov 15; hero and MedGemma), region-focused second pass'; advisor_alignment: 'Med-HallMark's MediHall severity tiers ... as optional weights'; the open-ended arm that would permit HalluCXR-style categorisation is itself a gated Step-9 extension; digest med_mitigation [7] describes CCD's stage 2 as the 'expert-informed stage'.
      fix: (a) Add Table 2b 'Failure-mode categorisation' from readouts already computed, zero extra compute: confabulation (yes on B/U with no referent) vs data-driven (yes on an edited trace or inserted seam) per Brooks and Anastasio; acquiescence (PN flips); over-confident guessing (wrong answers under P2-hi); representation-action gap (logit-separable errors not abstained); retrieval over-reliance; detector bias-shift; each mapped to a MediHall tier. (b) Promote a minimal Arm 3 into Week 4 on the hero only (CheXmask region crop vs random crop of equal size, agree-or-abstain, about 1,800 items, under one pod hour) so v1 carries a literal visual-grounding arm; fund it by moving P2-lo/PN/P0b/BLIND-demo on MedGemma to Week 5. (c) In Section 1 define 'grounding-based' as the family {expert/detector grounding, region grounding} with CCD, ARCD and EVADE cited, so Arm 1's label is defensible.
  [major] step 0: The person-time budget ignores the student's exams in term weeks 10-12 (Oct 18-Nov 5), which v1 and the design log named the likeliest cause of slippage. Those weeks are exactly Phases 2-4 (item set, core evaluation, mitigation, report v1), and the plan already admits 95-110 h of work against 80-100 h available.
      evidence: STRATEGY.md 1.2: mid-term break Oct 20-22 is week 10, week 13 is Nov 8-12, so weeks 10-12 are Oct 18-Nov 5; design log line 358: 'the student's exams in term weeks 10-12 are the most likely causes of slippage'; plan top_risks: 'about 95-110 h before Nov 8 against roughly 80-100 available with the break week'; v2 mentions the break but never exams.
      fix: On Day 0 write the exam dates into the schedule as zero-hour days and re-derive available hours; apply cut-list items 1, 2, 3 and 6 from Day 1 rather than 'if behind' (extra prompts hero-only; Arm 2 to the three interpretable rows; removal probe to LaMa plus E-irr; insertion to 50 hosts); keep Week 1 at 25-30 h only if no exam falls in it; schedule the second rater's two hours and the verification backlog on the break days.
  [major] step 3: Two synthetic probes before Nov 8 cost 10-15 h of the scarcest resource (pipelines, two 2AFC blocks times two raters, a rubric read, a DREAM audit, Appendix D) inside the exam weeks, and the title already calls them 'Verified Synthetic Device Probes' before any gate has run. The student title's 'synthetic visual data' is honoured by one label-certain insertion arm even if its 2AFC fails; the removal pilot's expected outcome is failure for RadEdit and unknown for LaMa.
      evidence: Steps 3-4 person-time: 'pipeline 2-3 h, 2AFC 1 h per rater, rubric 2 h per reader' plus '3-4 h plus 1 h per rater'; Step 4 gate wording is ambiguous about whether a failed 2AFC kills the panel run ('not significantly above 50%; the number reported regardless'); digest edited-arm fill [6]: cxr-grounding found RadEdit 'could not remove findings convincingly'; title string of the plan.
      fix: Before Nov 8 run only the CPU cut-paste insertion, with the two-verifier gate selecting items and the 2AFC reported as a covariate, never as a kill gate for the panel run; move the three-remover pilot to Week 5 (LaMa plus E-irr first; RadEdit and SD-1.5 only if pod budget and time remain); retitle to 'gated synthetic device probes' and describe them in the abstract as 'validity-measured'; keep Appendix D as the home of the measurement either way.
  [major] step 1: Pre-registration discipline leaks in three places: H7 (monotone gain with token budget, CVC lowest) is a 'pre-registered' hypothesis whose test data are the Day-2/3 pilot items, run and inspected before the Oct 17 registration, while the pilot is simultaneously 'registered as exploratory'; pilot images are neither excluded from nor flagged in the A/B/U lists; and the OSF registration (public by default) will characterise 'the advisor's claim 2' and its text-only provenance before the advisor has seen the memo.
      evidence: object_classes: 'The pilot items double as the resolution figure (Figure 1a, H7)'; protocol PRE-REGISTRATION: 'the floor pilot registered as exploratory'; Step 5: 'H7 ... from the pilot items with cluster-bootstrap trend intervals'; advisor_alignment: memo 'sent to Dr. Behzad in Week 3' while the OSF registration is due 'by Oct 17'.
      fix: Register H7 as exploratory on the pilot and confirmatory on the fresh hero 2x2 of Step 8 (100 pairs at 256/391/640/1,024 tokens); add a pilot_item flag to the item table and report its overlap with A/B/U; word the OSF H2 text neutrally ('the general-vs-medical difference reported by Kim et al. 2025 on Med-HALT and adopted by Alshahrani and Behzad 2026') and use OSF's embargo option until the advisor has reviewed the wording.
  [major] step 1: The advisor-paper handling is right in substance but wrong in sequence and framing: the five corrections come from automated PDF text extraction and are not yet checked by eye; the memo goes out in Week 3, after the OSF registration and after Sections 1-3 are drafted around 'the advisor's claim 2'; and the Kim-median replication note is listed as a contribution (why_unique 11) of a paper the advisor is meant to co-author.
      evidence: Digest advisor-references fill: corrections obtained by 'pypdf text extraction of the advisor's and Kim et al.'s PDFs'; plan advisor_alignment: 'Citation errors fixed in a one-page memo sent to Dr. Behzad in Week 3'; why_unique (11): 'A replication note that Kim et al.'s printed medians (76.6 vs 51.3) do not reproduce'; digest: Kim et al. v2 prints baselines whose medians are 71.2 and 36.4.
      fix: Week 1: open both PDFs and confirm each item by eye (the 76.6/51.3 sentence and Fig. 3 caption, reference [45], the four CXR-VisHal mentions, the 'over 20,000' figure, PMC11844622 vs PMC12866389, the Section 4.2 p-value); raise the Kim provenance verbally at the first meeting before the OSF text is frozen; send the written 'notes for your v2' in Week 2 with primary-source quotes attached; move the median note to a discussion footnote and let the advisor decide whether a co-authored manuscript mentions it.
  [major] step 10: The story has regrown past what a 4-page paper holds: four RQs, nine hypotheses (H0-H8), five contributions plus eleven 'why unique' points, Tables 1, 2, 3a, 3b, 3c and 4, Figures 1a/1b and Appendices A-F, undoing the v1 advisor-skeptic fix that collapsed the main text to one spine; the title is 45 words.
      evidence: Design log line 336: '[advisor, major, three papers in one] Accepted: main text collapsed to Table 1 ... Table 2 ... Figure 1 ... Table 3 ... Table 4 (if space)'; plan report_outline Section 5 lists 5.1-5.6 with Tables 1-4 plus 3c; the thesis field is a single 700-word paragraph.
      fix: For the 4-page manuscript: one thesis sentence; three RQs (see vs guess with penalised abstention; general vs medical on the matched pair; grounding vs retrieval net of bias controls); main text = Table 2 (headline with the claim-2 panel and prior rows), Table 3 (3a and 3b merged as two arm blocks with net-gain columns), Figure 1 (two panels); Table 1 and Table 4 to the appendix; four contributions (benchmark, headline behavioural numbers with abstention, matched-pair claim-2 test, controlled mitigation) with validity measurement as a methods statement; the URO report may keep the full set. Title: 'LongiDev-CXR: Guess, Abstain or Hallucinate? Device Existence in 2-4B Open VLMs on Same-Patient Chest Radiographs with Bias-Controlled Grounding and Retrieval Mitigation'.
  [minor] step 5: H1's primary test is a between-stratum difference (FAR_B with vs without other labelled lines) inside about 300 B items of one class, i.e. two groups of roughly 150 whose minimum detectable difference at 80% power is about 15 percentage points, not the 8-10 points the plan quotes for paired contrasts.
      evidence: Step 5 method: 'H1 FAR_B(other labelled lines present) minus FAR_B(none) per model, patient-clustered BCa bootstrap'; data_plan: '300 B items per class power the primary-question contrasts for about 8-10 points' (a McNemar paired figure); U is matched to B's stratum mix, so stratum sizes are uncontrolled.
      fix: Make the primary H1 analysis the pooled mixed-effects logistic on B items (other-line count as covariate, class and model as fixed effects, patient and image random effects) across both classes and all four questions; report per-model stratified FAR_B descriptively with Wilson intervals; state the minimum detectable difference in the power memo.
  [minor] step 5: Label-derived RANZCR content does reach the third-party pod despite the compliance sentence: per-item JSONL and directories organised by arm (A/B/U) encode the per-image device label, and the interleaved-image retrieval variant sends per-neighbour labels ('contains an ETT') for pool images.
      evidence: Protocol COMPLIANCE: 'the pod holds NIH pixels and prompt text (which may carry aggregate label-derived counts in the retrieval arm) only'; Step 5 method: 'incremental JSONL by image id: for each run, each image in A/B/U'; Arm 2: 'interleaved images with labels for Qwen3-VL-2B and Gemma 4 E2B'.
      fix: Use opaque item ids and a flat item list on the pod; join arms and labels locally after download; let retrieval prompts carry only the 'm of k' aggregate; for the interleaved variant show neighbour images with the same aggregate sentence, or drop the variant (it is already first on the Arm 2 cut list).
  [minor] step 3: Two generator licences are cited incompletely: stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-inpainting is under CreativeML OpenRAIL-M, whose Attachment A forbids use 'To provide medical advice and medical results interpretation' and states 'No use of the output can contravene any provision as stated in the License'; RadEdit's MSRLA card is silent on redistributing outputs. The plan nonetheless lists edited images as releasable without addressing either.
      evidence: CompVis stable-diffusion LICENSE, Attachment A and Section 6 (fetched 12 Oct 2026); digest med_synthetic [1] and cautions: RadEdit weights MSRLA, 'out-of-scope: any deployed use', output redistribution unaddressed; plan ethics_and_release: 'edited and inserted NIH-derived images if the Kaggle rules ... do not forbid'.
      fix: Add one methods sentence: edited radiographs are research stimuli for evaluating models, never provided to any person as medical interpretation, labelled synthetic and 'not for clinical use'; cite the OpenRAIL-M clause; withhold RadEdit-edited images from any release unless the MSRLA text is read as permitting research redistribution.
  [minor] step 1: H2's primary endpoint rests on CheXOne being 'the exact base ... identical encoder, LLM and chat format' of Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct, but the card's metadata names base_model Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct while its text says 'post-trained on Qwen2.5VL-3B-Instruct', reports '4B params' (the critic measured 3,754,622,976 parameters in safetensors; the plan says 4.07B), and lists no training datasets (the ChestXray14/Object-CXR exposure comes from the paper). The pair also fully fine-tunes the encoder, which the plan should name as the residual confound.
      evidence: CheXOne card (fetched 12 Oct 2026): license cc-by-nc-4.0; base_model Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct; 'post-trained on Qwen2.5VL-3B-Instruct'; '4B params'; no dataset list; critic: 'safetensors total is 3.75B, not 4.07B'; med_models caution: 'CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B fully fine-tunes the encoder'.
      fix: In the Day-3 load test diff config.json (architectures, vision config, tokenizer and chat template) between StanfordAIMI/CheXOne and Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct and hash the vision-tower tensors; report the loaded parameter count; cite arXiv 2604.00493 for the CheXinstruct-v2 sources; phrase H2 as 'CXR post-training of the whole model (encoder included)' rather than 'same encoder'.
  [minor] step 8: The clinician reader's status is undecided (participant vs team member), which changes the IRB paperwork (the consent form is a required IRB document) and the authorship plan; no compensation or co-authorship is offered for about four hours of a resident's time; and the reader sits at IAU/KFHU, outside KFUPM, whose own position is unaddressed.
      evidence: Plan ethics_and_release: 'a clinician reader's ratings, which may make the reader a research participant and is flagged to the IRB'; KFUPM IRB required documents include the 'Informed Consent Statement form'; digest edited-arm fill [12]: IAU runs its own SCRELC/DSR ethics process; the reader session is scheduled for the week of Nov 9 with a Nov 15 kill date.
      fix: Decide in the Day-1 advisor email: by default the reader joins as a team member (NCBE certificate, co-authorship on the ML4H/MIDL paper, named in the IRB application); if the reader prefers participant status, include the consent text and the session description in the IRB request now rather than as a later amendment; ask the reader to confirm with their department that a two-hour reading of public NIH images needs no IAU approval.
  [minor] step 1: The IRB request cites NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Article 10.33 as an exemption for 'publicly available, non-identifying data'; per the official PDF, 10.33 lets the committee exempt such research from periodic follow-up, not from review, while 10.19 governs expedited review. Mis-citing the regulation to the IRB weakens the request.
      evidence: Digest fit_and_ethics [13], read from the official NCBE PDF: 'Article 10.33 lets the committee exempt from periodic follow-up research on previously collected data ... No article exempting public de-identified secondary data from review altogether was found'; plan: '10.33 (publicly available, non-identifying data)'.
      fix: Ask for a determination (out of scope / exempt / expedited); cite 10.19 for expedited review of minimal-risk previously collected non-identifying records and 10.33 only for follow-up relief; quote the datasets' own statements (CLiP: 'IRB approval is waived'; NIH Clinical Center de-identification).
  [minor] step 0: Three 'first' claims are over-broad and one metric is mislabelled: 'the first 2B-generalist device numbers of any kind' (ReXVQA reports Qwen2.5-VL of unstated size and Phi-3.5-vision at 4.2B on tubes/lines; Lotfinia reports MedGemma-1.5-4B on a support-device block); 'measured validity numbers ... which no CXR editing paper reports' (Spatially Gated Diffusion reports a two-reader rubric with kappa and protected-region MAE; Sogancioglu 2018 a 2AFC); 'hallucination-free rate (1 - FAR), the paper's own quantity' (Kim et al.'s rate is Med-HALT resistance on MCQ, NOTA and fake-question tasks).
      evidence: Floor fill [1] ReXVQA Table 3 and [2] Lotfinia's support-device block; edited-arm fill [11]: SGD 'pre-adjudication Cohen's kappa 0.82 ... 82.1% for RadEdit' and Sogancioglu's 2AFC; advisor-references fill [2]-[3]: Kim et al. scoring on Med-HALT tasks.
      fix: Narrow to 'to our knowledge, the first per-class yes/no device-existence numbers at logged visual-token budgets for 2026 2B generalists'; 'no CXR device-removal study reports reader-audited validity against untouched negative controls'; 'an image-grounded analogue of Kim et al.'s hallucination-free rate'.
  [minor] step 1: The Kaggle-rules fallback is named but not designed: if the Rules forbid off-platform use or derivative works, ReXVQA/ReXGradient (gated non-commercial DUA, images at a quarter of DICOM resolution, MCQ format) cannot supply same-patient pairs, polylines, 1024-px pixel matching or the removal/insertion probes, so most of the pre-registered design would not survive; Object-CXR's mirror is an untested Drive link or a torrent of unverified provenance with an informal README licence.
      evidence: data_plan (9): 'the fallback real set if the Kaggle rules forbid the planned use'; floor fill [1]: ReXVQA images 'at 1/4 of original DICOM resolution', gated DUA; RANZCR fill [6] and med_data [2]: Object-CXR provenance unverified, original site 404, Drive link untested, terms 'free to download for scientific research and non-commercial usage' with no derivative clause.
      fix: Write the fallback design now as pre-registered branch (v): ReXGradient device MCQ or Object-CXR foreign objects as the primary class, unpaired A/U with patient clustering, no synthetic probes, the same abstention scoring and Arm 1/2 controls; on Day 0 record the exact Object-CXR source, its SHA-256 and the README terms before any use, and do not treat a torrent as an authorised channel unless the README names it.
  [minor] step 1: Licence drift in the model list: Gemma 4 E2B is Apache-2.0 per the card, but the named fallback google/gemma-3-4b-it (also the appendix MedGemma base) is under the Gemma Terms of Use with manual gating and its own prohibited-use policy, which the plan never lists or requests access to.
      evidence: Digest med_models [26]: 'Gemma Terms of Use; gated=manual'; plan model_set names 'fallback google/gemma-3-4b-it' with no terms; the LICENCES paragraph lists only 'Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5, Gemma 4 Apache-2.0'.
      fix: Add 'google/gemma-3-4b-it: Gemma Terms of Use (gated manual)' to the licence list, request access on Day 0 because manual gating can take days, and cite the terms if the fallback or appendix row runs.
STRENGTHS:
  - The credential-free critical path is genuinely lawful and verified: NIH pixels under attribution-only terms (trio named), RANZCR labels confined to Kaggle and the student's machine with the mapping built inside a Kaggle notebook, CheXmask CC BY 4.0, ChestX-Det Apache-2.0, PhysioNet filed on Day 0 but never on the critical path, and no online LLM API touches any restricted data, which satisfies the Sept 2025 PhysioNet policy by construction.
  - Advisor fit is literal and verified, not inferred: healthcare is the lab's only domain, the component is framed as the first image-grounded instantiation of Alshahrani and Behzad 2026, claim 2 becomes a two-sided matched-pair test (CheXOne vs its base) with MedGemma-vs-generalist correctly labelled size- and encoder-confounded, claim 3 becomes retrieval and grounding arms with the detector-alone, shuffled-expert, mismatched and label-shuffled controls that RULE, MMed-RAG and CCD lack, and the taxonomy vocabulary (Brooks and Anastasio, MediHall, DREAM) is used where each actually applies.
  - The Kim et al. provenance (arXiv 2503.05777v2, 11 text-only LLMs, 350 Med-HALT items, U = 27 of 28, p = 0.012) and all five citation corrections ([45] MedHallBench by Zuo and Jiang; CXR-VisHal is a MedHEval subset without grounding; Med-HallMark 6,341 samples as [4]; DREAM PMCID PMC12866389; Brooks and Anastasio in Radiol AI 2026) match the primary sources in the digest.
  - The edit-free same-patient negative arm is an artefact-free contribution with correct hedging ('within-patient contrast', 'no other labelled line', never 'counterfactual'), and the four-question design yields within-image negatives without any new annotation.
  - The verifier-independence rule is correct and stricter than the digests' own first drafts: ianpan restricted to masks and placement (in-sample on RANZCR plus 31,448 NIH images, no fold file), MedSigLIP and CheXzero as the only verifiers and experts with their AUC validated on held-out labels first, torchxrayvision correctly limited to drift (it has no support-devices head), and the expert-swap rule on shortlisted pacemaker items.
  - Contamination is reported, never claimed absent: the matrix is built from cards and papers, MedGemma's CXR14 status is to be quoted by eye from the card, generalists are marked 'unknown', the general-vs-medical test is label-clean because no model lists RANZCR device labels, and the text-only, A-vs-U and real-minus-synthetic rows are the right sensitivity readouts.
  - Protocol discipline carried over from v1 intact: verbatim Kalai instruction at t = 0.5 and 0.9 scored as Effective Reliability with the t = 0.75 slip footnoted, every mitigation gain net of a yes-ratio-matched control, Wilson intervals with patient-clustered bootstrap and mixed-effects models, one primary test per hypothesis with Holm per family, dev split and hashed lists on OSF, kill dates on every extension, an ordered cut list with a never-cut core, and report v1 complete at the end of Week 4.
  - Model and data terms are enumerated correctly for the panel and tools (HAI-DEF: outputs yours, no clinical use, derivatives carry the terms; MSRLA research-only; Qwen Research License; CC BY-NC(-ND); NVIDIA OneWay non-commercial; MIT for CheXzero, BiomedCLIP and CCD; creativeml-openrail-m noted), synthetic images are labelled 'not for clinical use', no diagnostic claim is made anywhere, non-expert verification is disclosed with kappa, and TRIPOD-LLM and CLAIM 2024 items are planned.
  - The budget logic survives the price correction (A5000 $0.27/h default, 3090 fallback, $30 pod cap, $12-28 expected under the $40 ceiling), and the realistic reader route (advisor-brokered IAU KFHU resident, two-hour blinded web form on NIH images only, two weeks of lead time, Nov 15 kill date) is the right one for a KFUPM undergraduate.
