# PART 2: supplementary tracks workflow (wf_3c6dcabd-073)

######## TRACK A (10-day proposal; superseded, reuse pieces) ########
DELIVERABLE: Submit on Oct 22 a "Research Proposal and Feasibility Report v0" in paper format (the course template if the RES201 instructor supplies one, otherwise an ACL-style body of 8-10 pages plus appendices): title, abstract, introduction with RQ1-RQ4, literature review, the LongiDev-CXR benchmark design, the evaluation protocol with the OSF pre-registration text, a preliminary-results section built from a real pilot (RANZCR-to-NIH mapping and realised-yield table, a one-model floor pilot at two token budgets with MedGemma-1.5-4B as positive control, MedSigLIP/CheXzero device AUC on RANZCR CLiP labels, pod timing and a cost ledger), the Track B schedule and budget, a conditional Track C for cluster training, ethics and data terms, risks, and references. It satisfies a proposal reading because every design choice is specified, pre-registered (H0-H8, decision rule, relaxation ladder) and costed, and a progress-report reading because it reports completed literature sweeps, decisions taken and measured numbers from runs the student executed. RES 201 (Vertically-Integrated Research I, 2 credits; outcomes listed by the KFUPM Deanship of Research as a report, a journal/conference paper or a prototype) is graded on an individual paper-format report due in Week 13 (registrar: classes began Aug 19, 2026, so Week 13 is Nov 8-12), so the Oct 22 document is written so that Sections 1-5 carry unchanged into the final report and Section 6 grows from "preliminary" to "results".

-- document_outline --
  * Front matter: title (digest3 TITLE, shortened to one line), 200-word abstract, keywords; source: digest3 THESIS and HANDOFF Section 1.
  * 1. Introduction and research questions (1 page): the advisor's framework (Alshahrani and Behzad, arXiv 2606.13211, verified) cited first; Kalai et al. (arXiv 2509.04664, verified; cite as arXiv, no Nature evidence found) for guess-rewarding evaluation; RQ1-RQ4 and contributions C1-C5 from digest3 THESIS; HANDOFF wording rules applied ('to our knowledge', 'within-patient contrast').
  * 2. Literature review (2.5-3 pages plus a related-work matrix table): medical digest items med_benchmarks [1]-[18], med_mitigation [1]-[16], FILL floor-effect [1]-[12] and FILL edited-arm [1]-[12]; general digest benchmarks [1]-[18] and the statistics fill; HANDOFF Section 3 precedent list; verified today: Güzel et al. Diagnostics 2026;16(11):1602 (4,813 RANZCR CLiP radiographs, ETT/NGT/CVC/Swan-Ganz, three closed models, PadChest n=200), Lotfinia et al. arXiv 2606.17710 v3, ReXVQA arXiv 2506.04353 Table 3 (MedGemma-4B 83.86 vs Phi-3.5-vision 32.10 on tubes/lines).
  * 3. Benchmark design, LongiDev-CXR (1.5 pages): data sources and terms, the mapping procedure, strata S1-S5, same-patient A/B pairs, context-matched U, prior rows, four questions per image, relaxation ladder, contamination matrix; source: digest3 DATA_PLAN and OBJECT CLASSES, medical digest FILL RANZCR [1]-[12].
  * 4. Evaluation protocol and pre-registration (1.5 pages): prompts P0/P1/P2/PN/BLIND, greedy decoding, token budgets per family, metrics including Phi_c and persistence rate, Wilson intervals and patient-clustered bootstrap, H0-H8 with one primary test each, GO/NO-GO rule; source: digest3 PROTOCOL and GO/NO-GO; general digest statistics fill (Miller 2024, Bowyer 2025).
  * 5. Mitigation plan (0.75 page): Arm 1 detector-gated answers, Arm 2 similar-case retrieval, Arm 3 region crop, each with its yes-ratio-matched controls; Track C training-based options (LoRA-DPO on verified same-patient pairs; RULE EMNLP 2024 and MMed-RAG ICLR 2025 style preference tuning, both MIT code) conditional on cluster access; source: HANDOFF Section 3, medical digest med_mitigation.
  * 6. Feasibility pilot results (1.5 pages; Tables 1-4, Figure 1): mapping statistics, realised-yield table, floor-pilot table with CIs and the decision line, MedGemma positive-control row, detector AUROC table, bf16-vs-fp32 flip count, pod timing and cost ledger; source: this Track A's own runs (per-item JSONL in the supplementary zip).
  * 7. Execution plan, schedule and budget (1 page): Track B weeks (Oct 25 to Nov 22) adapted from digest3 PHASES 2-6, Track C trigger conditions, compute ledger (RunPod RTX A5000 $0.27/h secure or $0.16/h community, verified Oct 12; Kaggle 30 GPU-h/week; GTX 1060 pinned to torch 2.14 cu126, the last Pascal wheels per the PyTorch notice of Sept 4, 2026).
  * 8. Ethics, data terms and compliance (0.5 page): NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Arts 10.19/10.33, the IRB@kfupm.edu.sa request (date sent), NIH attribution trio, RANZCR CLiP under the Kaggle competition rules (clauses quoted from the page), HAI-DEF, CC BY-NC, Apache-2.0 and MIT terms per model; source: digest3 ETHICS_AND_RELEASE, medical digest fit_and_ethics [13]-[14].
  * 9. Risks and cut list (0.5 page): source: the risks_and_fallbacks list of this plan.
  * References (40-60 entries, each URL re-opened in the Oct 20 provenance pass): the two digests' verified URLs plus today's verifications.
  * Appendices: A pre-registration text (H0-H8, decision rule and branches, relaxation ladder, probe gates, kill dates; OSF 'OSF Preregistration' template, embargo up to four years, frozen record with DOI); B mapping and yield tables with SHA-256 of inputs; C contamination matrix with the MedGemma card quotes; D exact prompt strings and parsing regex; E (private, not submitted unless the advisor agrees) the citation-correction memo on arXiv 2606.13211.

-- feasibility_evidence_to_include --
  * RANZCR-to-NIH mapping (Kaggle CPU notebook with the competition data and the nih-chest-xrays/data mirror attached; 3 h person-time, 1-3 CPU-h, $0): PIL .size of all 30,083 JPEGs joined to OriginalImage[Width,Height] in the NIH metadata CSV, multi-candidates resolved with ImageHash 4.3.2 phash/dhash (BSD-2) at 256 px, a 50-200 pair visual spot check, unmatched log; report match rate, method and a releasable NIH-filename-to-pair-id table (UIDs withheld); fallback hash-only pass over all 112,120 NIH PNGs (about 40 CPU-min).
  * Realised-yield table (pandas on the mapped train.csv; 1 h, $0): per class (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz) images present/absent, unique patients, polyline coverage, patients with both states, view-matched pairs, S1-S5 counts, U pool and retrieval pool, printed per relaxation rung; this replaces the descriptor's label totals (50,612 labels on 30,083 images from 3,791 patients) with image and patient counts, which no paper publishes.
  * Floor pilot, model 1 (Kaggle T4, fp32, Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct, Apache-2.0, transformers >= 4.57; 3 h person-time, about 1.5 GPU-h of the 30-h weekly quota, $0): 100 ETT-present and 100 ETT-absent mapped NIH images (one per patient, half of the negatives with another labelled line), ETT and NGT questions per image under P0 at 256 tokens (512 px) and 1,024 tokens (1,024 px; Qwen3-VL compression ratio 32), plus P1 at 1,024 tokens and a text-only blind row; outputs balanced accuracy, hit rate, yes-rate with Wilson 95% CIs (half-width about 0.07 at n=200) and the pre-registered GO/NO-GO verdict.
  * Positive control, precision and timing (RunPod RTX A5000 24 GB bf16, $0.27/h secure or $0.16/h community; 2.5 h person-time, 1.5-2 pod-h, $0.30-0.60): google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (HAI-DEF gated; 896 px to 256 tokens) on the same 200 images and prompts (400-800 items), Qwen3-VL-2B in bf16 on the 800 P0 items to count bf16-vs-fp32 flips, s/item, peak VRAM and tokens per image logged; the pod holds only NIH PNGs and prompt text.
  * Detector AUC on a held-out pool (GTX 1060 fp32 with torch==2.14 cu126; 3-4 h person-time incl. setup, $0; Kaggle T4 as fallback): google/medsiglip-448 zero-shot with the CCD ccd/ccd_utils.py prompt pairs ('a chest x-ray with/without a ...', MIT) and CheXzero (MIT; weights via the README Google Drive link) 'Support Devices' vs 'no Support Devices' at 320 px, scored on >= 1,000 patient-disjoint mapped images per class against RANZCR CLiP labels; AUROC per class with bootstrap CIs; this is the first validation of both on radiologist-labelled device presence (the MedSigLIP card's 0.852 is on CheXpert-test 'Support Devices').
  * Optional model 2 if the T4 quota allows (OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B, Apache-2.0, 2.3B, fp32 about 9.2 GB, 448-px tiles at 256 tokens each; 1 h, $0): the same 200 images at max_num 1 and 6 tiles under P0; reported as a second row or omitted without harm.
  * Compliance and cost ledger (0.5 h, $0): Kaggle rules clauses quoted and screenshotted, HAI-DEF acceptance, MedGemma card section quoted on ChestX-ray14, IRB email date, SHA-256 of every input file and notebook version; Track A cloud spend expected at $1-4 against the $40 ceiling (plus any RunPod minimum credit top-up), with Track B projected from the measured s/item.

-- day_by_day --
  Mon Oct 12 (class day, evening) [1.5]: Email the RES201 instructor (exact due date, what is due, template, rubric) and the advisor (the advisor-questions list below, including cluster access details). | Join the Kaggle competition ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification, accept the rules (rulesRequired per Kaggle's competition endpoint), screenshot the Rules tab and copy the data-access and data-security clauses into PROTOCOL.md; confirm nih-chest-xrays/data (CC0 mirror, 112,120 PNGs) can be attached. | Accept HAI-DEF terms for google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it and google/medsiglip-448; create RunPod (small credit), OSF and GitHub accounts; start the NCBE certificate at ncbeportal.kacst.gov.sa.  => Both emails sent, rules screenshot and quoted clauses saved, HAI-DEF accepted, accounts exist.
  Tue Oct 13 (class day) [3]: Kaggle CPU notebook: read (width,height) of all 30,083 RANZCR JPEGs with PIL .size, join to OriginalImage[Width,Height]; test the dimension-key hypothesis in the first hour; resolve multi-candidates with ImageHash phash/dhash at 256 px; spot-check 50-200 pairs; log unmatched and ambiguous UIDs. | Compute the realised-yield table per class and relaxation rung in pandas; save Table 1 draft and SHA-256 of inputs. | Draw the pilot lists (ETT 100 present / 100 absent, one image per patient, half of the negatives with another labelled line; NGT labels for the same images with 'incompletely imaged' counted present and flagged) and a >= 1,000-image patient-disjoint detector pool per class; export NIH filenames only and a private Kaggle dataset of the 200 PNGs.  => Mapping table with match rate and spot-check log, yield table v0, hashed pilot and pool lists.
  Wed Oct 14 (class day) [3]: Kaggle T4 notebook: Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct in torch.float32 (transformers >= 4.57); 50-item smoke test logging s/item, VRAM and image_grid_thw tokens. | Run 200 images x {ETT, NGT} x {256, 1,024 tokens} under P0 ('Is a {device} present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.'; wording checked against Lotfinia et al. Supplementary Note 1), then P1 ('yes, no, or cannot tell') at 1,024 tokens, then the text-only blind row; greedy, max_new_tokens 16, per-item JSONL. | Score accuracy, balanced accuracy, hit rate, yes-rate with Wilson CIs (statsmodels proportion_confint 'wilson'); apply the GO/NO-GO rule.  => Table 2 draft (model x class x budget x prompt) with CIs and the decision line; JSONL archived.
  Thu Oct 15 (class day) [2.5]: RunPod RTX A5000 bf16 pod: upload only the 200 NIH PNGs and prompts; run MedGemma-1.5-4B (256 tokens) on the ETT and NGT questions under P0 and P1; run Qwen3-VL-2B in bf16 on the 800 P0 items; log s/item, peak VRAM, tokens; stop the pod within 2 h. | Count bf16-vs-fp32 answer flips for Qwen3-VL-2B and record the precision rule (over 1% flips means headline numbers run only in bf16). | Send the IRB determination request to IRB@kfupm.edu.sa citing NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Arts 10.19 and 10.33, the datasets' own IRB statements and the 'no new human data' sentence.  => MedGemma row in Table 2, flip count and timing table, ledger entry ($0.30-0.60), IRB email sent with date recorded.
  Fri Oct 16 (weekend) [4]: GTX 1060 venv pinned to torch==2.14 from the cu126 index; download the detector pool PNGs via the Kaggle API; run MedSigLIP zero-shot with the CCD prompt pairs and CheXzero 'Support Devices' at 320 px; AUROC per class with bootstrap CIs (Kaggle T4 fallback if the venv fails). | Write Sections 1-2 (introduction, literature review) from the two digests and the HANDOFF precedent list; build the related-work matrix.  => Table 3 (detector AUROC per class with CIs) and Sections 1-2 drafted (about 3.5 pages).
  Sat Oct 17 (weekend) [4.5]: Write Sections 3-5 (benchmark design, protocol, mitigation plan) from digest3 DATA_PLAN, PROTOCOL and the HANDOFF mitigation decisions. | Write Appendix A as the OSF pre-registration draft (H0-H8, decision rule and branches, relaxation ladder, N rule, probe gates, kill dates) and Appendix D (exact prompt strings, parsing regex); create the OSF draft under the 'OSF Preregistration' template without submitting.  => Sections 3-5 and Appendices A and D drafted; OSF draft exists.
  Sun Oct 18 (class day) [2]: Apply the decision rule formally (branch i-iv) and write Section 6 (feasibility results: mapping, yield, floor table, MedGemma control, detector AUC, timing, ledger) with Figure 1. | Send the advisor an outline-plus-tables packet with the questions list and the proposed Oct 22 scope.  => Section 6 drafted; advisor packet sent.
  Mon Oct 19 (class day) [1.5]: Write Section 7 (Track B weeks and Track C conditions), Section 8 (ethics and terms) and Section 9 (risks); fold in any instructor or advisor answers. | Compile the whole document as one PDF in the course template.  => Every section exists and the PDF compiles.
  Tue Oct 20 (mid-term break) [3]: Full read-through; number-provenance check (every number traced to a JSONL, table or source URL); re-open every reference URL once. | Apply the wording rules ('RANZCR CLiP labels (43 trained labellers: radiologists, trainees, medical officers)', 'to our knowledge', 'within-patient contrast', 'not for clinical use', no model called 'unseen' without a card citation).  => Draft v0.9 complete and within the page limit.
  Wed Oct 21 (mid-term break) [3]: Apply advisor comments; if the advisor approves, submit the OSF registration (embargoed) and cite its DOI, otherwise label Appendix A 'draft, not yet registered'. | Final formatting, abstract written last, supplementary zip assembled (mapping notebook, per-item JSONL, yield table, prompts).  => v1.0 PDF and supplementary zip ready; OSF status recorded.
  Thu Oct 22 (mid-term break, deadline) [1]: Final read and submission through the channel the instructor named; save the confirmation. | Update HANDOFF.md, STRATEGY.md and memory with the measured numbers, the branch taken and the Track B start plan for Oct 25.  => Submission confirmation saved; HANDOFF updated; total Track A time about 29 h.

-- advisor_questions_before_submission --
  * What exactly is due on Oct 22 (proposal, progress report or full draft), in which template, with which rubric, to whom, and is the Week 13 (Nov 8-12) date the final report?
  * Oct 22 is the Thursday of the registrar's mid-term break (Oct 20-22): is the due date Oct 19 (before the break), Oct 22, or Oct 25 (first class day after it)?
  * Does he endorse the chest X-ray device-existence design, the same-patient negative arm and the CheXOne-versus-Qwen2.5-VL-3B matched-pair test of his paper's claim 2 as a two-sided hypothesis?
  * Cluster or cloud training access: which GPUs, how many hours, from when, and under which data rules (NIH PNGs allowed, PhysioNet never), so Track C (LoRA-DPO on verified pairs; RULE/MMed-RAG-style preference tuning) can be scoped honestly in Section 7.
  * IRB: does the JRCAI umbrella project hold an IRB number the student can be added to, or should the standalone determination request (NCBE v3 Arts 10.19/10.33) stand; NCBE certificate required for every rater.
  * Will he broker one radiology resident or attending (KFHU Khobar or JHAH) for a two-hour blinded web-form reading in the week of Nov 9, given the two-week lead time?
  * Approval of about $30 cloud spend for Track B (Track A uses $1-4).
  * OSF pre-registration: public, embargoed or none, and whose names go on the registration.
  * The citation-correction memo on arXiv 2606.13211 (Kim et al. attribution, reference [45], 'CXR-VisHal', Med-HallMark count, DREAM PMCID): deliver it now with the proposal or in Week 3, and in either case before any co-authored manuscript.
  * Does he accept RANZCR CLiP labels under the Kaggle competition rules (no redistribution of labels, polylines or UIDs) and NIH pixels on a RunPod pod as the compliance design?
  * His reading of the MedGemma 1.5 card: the card page names ChestX-ray14 in its dataset listing while the technical report marks CXR14 as evaluation-only, so which wording may the contamination matrix carry?
  * Venue preference among ML4H 2027 Findings, MIDL 2027 short paper and EMBC 2027 (Jan 24, 2027), and whether a PhysioNet-hosted replication is wanted as a lab asset.

-- risks_and_fallbacks --
  * Dimension key not unique or all JPEG sizes identical: hash all 112,120 NIH PNGs at 256 px (about 40 CPU-min) and match on phash/dhash with a threshold tuned on the spot-checked pairs; report the method actually used.
  * Kaggle rules text forbids the planned use or derived works: keep labels inside Kaggle, release only NIH filenames and scripts, and name the gated ReXVQA/ReXGradient DUA route or ChestX-Det findings as the pre-registered fallback in Section 3.
  * Qwen3-VL-2B sits at the floor on ETT: report it as Result 1 with MedGemma as the positive control and take branch (ii) NGT or (iii) MedGemma-centred core, exactly as pre-registered; the proposal is complete either way.
  * Kaggle GPU queue, quota or fp32 slowness: cut to the 1,024-token budget and P0 only (400 items), or move the pilot to the A5000 pod at $0.27/h.
  * RunPod account, card or availability problems: defer the MedGemma positive control to Track B and submit with the Qwen3-VL-2B fp32 pilot plus detector AUC; say so in Section 6.
  * GTX 1060 software cliff or HAI-DEF gating delays: run MedSigLIP and CheXzero on the Kaggle T4 instead (both are small encoders); if MedSigLIP is unavailable, CheXzero alone with the other row marked 'not run'.
  * Instructor or advisor silent by Oct 16: assume a proposal-grade paper-format document, submit on Oct 22 with a one-paragraph cover note, and keep the Week 13 report as the graded deliverable.
  * IRB answer later than Oct 22: write 'determination pending, request submitted on <date>' and state that Track A involves no new human data.
  * Writing overruns: the literature review is assembled from already-verified digest items, so cap the pilot at mapping, yield and the one-model floor table and mark detector AUC as 'in progress'.
  * MedGemma card ambiguity persists: carry both readings in the contamination matrix, cite the technical report's evaluation-only table, and never call any model 'unseen'.
  * RANZCR label noise (about 15% re-review discrepancy on 5,100 consensus labels): the pilot reports labels as recorded with this caveat; non-expert verification with a blinded second rater and kappa is scheduled in Track B.
  * Unknown contamination of Qwen3-VL-2B: the text-only blind row in the pilot checks label leakage through text at zero cost; pixel-level exposure is bounded by the matrix, not removed.
  * Güzel et al. device-absent count: the HANDOFF records 'only 3 device-absent cases', which the abstract does not state; cite only the verified abstract facts until the full text is read.

-- what_is_deferred_to_track_b --
  * The full six-run panel in bf16 on the pod (InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B, CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B, medgemma-4b-it alongside the two Track A models) and the three-budget resolution sweep per class; Gemma 4 E2B (5.12B parameters) does not fit a T4 in fp32 and fp16 is unsafe for the Gemma family, so it never runs in Track A.
  * The core item set: same-patient A/B pairs, context-matched U negatives, four questions per image, the 40-patient dev split, non-expert verification with a blinded second rater (Cohen's kappa target >= 0.8, PABAK, adjudication) and the hashed lists registered as an OSF amendment.
  * The 35-pair discordance pilot and the McNemar power memo that fixes N (agent-computed reference points such as 940 pairs for a 5-point difference at discordance 0.30; re-run with the measured discordance).
  * The lmms-eval task cxr_device_pope (lmms-eval ships MIT core, Apache-2.0 tasks and models, the pope yaml family and qwen3_vl, qwen2_5_vl, gemma3, gemma4 and internvl3_5 wrappers, no MedGemma entry) and the 99% argmax agreement check against the HF runner; the SLAKE closed X-ray anchor.
  * Penalised abstention scoring: the verbatim Kalai confidence-target prompt at t = 0.5 and 0.9 scored as Effective Reliability, PN negated prompts, BLIND-demo and BLIND-grey rows, and the first-token logit risk-coverage curves.
  * Mitigation Arms 1-3 with their yes-ratio-matched controls (detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias sweep, OLM; kNN-vote-alone, mismatched and label-shuffled retrieval; wrong-region and oracle crops) and the MedSigLIP/BiomedCLIP retrieval index over the patient-disjoint pool.
  * Synthetic probes: the 40-image three-remover removal pilot (LaMa via IOPaint, Apache-2.0, archived Aug 2025 but installable; SD-1.5 inpainting; RadEdit on the pod), the 100-image cut-paste insertion set, the pre-registered validity gate with two independent verifiers, protected-region error, the 2AFC block and the two-reader rubric (kill dates Nov 1 and Nov 3).
  * Reader session logistics, NCBE certificates for every rater and the clinician-as-participant decision.
  * Release engineering: GitHub repository and HF dataset card with NIH filenames, pair ids, masks, task yaml, PROTOCOL.md and the rebuild script that joins RANZCR train.csv after rule acceptance; the awesome-list pull request; venue submissions (EACL SRW mentorship Nov 6 only if v1 exists; EMBC Jan 24, 2027; MIDL short; ML4H 2027 Findings).
  * Track C (only if cluster access and its data rules are confirmed by the advisor): LoRA-DPO on verified same-patient pairs and RULE/MMed-RAG-style preference tuning on 2-4B models, which requires the Track B item set first and is costed once the GPU type and hours are known.
  * PhysioNet credentialing (MIMIC-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, VinDr-CXR) as background only, never on the critical path; the hero resolution 2x2, the hidden-state LLM-Check score and the open-ended arm as kill-dated extensions.


######## TRACK C (training-based mitigation) ########
RATIONALE: Track B's arms are inference-time and cannot change a model's co-occurrence prior or teach it to abstain; the benchmark's own stimuli (same-patient A/B pairs, context-matched U negatives, gated edited and inserted probes, RANZCR-label targets) are exactly the label-grounded preference and reward data that 2025-2026 alignment papers (OPA-DPO, HII-DPO, SENTINEL, Antidote, FINER, MMedPO, Reinforced Hesitation) build synthetically on natural images, so a training track turns the title's "synthetic and real visual data" into a measured transfer (train on synthetic negatives, test on real held-out patients) rather than a stimulus only. It can claim three things no training-free arm can: (1) a change in the prior itself (FAR_B on the other-lines stratum of held-out patients at matched hit_A), (2) penalty-trained abstention whose rate tracks evidence (up on blind rows and edited probes, down on A) with the training penalty lambda equal to the evaluation penalty c = t/(1-t), which is the only tool that closes the representation-action gap if RQ2 finds informative logits beside zero verbal abstention, and (3) a first 2B-generalist head-to-head of label-grounded tuning on about 3k pairs against a 14.7M-sample CXR specialist (CheXOne) on device existence. Every gain is reported net of the base model's constant-bias curve and a label-shuffled training control, so a null is still a finding; tuned models are a separate table because tuning on RANZCR-derived targets removes the label-clean property that H2 relies on.

[1] C1 PairDPO-CXR: on-policy LoRA-DPO on same-patient device pairs (OPA-DPO ordering)
  idea: Direct preference optimisation on the benchmark's own yes/no items: chosen = the RANZCR-label answer, rejected = the base model's own wrong answer sampled on-policy (OPA-DPO's finding that off-policy corrections are ignored under the KL constraint), with the A and B studies of one patient in the same batch so the contrast is within-patient. A second column adds the gated synthetic probes as training negatives/positives to measure synthetic-to-real transfer.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0, HF card verified) as the hero policy; Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (Qwen Research License: non-commercial only, derivatives must display 'Built with Qwen', redistribution carries the agreement and a Notice file; HF API license 'qwen-research') as the second policy because it is CheXOne's exact base; google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (HAI-DEF terms; fine-tuning permitted; Model Derivatives must pass on the use restrictions and the Notice file) appendix only; StanfordAIMI/CheXOne (cc-by-nc-4.0 on the card, CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 in the paper) is never tuned.
  data_construction: Training pool = mapped RANZCR train images from patients outside the OSF-registered test set and the 40-patient dev split (expected over 10,000 images), one A/B pair per patient where both states exist plus U-type negatives, four questions per image keyed to the RANZCR columns, 3,000-6,000 pairs. Chosen/rejected: P0 (and P1/P2 variants) prompt on the 1024-px NIH PNG; rejected = a wrong answer sampled from the base model at temperature 1 (template wrong answer when the model is right); ambiguous items flagged by the student are excluded. Synthetic column: edited (absent-by-edit) and inserted (present-by-insertion) images built only on training-pool hosts after the Track-B gates pass. Contamination rules: splits are patient-disjoint by NIH Patient ID; no edited or inserted image whose host patient is in test or dev; no test-set output inspected before the tuned model is frozen; the training node holds NIH PNGs and a JSONL of (NIH filename, question, chosen, rejected) only, never train.csv rows, polyline coordinates or StudyInstanceUIDs.
  code_and_licence: TRL DPOTrainer, https://github.com/huggingface/trl (Apache-2.0; VLM training with an image column, PEFT LoRA, precompute_ref_log_probs, docs example model Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct, max_length=None required so image tokens are not truncated). OPA-DPO, https://github.com/zhyang2226/OPA-DPO (MIT; LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B only, about 4,800 samples, 4xA100-80GB, OPA step about 30 min) reused as the recipe, not the code. ms-swift, https://github.com/modelscope/ms-swift (Apache-2.0; lists Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5, Gemma4; DPO/GRPO/ORPO/SimPO/KTO for MLLMs) as the fallback trainer.
  compute_estimate: Estimate, not measured: 6k pairs x 2 sequences x 3 epochs at about 1.1k tokens (1,024 visual tokens), LoRA rank 64, gradient checkpointing: about 2-4 h on an A100 80GB, 3-5 h on an RTX 4090, 5-8 h on an RTX A5000, plus about 5 h to re-score the test battery (about 18k items at about 1 s) and 1 h for the shuffled-label control. RunPod official page (updated Sept 27, 2026): A5000 $0.27/h, 3090 $0.50/h, 4090 $0.89/h, A100 80GB $1.79/h, H100 PCIe $2.89/h, H100 SXM $3.99/h; community/third-party quotes 4090 $0.34, A100 $1.19-1.39, H100 $1.99-2.69; Lambda A100 80GB $2.79, H100 $3.29-4.29. Total per policy about $3-6 on A5000/4090-community, $15-25 on an A100 at the official rate, $0 on a university cluster. A single 24 GB bf16 GPU suffices for the 2B policy (batch 1-2) and is tight for 3B; 48-80 GB allows batch 8.
  evaluation: Same HF runner and cxr_device_pope task; P0/P1/P2-hi (plus P2-lo, PN, BLIND-text/demo/grey) on A/B/U and gated probes of held-out patients; hit_A, FAR_B stratified by other labelled lines, FAR_U, yes-ratio, balanced accuracy, abstention, Phi_c, risk-coverage from cached logits, Wilson intervals and patient-clustered bootstrap. Real gain = the tuned model lies above the base model's constant-bias curve at matched yes-ratio AND above the label-shuffled-training control, with the BLIND-row yes-rate not increased; a point on the curve is a bias shift. Pre-registered primary endpoint: paired difference in FAR_B at matched hit_A on the primary class; secondary: cross-class transfer (train ETT, test NGT and CVC), real-only versus real-plus-synthetic training, and untuned CheXOne versus tuned Qwen2.5-VL-3B.
  novelty_vs_2026: OPA-DPO (CVPR 2025) trains LLaVA on GPT-4V-revised captions; MMedPO (ICML 2025; code github.com/aiming-lab/MMedPO, Apache-2.0) injects GPT-4o hallucinations and lesion-noised images for LLaVA-Med 7B; CheXPO (arXiv 2507.06959, Jul 2025) mines hard examples by token confidence with counterfactual rationales; FiRe-MPO (arXiv 2606.12590, Jun 2026, revised Oct 2026) uses minimally edited spans plus corrupted images for one medical and one general LVLM. None uses device existence, same-patient present/absent pairs as the preference contrast, on-policy rejected answers without a stronger model, a 2B generalist, or penalised abstention scoring.
  risk: With a three-way answer space, DPO on label targets is close to supervised training on ICU priors, so the gain may be memorised co-occurrence statistics rather than perception; held-out patients, cross-class transfer, BLIND rows and the shuffled-label control are the guards. With about 300 test pairs per class, effects below about 5-8 points are undetectable, so the minimal detectable effect must be pre-registered.

[2] C2 Reinforced Hesitation-CXR: ternary-reward GRPO for image-grounded abstention
  idea: Apply the +1 / 0 / -lambda reward of arXiv 2511.11500 to P2-style device questions: the policy answers yes, no or 'I don't know'; the reward comes from RANZCR labels on the training pool; lambda in {1, 3, 9} equals the evaluation penalty c = t/(1-t) at t = 0.5, 0.75, 0.9, with lambda = 0 as the accuracy-only control. The claims are the Pareto property (each model is best under its own Phi_c) and that abstention tracks visual evidence.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0; TRL's GRPO page lists 'Qwen3-VL, e.g. Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct' and its tested list names Gemma3, LLaVA-NeXT, Qwen2-VL, Qwen2.5-VL, SmolVLM2); google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (HAI-DEF; Gemma3 architecture; google-health/medgemma ships reinforcement_learning_with_hugging_face.ipynb and fine_tune_with_hugging_face.ipynb, verified by the repository file listing) as the medical policy; CheXOne never tuned.
  data_construction: Prompts = the verbatim Kalai instruction at the matching t plus the question over training-pool A/B/U images (3-6k prompts, patient-disjoint from test and dev, four questions per image). Reward function: regex parse of the completion, +1 correct, 0 'I don't know', -lambda wrong, -0.5 lambda for a format violation (as in the paper); student-flagged ambiguous items are dropped rather than given a target. Synthetic column: gated edited and inserted training-pool images with by-construction labels. Same patient-disjoint and no-redistribution rules as C1; labels exist on the training node only inside the reward table keyed by NIH filename.
  code_and_licence: TRL GRPOTrainer (Apache-2.0): custom reward_func(completions, ground_truth, **kwargs) returning a list of floats, PEFT LoRA, vLLM colocate or server mode or transformers continuous batching, loss_type='dr_grpo' and scale_rewards='none' available (Dr.GRPO as used by the paper), vllm_enable_sleep_mode for small cards. verl, https://github.com/volcengine/verl (Apache-2.0; the paper's framework; Qwen2.5-VL multimodal example) for a cluster. ms-swift GRPO (Apache-2.0) as fallback. Reinforced Hesitation: no code release found; recipe from the paper (Dr.GRPO, 8 rollouts, 256 prompts per step, lr 2e-6, KL disabled, 312 steps on 4 H100 for Qwen3-1.7B on 80k Knights-and-Knaves puzzles).
  compute_estimate: Estimate: completions are at most 16 tokens, so cost is prefill-dominated; 4k prompts x 4-8 rollouts x 2 epochs plus policy forward/backward at about 1.1k tokens is about 4-8 h per lambda on an A100 80GB (2-4 h on an H100), 12-30 h for four lambda values: about $20-55 on a RunPod A100 at $1.79/h, $15-40 at community rates, $50-120 on an H100; add about 5 h scoring per lambda. A 24 GB card runs the 2B policy with vLLM at 0.3 memory utilisation or sleep mode but 2-3x slower; MedGemma 4B needs 48-80 GB. This is the most expensive recipe; a cluster makes it free but queue-bound.
  evaluation: Primary endpoint: Phi_c at c = lambda on held-out A/B/U with coverage and risk, against the base model under the same P2 prompt and against the lambda = 0 model, patient-clustered bootstrap. Real gain = higher Phi_c with abstention concentrated where evidence is weak (abstention higher on BLIND rows and edited probes than on A, lower FAR_B at equal coverage, risk-coverage curve above the base model's logit curve); bias shift = abstention rising uniformly, or Phi_c gains that vanish at matched yes-ratio. Pareto test: the model-lambda x evaluation-c matrix; the paper's cascade is not attempted.
  novelty_vs_2026: Reinforced Hesitation (arXiv 2511.11500, Nov 2025) is text-only (Qwen3-1.7B on logic puzzles; frontier models on GSM8K/MedQA/GPQA); SAVOR (arXiv 2609.16601, Sep 2026) adds calibration and abstention reward terms to GRPO on InternVL3-8B and Qwen3-VL-8B for POPE/AMBER/HallusionBench, no medical data, no code found; DualRead (arXiv 2609.06419, Sep 2026) freezes a GRPO-trained medical actor and reads confidence from its states, no penalty-trained abstention, no code found; CheXPO-v2 (arXiv 2512.17213; code github.com/ecoxial2007/CheX-Phi4MM) uses verifiable RL with a knowledge-graph reward on MIMIC-CXR-VQA without abstention. Delta: first image-grounded ternary-reward training on radiograph device existence with lambda matched to the evaluation penalty, scored on edit-free same-patient negatives, blind rows and synthetic probes.
  risk: Reward hacking into constant abstention at high lambda or constant 'yes' at lambda = 0; the three-way output gives low rollout diversity so group advantages collapse (use 4 rollouts, temperature 1, entropy logging, mask truncated completions); TRL has open Qwen3-VL issues (#4559 vLLM stall, #4582 multi-image), so pin versions and smoke-test 50 prompts before renting hours.

[3] C3 SENTINEL-CXR: detector-consensus sentence-level preference data for open-ended device descriptions, plus a zero-training transfer row
  idea: For the gated open-ended arm ('Describe the support devices in this chest X-ray'), sample the model's descriptions, verify each device sentence with the two independent verifiers allowed by PROTOCOL.md (MedSigLIP zero-shot device prompts, CheXzero 'support devices'), label sentences hallucinated or not, and build context-aware pairs with shared prefixes as SENTINEL does. Before any training, evaluate SENTINEL's released Qwen2-VL-2B LoRA against its base on the yes/no items as a free 'does COCO alignment transfer to radiographs?' row.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0) for training; Qwen/Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0) with the Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct-SENTINEL LoRA (Apache-2.0; trained on 12K samples) for the zero-training row; both run in bf16 on the pod.
  data_construction: 1,500-2,000 training-pool images (patient-disjoint), 8-10 sampled descriptions each at 128 tokens; device claims parsed by regex onto the four classes; a claim counts as supported only if the RANZCR label says present and both verifiers agree (labels arbitrate, verifiers filter wording); chosen = coherent non-hallucinated continuation, rejected = the first hallucinated sentence under the same prefix; 7-12k pairs as in SENTINEL. The ianpan model is never a verifier (in-sample on RANZCR). Same compliance rules as C1.
  code_and_licence: SENTINEL, https://github.com/pspdada/SENTINEL (Apache-2.0; code, data and LoRA weights released for LLaVA-1.5/1.6, Qwen2-VL-2B/7B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B; detector consensus is GroundingDINO plus YOLO-World in the paper, replaced here by MedSigLIP (HAI-DEF, gated) and CheXzero (MIT)); TRL DPOTrainer for the Qwen3-VL port; the Track-B rule classifier for scoring.
  compute_estimate: Sampling 2,000 x 10 descriptions at 128 tokens about 5-8 h on an A5000 (about $2) or 2-3 h on a 4090/A100; verifier passes free on the GTX 1060; LoRA-DPO on 10k sentence pairs about 2-4 h on a 24 GB card; scoring the open-ended arm about 1-2 h. Total about $4-10 on A5000/4090, $15-30 on an A100 at the official rate; 24 GB suffices. The zero-training row costs about 1 h of pod time.
  evaluation: Open-ended arm metrics from the protocol (TAR beside FAR_open on A versus B, rule classifier validated on 200 hand labels) plus the full yes/no battery to check for regression; controls: shuffled-verifier pairs and a length-matched control, because sentence-level tuning can simply shorten descriptions (the 'Less is More' EOS effect); real gain = fewer device mentions on B at an unchanged mention rate on A.
  novelty_vs_2026: SENTINEL (ICCV 2025) relies on open-vocabulary detectors on natural images; no tube/line open-vocabulary detector exists, so the consensus must come from CXR classifiers, and no paper applies sentence-level early intervention to radiographs; the transfer row (a COCO-aligned LoRA scored on CXR device items) has no precedent in either digest.
  risk: Depends on the open-ended arm, itself a kill-dated extension; 2B descriptions of radiographs may be too short or templated to yield 7k informative pairs; verifier AUC on NGT and CVC may be too low (the Step-2 AUC table decides).

[4] C4 Antidote-CXR: synthetic counterfactual-presupposition questions with factual-prior self-correction
  idea: Generate questions that presuppose a device the image does not contain ('Where is the tip of the endotracheal tube?', 'Is the nasogastric tube correctly positioned?' on B and U images) alongside true-premise questions on A images; the chosen answer is the base model's own answer after a factual prior from the label is prepended ('There is no endotracheal tube in this radiograph'), the rejected answer is the plain answer that accepts the presupposition; DPO without any stronger model, as Antidote does.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0); optional Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (Qwen Research License).
  data_construction: Training-pool B and U images (device absent per label) x 3-5 presupposition templates per class, balanced with A images and matched true-premise templates so 'questions about tips imply absence' cannot be learned; priors come only from RANZCR labels, never from detectors; chosen and rejected generated by the base model with and without the prior; 2-4k pairs; held-out templates for testing; same patient-disjoint and no-redistribution rules.
  code_and_licence: Antidote, https://github.com/Wu0409/Antidote (CVPR 2025, arXiv 2504.20468; GitHub API licence field null, i.e. no licence, so reference only; the README says the code was refactored onto the SWIFT framework); implement with ms-swift DPO (Apache-2.0) or TRL DPOTrainer (Apache-2.0).
  compute_estimate: Pair generation 4k x 2 passes about 1-2 h; LoRA-DPO about 1-3 h on a 24 GB card; scoring the PN/P0/P1/P2 battery about 5 h. About $2-5 on A5000/4090, $10-20 on an A100 at the official rate; 24 GB suffices.
  evaluation: Primary endpoint: the PN acquiescence shift and FAR_B/FAR_U under P0 on held-out patients; controls: the constant-bias curve, shuffled-prior pairs (random prior text) and a MedVH-style false-premise accuracy row for comparability; a model that merely says 'no device' more often is a bias shift, so hit_A must hold.
  novelty_vs_2026: Antidote (CVPR 2025) builds CP-Bench and synthetic data on natural images for the LLaVA series (CP-Bench +50%, POPE +1.8-3.3%); MedVH's clinically incorrect questions and CXR-ContraBench's negated options only evaluate; no paper trains a CXR model against device presuppositions with radiologist-label priors.
  risk: Template leakage (the model learns the question family, not the image); mitigated by balanced true-premise items and held-out templates, but a small battery limits power.

[5] C5 HII-DPO-CXR: counterfactual-image preference data from verified device removal and insertion
  idea: Treat the gated LaMa/SD/RadEdit removals and cut-paste insertions built on training-pool hosts as hallucination-inducing images: keep an edited image only if the base model still answers 'yes' in at least 5 of 10 samples (HII-DPO's filter), build pairs (chosen 'No' / rejected 'Yes' on edited; chosen 'Yes' / rejected 'No' on inserted; shared-prefix sentence pairs for descriptions) and measure transfer to real B and U negatives.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0); HII-DPO itself trained LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B, Qwen2-VL-2B/7B and Qwen2.5-VL-7B.
  data_construction: Requires the Week-3 gate (two-verifier pass, protected-region error, 2AFC); edits only on training-pool images, with RANZCR polylines rasterised locally into masks (coordinate files never leave the student's machine); 300-1,000 edited plus 300-1,000 inserted training images; HII filter; 1-3k pairs; synthetic training images are labelled synthetic and 'not for clinical use' and are never released; patient-disjoint hosts.
  code_and_licence: HII-DPO (arXiv 2602.10425, Feb 2026): no code found (the Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination list marks it 'soon'); recipe = GroundingDINO-guided iterative masking, the 5-of-10 filter and shared-prefix sentence pairs, re-implemented with the Track-B edit pipeline (IOPaint/LaMa Apache-2.0; stable-diffusion-v1-5 inpainting creativeml-openrail-m; microsoft/radedit MSRLA weights with an MIT pipeline) and TRL DPOTrainer (Apache-2.0).
  compute_estimate: Filtering 2,000 edits x 10 samples at 16 tokens about 1-2 h on an A5000; LoRA-DPO 1-3 h; scoring about 5 h; about $2-5 on A5000/4090, $10-20 on an A100 at the official rate; the edits themselves run on the GTX 1060 and CPU; 24 GB suffices.
  evaluation: The endpoint is transfer: FAR_B and FAR_U on real held-out negatives after training only on synthetic negatives (the title's claim), beside FAR_edited on held-out edited images; controls: C1 trained on an equal number of real B negatives, the shuffled-label control and the constant-bias curve; FAR_edited falling while FAR_B does not means the model learned the inpainting trace, reported as a negative result.
  novelty_vs_2026: HII-DPO masks objects without inpainting on Visual Genome; Xiong et al. 2026 (arXiv 2605.20158) and RadCF (arXiv 2609.24879) build CXR counterfactuals for evaluation only; no paper trains on validity-gated device removals and measures real-image transfer.
  risk: Entirely gated on the removal and insertion validity gates; if LaMa leaves traces the model learns the trace, which the FAR_edited-versus-FAR_B gap exposes but which turns the arm into a negative result.

[6] C6 RULE/MMed-RAG-style retrieval-reliability preference tuning
  idea: Take Arm 2's over-reliance and copy cases (the model flips to a wrong retrieved kNN label) and tune it to weigh retrieved similar-case labels against pixels: chosen = the label-correct answer under mismatched or label-shuffled retrieval, rejected = the copied-context answer; include pairs where correct retrieval was ignored so the model does not learn to discard retrieval.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0).
  data_construction: Retrieval prompts built from the patient-disjoint retrieval pool (MedSigLIP or BiomedCLIP kNN, k in {1, 3, 5}) on training-pool queries; relevant, mismatched (opposite-label) and shuffled conditions mixed 1:1:1; rejected answers sampled on-policy; 2-4k pairs; prompts carry only aggregate label-derived counts ('2 of 3 similar cases: ETT present'), which the compliance sentence already allows.
  code_and_licence: RULE, https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/RULE (MIT; EMNLP 2024) and MMed-RAG, https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/MMed-RAG (MIT; ICLR 2025; LLaVA-Med-1.5-mistral-7b; train_dpo_2stages.py) as recipes; their MIMIC-CXR and IU-Xray corpora are not downloaded; TRL DPOTrainer (Apache-2.0) for the port.
  compute_estimate: Pair generation 4k x 3 conditions about 2-3 h; LoRA-DPO 1-3 h; scoring the retrieval battery (3 conditions x 3 prompts) about 6-8 h; about $3-7 on A5000/4090, $15-30 on an A100 at the official rate; 24 GB suffices.
  evaluation: Endpoints from the Arm 2 table on held-out patients: net gain of relevant retrieval over greedy, MMed-RAG's over-reliance rate, copy rate and mismatched-retrieval harm from When Retrieval Hurts; real gain = lower harm under mismatched retrieval with the relevant-retrieval gain preserved; a model that ignores retrieval altogether (gain and harm both vanish) is reported as such, not as a gain.
  novelty_vs_2026: RULE and MMed-RAG preference-tune a 7B medical model with report retrieval and report gains without mismatched controls; When Retrieval Hurts (arXiv 2609.31733) and MC-CXR (arXiv 2608.24118) only measure harm; no 2B result and no label-kNN variant exists.
  risk: Only meaningful if Arm 2 shows measurable over-reliance (pre-register a threshold such as at least 10% of correct answers flipped); otherwise skipped.

[7] C7 FINER-Tuning-CXR: minimally edited device contradictions
  idea: Preference pairs that differ in exactly one semantic component of a device statement (type: ETT versus NGT versus CVC; side of a CVC; 'tip visible' versus 'incompletely imaged'), derived from RANZCR columns and polyline geometry, then DPO to reduce hallucination under fine-grained negative queries, as FINER-Tuning does for objects, attributes and relations.
  base_models: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0); FINER tuned InternVL3.5-14B and Qwen2.5-VL.
  data_construction: Attributes derived locally: device type from the columns, CVC side from the polyline's position relative to the CheXmask midline, NGT 'incompletely imaged' from its column; chosen = true statement, rejected = single-component edit; training pool only; 2-4k pairs; attribute labels are the student's derivations and are disclosed as such; polylines never leave the student's machine.
  code_and_licence: FINER, https://github.com/ExplainableML/finer (CVPR 2026 oral; the project page states CC BY-SA 4.0 while the GitHub API licence field is null, so confirm before reuse); TRL DPOTrainer (Apache-2.0).
  compute_estimate: 1-3 h LoRA-DPO plus about 5 h scoring; about $2-5 on A5000/4090, $10-20 on an A100 at the official rate; 24 GB suffices.
  evaluation: A fine-grained negative-query battery (side- and type-swapped questions) on held-out patients scored with the same yes/no metrics and the constant-bias control, plus no regression on the core battery; a real gain is lower false 'yes' on swapped attributes at unchanged hit_A on true attributes.
  novelty_vs_2026: FINER (CVPR 2026) is natural-image; GEMeX-VQA and ReXErr-v1 supply device-error templates for evaluation only; no CXR fine-grained device DPO exists.
  risk: Side and type attributes from polylines carry student-derived label noise and the battery is small, so power is weak; lowest priority.

-- recommended_order --
  * C0 (no training, Track B Weeks 4-5): score SENTINEL's Qwen2-VL-2B LoRA against its base on the frozen items as the COCO-to-CXR transfer row; about 1 pod-hour.
  * C1 PairDPO on Qwen3-VL-2B with real pairs only, with the label-shuffled control in the same session; it builds the one TRL pipeline every later recipe reuses.
  * C2 Reinforced Hesitation GRPO with lambda in {0, 1, 3, 9} on Qwen3-VL-2B (MedGemma 1.5 4B only if 48-80 GB is available); this carries the abstention claim.
  * C5 synthetic-negative column added to C1, only if the Week-3 removal/insertion gates passed.
  * C4 presupposition column (cheap, same pipeline).
  * C6 retrieval-reliability tuning, only if Arm 2 over-reliance is at or above the pre-registered threshold.
  * C3 detector-consensus tuning, only if the open-ended arm ran; C7 last and optional.
  * Timing: nothing in Track C starts before Table 2 exists (Week 4); realistic window is Weeks 5-8 or post-course with cluster access; the Oct 22 proposal presents Track C as a pre-registered extension with kill dates and no numbers; report v1 is never displaced by it.

-- prerequisites_from_track_b --
  * OSF-registered item set with patient-disjoint test, dev (40 patients) and training-pool splits keyed by NIH Patient ID, built on the RANZCR-to-NIH mapping and realised-yield table; the training-pool list hashed before any tuning.
  * Baseline Table 2 (six runs x A/B/U x P0/P1/P2) with cached first-token logits and the constant-bias curve per model; without it 'gain' is undefined.
  * Verified probes: gate results for removal and insertion on training-pool hosts (two-verifier pass, protected-region error, 2AFC), or a documented gate failure that drops C5.
  * The HF runner and cxr_device_pope lmms-eval task with the 99% agreement check, extended to load PEFT adapters or merged weights so tuned models are scored identically.
  * Detector AUC table for MedSigLIP and CheXzero on the held-out pool (the only permitted verifiers for C3 and the gate calibration).
  * Retrieval index and Arm 2 results with over-reliance and copy rates (the gate for C6).
  * The Kaggle rules text read, screenshotted and quoted into PROTOCOL.md (decides whether adapters trained on RANZCR-derived targets may be released) and the MedGemma card check (decides whether MedGemma is tuned).
  * A 30-minute smoke test on the A5000 or the cluster measuring seconds per step for DPO and GRPO at 1,024 visual tokens, replacing every hour estimate above before any GPU hours are bought.
  * An OSF amendment registering the Track C hypotheses (C1 prior change, C2 Pareto and evidence-tracking abstention, C5 synthetic-to-real transfer), their minimal detectable effects and kill dates before any tuned-model output is inspected.
  * Pinned TRL, PEFT and transformers versions per model family, with ms-swift tested as the fallback trainer; a cluster account or a cloud account with a stated budget approved by the advisor.

-- ethics_and_licence_notes --
  * NIH ChestX-ray14 pixels carry 'no restrictions' beyond the attribution trio (Box link, Wang et al. CVPR 2017, NIH Clinical Center); training on them is allowed and the trio goes into any adapter card.
  * RANZCR-derived training targets: used only under the Kaggle competition rules accepted on the student's own account; the clauses recalled by earlier agents (academic research and non-commercial use; no redistribution of competition data) are unverified because the rules page is client-rendered, so every statement here is conditional. The training node holds NIH PNGs and derived JSONL targets or reward tables keyed by NIH filename, never train.csv rows, polyline coordinates or StudyInstanceUIDs; polylines are rasterised into masks locally. Prefer a university cluster or secure cloud over community pods for any node that holds label-derived targets until the Data Security clause has been read.
  * LoRA adapters trained on RANZCR-derived targets are derived works; their public release is deferred until the rules are read, and the default release is code, NIH-filename item lists and scripts only. If release is permitted: Qwen3-VL-2B adapters under Apache-2.0; Qwen2.5-VL-3B adapters must display 'Built with Qwen' and carry the Qwen Research License agreement and Notice file (non-commercial only); MedGemma derivatives must carry the HAI-DEF Notice and pass on the use restrictions, with nothing that could make Google a medical-device 'manufacturer'; CheXOne is CC BY-NC-4.0 on the card and CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 in the paper, so it is never fine-tuned (ND) and only evaluated.
  * No PhysioNet data anywhere in Track C: RULE, MMed-RAG, MMedPO and CheXPO code may be reused as recipes, but their MIMIC-CXR and IU-Xray corpora are not downloaded and no MIMIC-derived artefact is produced.
  * Code licences: TRL, verl, ms-swift, SENTINEL, MMedPO and HA-DPO are Apache-2.0; OPA-DPO, RULE and MMed-RAG are MIT; CheXPO-v2 code is stated CC-BY 4.0; Antidote and FINER repositories show no licence field on the GitHub API (treat as all rights reserved, reference only; FINER's project page claims CC BY-SA 4.0); HII-DPO, SAVOR, DualRead and Reinforced Hesitation have no released code, so they are re-implemented from the papers.
  * ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes (research-only weights, in-sample on RANZCR) is never a training target, reward source or verifier.
  * Synthetic training images are labelled synthetic in filenames and metadata, marked 'not for clinical use', audited with the DREAM definition and never released; on-policy preference data (the model's own outputs) are not redistributed as training data, consistent with the Track B release rule.
  * IRB: the determination request to IRB@kfupm.edu.sa (NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Art. 10.19, 10.33) should state that the same public de-identified images will also be used to train models; no new human data are involved; if a determination is issued for evaluation only, a one-paragraph amendment is sent before tuning starts.
  * No online LLM APIs are needed (regex rewards and label targets), consistent with the no-API policy; every tuned model is 'not for clinical use' and is reported under TRIPOD-LLM and CLAIM 2024 items with its training data, licences and contamination status.

-- cautions --
  * Every GPU-hour figure is an estimate derived from token counts, not a measurement; the smoke test replaces them. Prices are RunPod's official page (updated Sept 27, 2026: A5000 $0.27, 3090 $0.50, 4090 $0.89, A100 80GB $1.79, H100 SXM $3.99) versus community and third-party quotes (4090 $0.34, A100 $1.19-1.39, H100 $1.99-2.69) and Lambda (A100 80GB $2.79, H100 $3.29-4.29); they move monthly and the console price governs.
  * Tuning on RANZCR-derived targets removes the label-clean property: H2 (general versus medical) must be tested on untuned models only, and a tuned 2B model that beats untuned CheXOne on devices is a label-exposure result, which the report must say.
  * With about 300 test pairs per class, effects below about 5-8 points are undetectable; pre-register the minimal detectable effect and report patient-clustered intervals.
  * Any tuned model's gain must be reported net of the base model's constant-bias curve at matched yes-ratio, the lambda = 0 or label-shuffled training control and the BLIND rows; a point on the curve is a bias shift, not mitigation.
  * TRL VLM support is uneven: Qwen3-VL is listed for GRPO but issues #4559 (vLLM stall) and #4582 (multi-image) are open; DPO needs max_length=None to keep image tokens; pin versions and keep ms-swift (which lists Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5 and Gemma4) as the tested fallback.
  * MedGemma must be trained in bf16 (the 8-bit gibberish issue) and needs 48-80 GB for GRPO; its ChestX-ray14 training status reads differently across automated reads of the card (one read lists it under training data, the tech report marks it evaluation-only), so confirm by eye before tuning it.
  * GRPO on a three-way answer space is close to supervised learning with low rollout diversity; without entropy monitoring, format masking and a lambda sweep the result is either constant abstention or constant 'yes'.
  * Preference and reward data built from a model's own outputs must be regenerated per policy, so costs scale with the number of base models; keep to one hero plus one appendix policy.
  * Track C cannot start before Week 4 and must not displace report v1 (Nov 8) or v2 (Nov 22); if cluster access arrives after the course, the same plan runs as a post-course paper with the advisor, which is why it is pre-registered now.
  * Several closest-prior numbers (OPA-DPO 4.8k samples and 4xA100, Reinforced Hesitation 4xH100 and 312 steps, SENTINEL 12K samples, HII-DPO 8,000 images and 5-of-10 filter) come from repository READMEs or paper HTML read on 12 Oct 2026; re-read the tables before quoting them in the report.

PROTOCOL says every headline HF number runs in bf16 on the pod and T4 fp32 is only a backup.

If the session has time left, add Qwen2.5-VL-3B and CheXOne on the floor set (the advisor's claim-2 pair) as an optional row. Use the Kaggle T4 fp32 only for the 200-item flip check, or as the fallback if RunPod fails; log s/item and peak VRAM in both places.
  [major] The mapping budget (1-3 CPU-h) assumes the (width,height) dimension key resolves most JPEGs and hashing is only for 'multi-candidates'. Radiograph plates come in standard sizes, so the size buckets will be large and a hash pass over all 112,120 NIH PNGs is the expected path, not a fallback; decoding 112k PNGs plus 30k large JPEGs on Kaggle's CPU with network I/O is about 1-2 h wall-clock, not 40 min, and a 64-bit pHash can tie on near-identical chest films.

      fix: Keep the first-hour dimension test, but schedule the full-NIH hash pass as the default (multiprocessing, 256-px resize, 16x16 pHash plus dHash from ImageHash 4.3.2, BSD-2, verified), match within the dimension bucket, require a Hamming margin between the top-1 and top-2 candidates tuned on the 200 spot-checked pairs, log ties as unmatched, and give Oct 13 a half-day buffer into Oct 14 morning. Store the UID-to-filename table only as a private Kaggle dataset.

If both instructor and advisor are silent by Oct 16, assume the HANDOFF reading (results report) and a one-paragraph cover note.
