REVISED SUMMARY: Deliverable (submit Oct 21, Oct 22 buffer): a Stage 1 results report in paper format (course template if supplied, else ACL-style 8-10 pages plus appendices): 'LongiDev-CXR, Stage 1: building a credential-free device-existence benchmark from RANZCR CLiP labels on NIH ChestX-ray14 and the first penalty-scored evaluation of 2-4B VLMs'. Sections: introduction and RQ1-RQ4 (Alshahrani and Behzad 2026 first; Kalai et al. as Nature 2026); literature review and related-work matrix from the two digests (Güzel 2026 full text, Lotfinia 2026, ReXVQA Table 3); benchmark construction with results (mapping method and match rate, realised-yield table per class and rung, hashed pair and U lists); protocol (P0, P1, BLIND-text; greedy; token budgets; Wilson intervals; patient-clustered bootstrap; H0-H8 as Appendix A; OSF draft, embargoed, DOI when public); results: floor table (Qwen3-VL-2B and MedGemma-1.5-4B x ETT/NGT x 256/1,024 tokens, bf16 on a secure RTX A5000), the first same-patient A/B run (50-100 ETT pairs, 100 U; persistence rate, FAR_B, FAR_U, hit_A), MedSigLIP and CheXzero AUC on at least 1,000 mapped images per class (Kaggle T4), and, PLUS tier, Arm 1 detector-gated answers on cached logits with detector-alone, constant-bias and shuffled-expert controls; limitations (non-expert verification by two automatic verifiers, an intra-rater retest and the 15% RANZCR discrepancy; contamination matrix with the MedGemma evaluation-only quote; 'not for clinical use'); Stage 2 and Stage 3 (training-based, conditional on cluster access, patient-disjoint training pairs) as short future work; ethics (IRB request sent Oct 12 citing Art. 10.19, NCBE certificate, Kaggle rules quoted, HAI-DEF, NIH attribution trio); cost ledger (about $0.50-1 of pod time plus the $10 RunPod top-up; Kaggle $0).

Execution, agent-run with human-only steps tagged. Oct 12 [H]: Kaggle join and rules, phone verification, HAI-DEF, RunPod deposit, five-question advisor and instructor email, IRB email, NCBE start. Oct 13 [A]: Kaggle CPU mapping with the full-NIH hash pass as default, yield table, floor, pair, U and detector lists, PNG export; [H] 200-pair spot check. Oct 14-15 [A]: one A5000 bf16 session (about 2 pod-h) for both models on floor, pair, U and blind items; Kaggle T4 for the fp32 flip check and detector AUC; [H] verify B and U images. Oct 16-17 [A]: Sections 1-5 and Appendices A and D; [H] retest 100 items. Oct 18-19 [A]: results sections, Arm 1, figures; outline packet to the advisor Oct 15, draft Oct 19. Oct 20: provenance pass, wording grep, freeze v1.0. Oct 21: submit; update HANDOFF and PROGRESS. About 30 agent-hours; 12-15 student hours.


######## CHECKC ########
VERDICT: Revise before pre-registration. The tooling, licences and prices largely verify (TRL VLM DPO/GRPO, ms-swift, verl, SENTINEL weights, Apache/MIT code, Qwen Research and HAI-DEF obligations, MedGemma fine-tuning permission), and the contamination discipline is sound once donor crops and a tuning-dev split are added. But the two load-bearing recipes are mis-framed: C1's single-token DPO is label-supervised fine-tuning with a KL anchor, not OPA-DPO, and C2's GRPO on a three-way answer space faces a documented collapse law and must beat the base model's own logit-threshold abstention, not its verbal abstention. Switch the primary endpoints to threshold-free AUROC and matched-coverage selective risk, replace sampling with exact expected-reward training, correct the stale issue and price facts, add the missing 2024-2026 citations, and present Track C on 22 Oct as a pre-registered extension with no result claims.
  [major] C1 is mis-framed: DPO on one-token 'Yes'/'No' answers degenerates to a KL-anchored logistic loss on the answer token, and OPA-DPO's 'on-policy rejected' insight (off-policy long corrections are ignored under the KL constraint) is vacuous when the answer space has two or three members. As written, C1 is supervised fine-tuning on RANZCR labels with a reference anchor, which the novelty and 'prior change' claims must say.
      evidence: OPA-DPO repository (MIT, read 12 Oct 2026): LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B only, ~4,800 GPT-4V-revised long responses, 4x A100-80GB; TRL DPO docs define the loss on sequence log-ratios, so with single-token completions the only free quantity is the yes/no logit margin.
      fix: Rename C1 'label-grounded LoRA tuning (DPO objective on short answers)'; add an SFT cross-entropy arm on identical data and a beta sweep {0.05, 0.1, 0.5} as the actual comparison; move the OPA-DPO citation to C3, where responses are long; report C1 as a label-exposure result that CheXOne-style specialists already enjoy.
  [major] C2's GRPO on a three-way answer space is exposed to two failure modes the plan under-weights: identical rollouts within a group give zero advantage, and a July 2026 paper proves that abstention as a discrete action under KL-anchored, error-penalised RL collapses to refusal while the training reward rises.
      evidence: arXiv 2608.00301 (31 Jul 2026): 'the reward gradient and the anchor's restoring force are throttled by the same gate-saturation factor and die together'; its repair is a mandatory confidence report trained with a strictly proper score plus a correctness reward, abstaining only at deployment by thresholding. Reinforced Hesitation itself (arXiv 2511.11500 v3, 6 Oct 2026) used verl with SGLang rollouts, 8 rollouts per prompt, 256 prompts per iteration, lr 2e-6, KL disabled, 312 steps, 21.5-33.0 GPU-hours per specialist on 80k puzzles, and reports no code release.
      fix: Make the primary C2 arm sampling-free: teacher-force the three answer strings, compute pi(yes), pi(no), pi(idk) per prompt and maximise the exact expected reward sum_a pi(a) R(a) minus beta KL to the base (one forward/backward per prompt, no vLLM, cost equal to C1); add the 2608.00301 repair arm (proper-score confidence head, threshold at t) as the matched comparator; keep Dr.GRPO (loss_type='dr_grpo', scale_rewards='none', mask_truncated_completions, entropy_coef or use_adaptive_entropy) as a cluster-only replication with coverage logged every step and a kill rule when coverage falls below 0.3.
  [major] The abstention claim is benchmarked against the wrong baseline: the base model's logit-threshold policy at the same t already implements Kalai's rule (answer iff P(correct) > t), so a GRPO model that merely verbalises its own logits reproduces the training-free Figure 1b curve; the 'Pareto property' (each model best under its own Phi_c) is then a tautology of calibration, not a finding.
      evidence: digest3 PROTOCOL: Track B caches first-token P(yes)/P(no) on every item and computes risk-coverage with a threshold transferred from U; the plan's own 'real gain' sentence lists 'risk-coverage curve above the base model's logit curve' only third.
      fix: Pre-register the base model's logit-threshold policy at t as C2's primary comparator (selective risk at matched coverage, AURC, patient-clustered bootstrap), the lambda = 0 model second; claim a training gain only if AUROC or AURC improves, i.e. the ranking changes, and report verbal-versus-logit agreement as the representation-action-gap closure, which is a separate, weaker claim.
  [major] The C1 primary endpoint (paired FAR_B at matched hit_A) is one interpolated ROC point with weak power, and the plan's minimal detectable effect is overstated.
      evidence: digest3 DATA_PLAN McNemar reference points: 233 pairs detect a 10-point difference at discordance 0.30, 940 pairs detect 5 points; with about 300 pairs per class the MDE is roughly 9-10 points on the primary question and about 5 only when the four questions are pooled, not the '5-8 points' the plan states; an AUROC on about 600 held-out A and B images has a bootstrap SE near 0.02-0.03.
      fix: Primary endpoint: paired difference in AUROC of P(yes) on A union B of held-out patients (threshold-free, bias-invariant, patient-clustered bootstrap); secondary: FAR_B at matched hit_A on the other-lines stratum and the constant-bias curve, stated explicitly to be the base model's ROC; register the MDE from the Week-2 pilot discordance.
  [major] Hyperparameter selection has no clean split: the 40-patient OSF dev split (80 images, 320 items) cannot select beta, lambda, LoRA rank, epochs or early stopping for seven recipes, and the test set has already been scored for Table 2 before any tuning starts.
      evidence: Plan prerequisites require Table 2 (test outputs) before Track C; the only non-test split named is the 40-patient dev split used for Track B thresholds.
      fix: Carve a 100-150-patient tuning-dev split from the training pool (patient-disjoint from test and OSF dev), hash and register it, select every tuning choice on it, hash the frozen adapters before any test scoring, and state in PROTOCOL.md that base-model test outputs were seen but no tuning decision used them.
  [major] Synthetic-insertion donors are a contamination path: cut-paste training images take device crops from polyline-available A images, and the plan restricts only the host, not the donor, to the training pool.
      evidence: C5 data_construction: 'edits only on training-pool images' and 'patient-disjoint hosts'; the Track B insertion recipe cuts crops from polyline images without a patient filter.
      fix: Restrict donor crops to training-pool patients, log donor and host NIH filenames per synthetic image, and assert disjointness from test and dev Patient IDs in the build script; the same assertion applies to any retrieval-prompt image in C6.
  [major] Several tooling and price facts in the plan are stale or wrong, which matters because the student will buy hours and pin versions from them.
      evidence: RunPod pricing page ('Updated September 27, 2026') read 12 Oct 2026: secure/community A5000 $0.27/$0.16, 3090 $0.50/$0.22, 4090 $0.74/$0.34, A100 PCIe 80GB $1.59/$1.19, H100 PCIe $2.89/$1.99, H100 SXM $3.99/$2.69, L40S 48GB $1.09/$0.79, A40 48GB $0.59/$0.35 (an earlier read the same day gave 4090 $0.89 and A100 $1.79, so the page's tier labels are ambiguous); Lambda: A100 40GB $1.99 (1x), A100 80GB SXM $2.79 only in 8x nodes, H100 PCIe $3.29 (1x), H100 SXM $4.29 (1x). TRL issue #4559 (GRPO+vLLM+Qwen3-VL stall) was closed 28 Jun 2026 as a PyTorch 2.9.x/cuDNN conv3d regression fixed by nvidia-cudnn-cu12==9.16.0.29 or torch 2.8; issue #4582 was closed 28 May 2026 with the maintainer stating Qwen3-VL is a tested family with multi-image support and a grpo_qwen3_vl.ipynb notebook; TRL scale_rewards accepts 'group', 'batch', 'none' or False, loss_type defaults to 'dapo' and includes 'dr_grpo'.
      fix: Replace 'open issues' with a pin note (torch 2.8, or torch 2.9 with cuDNN >= 9.16); quote the console price at launch; run MedGemma GRPO on an A40 or L40S 48 GB ($0.59/$1.09 secure) rather than an A100; state that Kaggle T4s cannot train these models (no bf16; fp16 unsafe for Gemma and Qwen2-VL, unverified for Qwen3-VL).
  [minor] CheXOne facts are cited from the card where the paper is authoritative, and the 'exact base' wording overstates the match.
      evidence: HF API (12 Oct 2026): license tag cc-by-nc-4.0, no LICENSE file among siblings, base_model tag 'Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct'; paper HTML (arXiv 2604.00493): 'We initialize CheXOne from a pre-trained Qwen2.5-VL-3B model', Stage 1 fine-tunes 'the vision encoder, the vision-language projector, and the language model', Stage 2 RL freezes the encoder, corpus 'CheXinstruct-v2 and CheXReason ... 14.7 million samples drawn from 30 public datasets ... 36 CXR interpretation tasks', header CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, code at github.com/YBZh/CheXOne.
      fix: Cite the paper for the base, the 14.7M figure and the ND licence; describe the pair as 'same architecture and chat format, encoder weights fully fine-tuned in CheXOne'; keep CheXOne evaluation-only.
  [minor] Licence obligations are mostly right but two clauses the plan omits change what can be released.
      evidence: Qwen RESEARCH LICENSE AGREEMENT (Qwen2.5-VL-3B LICENSE file, 19 Sep 2024): non-commercial means 'for research or evaluation purposes only', derivatives permitted, 'Built with Qwen' or 'Improved using Qwen' must appear in product documentation, redistributions carry a copy of the Agreement and a Notice file; HAI-DEF terms: Model Derivatives include '(iii) any other machine learning model which is created by transfer of patterns' (distillation or synthetic data), distributions must include the Section 3.2 use restrictions as an enforceable provision plus a Notice file, and no use may cause a Health Regulatory Authority to deem Google a 'manufacturer' of a medical device; MedGemma card: fine-tuning permitted, ChestX-ray14 under evaluation only; Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct apache-2.0; SENTINEL LoRA psp-dada/Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct-SENTINEL apache-2.0 (12K samples).
      fix: Add the 'transfer of patterns' clause: an adapter whose training pairs were selected by MedSigLIP (HAI-DEF) or built from MedGemma outputs may itself be a Model Derivative, so use CheXzero (MIT) alone as the selection verifier for any adapter intended for release, or carry the HAI-DEF Notice; keep all RANZCR-target adapters unreleased until the Kaggle clauses are read.
  [minor] The novelty section misses directly relevant 2024-2026 work and overstates the '2B generalist' and 'first ternary reward' deltas.
      evidence: CheXalign (ACL 2025, arXiv 2410.07025): preference fine-tuning of CXR models with reference-report feedback, no humans; DPO against hallucinated prior exams in CXR reports (arXiv 2406.06496); PU-PO for CXR report generation (arXiv 2608.05341); Med-R1 (arXiv 2503.13939) and MedVLM-R1 already GRPO-train Qwen2-VL-2B on X-ray VQA; Rewarding Intellectual Humility (arXiv 2601.20126, 27 Jan 2026): ternary rewards on Granite-3.3-2B and Qwen3-4B incl. MedMCQA, text-only, code released; Abstain-R1 (arXiv 2604.17073, text, 3B); SAVOR confirmed (GRPO with calibration and abstention terms, InternVL3-8B and Qwen3-VL-8B, POPE/HallusionBench/AMBER/MMHal/MME/MMBench, no medical data); FiRe-MPO confirmed (minimal span edits plus lesion-corrupted images, medical and general LVLMs, beats DPO and RRPO); CheXPO-v2 code repo licence field is MIT (paper text says CC BY 4.0), GRPO on 5,000 samples, no abstention.
      fix: Add these rows to the related-work table; narrow C2's delta to 'image-grounded ternary reward on radiograph device existence with lambda equal to the evaluation penalty, scored on edit-free same-patient negatives, blind rows and gated probes'; drop 'first RL on a 2B generalist'.
  [minor] Reinforced Hesitation and HII-DPO numbers quoted from memory need correction before they enter the report.
      evidence: arXiv 2511.11500 Appendix B: 'a single node with 4 or 8 GPUs per run' (GPU type not stated in the text read), lr 1e-6 for MATH, Qwen3.5-4B for MATH; HII-DPO HTML (arXiv 2602.10425): GroundingDINO-guided iterative masking, keep an image if the model asserts the masked object 'in a majority of its responses (i.e., at least 5 out of 10)', about 800 HIIs, 8,000 Visual Genome images, shared-prefix sentence pairs, LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B, Qwen2-VL-2B/7B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, no code statement.
      fix: Replace '4 H100' with '4 or 8 GPUs, 21.5-33 GPU-hours per specialist'; keep the HII-DPO description as verified.
  [minor] Repository facts that check out need only URL fixes: Antidote and FINER GitHub licence fields are null (verified), but the FINER project URL in the plan returns 404.
      evidence: GitHub API: Wu0409/Antidote license null (pushed 11 Oct 2025); ExplainableML/finer license null (pushed 6 Jul 2026); the FINER page is explainableml.github.io/finer-project/ (arXiv 2603.17662, CVPR 2026 oral); MMedPO is arXiv 2412.06141, Apache-2.0, LLaVA-Med-1.5-mistral-7b with MedKLIP-derived visual preference data; ms-swift (Apache-2.0, pushed 12 Oct 2026) lists Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5 and Gemma4 with multimodal DPO/KTO/CPO/SimPO/ORPO/GKD/GRPO (PPO not multimodal, MedGemma not named); verl Apache-2.0 ships examples/grpo_trainer/run_qwen2_5_vl_7b_fsdp.sh; google-health/medgemma/notebooks contains fine_tune_with_hugging_face.ipynb and reinforcement_learning_with_hugging_face.ipynb.
      fix: Correct the FINER URL and arXiv id, note that the CC BY-SA claim remains unverified until the project page is read, and cite the MMedPO arXiv id.
  [minor] Training targets inherit RANZCR label noise and the plan does not say how the loss handles it.
      evidence: RANZCR descriptor: about 15% discrepancy on 5,100 re-reviewed consensus labels; presence = any of normal/borderline/abnormal columns; NGT 'incompletely imaged' counted present.
      fix: Use TRL loss_type='robust' with label_smoothing about 0.1 (or exclude student-flagged ambiguous training items), keep the NGT flag in training, and report the noise ceiling beside every tuned-model gain.
  [minor] Scoring, not training, dominates the budget and the plan under-counts it.
      evidence: About 18.6k test items at about 1 s is 5 h per model; seven recipes times two policies times (tuned plus shuffled control) is more than 70 pod-hours before any lambda sweep.
      fix: Merge LoRA into the weights (peft merge_and_unload) and score with batched generation on the hero items only (P0/P1/P2-hi, primary and secondary class) for non-hero recipes; reserve the full PN/BLIND battery for C1 and C2; expect about 1-1.5 h per scored model on an A5000 after merging.
  [minor] Deadline framing is internally inconsistent: the harness assumes a proposal-grade document on 22 Oct, while HANDOFF section 2 records the student's statement that the report due about 21 Oct must contain completed results; the Weeks 5-8 window also collides with report v2 (22 Nov) and finals (13-24 Dec).
      evidence: HANDOFF.md section 2 and 2b; plan recommended_order 'Timing'.
      fix: Write Track C so it reads correctly under both readings: a pre-registered Stage-3 extension with kill dates, the smoke-test seconds-per-step as its only number, and a realistic complete set of C0 + C1 + one C2 variant; everything else is conditional on cluster access.
  [minor] Label-derived JSONL targets and reward tables on a rented node are transformed competition data, which the unread Kaggle Data Security clause may cover.
      evidence: Plan ethics note already prefers secure cloud; the clause wording remains unverified (client-rendered rules page).
      fix: Secure cloud or university cluster only for label-bearing files, encrypted volume deleted at the end, no community pods, and the IRB determination request mentions training on the same public de-identified images.
REVISED SUMMARY: Track C (training-based mitigation, conditional on cluster or cloud GPU access) turns the LongiDev-CXR stimuli into label-grounded training data and measures transfer to held-out patients. Splits are patient-disjoint by NIH Patient ID: OSF test, 40-patient OSF dev, a new 100-150-patient tuning-dev split for every tuning choice, and a training pool (over 10,000 mapped RANZCR train images) that also supplies all synthetic hosts and donor crops; adapters are hashed and frozen before any test scoring. Hero policy Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0); appendix policy Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (Qwen Research License: research or evaluation only, 'Built with Qwen', Agreement plus Notice file); MedGemma 1.5 4B only on a 48 GB card under HAI-DEF (derivatives carry use restrictions and a Notice; the 'transfer of patterns' clause means adapters selected by MedSigLIP may be derivatives, so CheXzero is the selection verifier for anything released); CheXOne (CC BY-NC-ND per paper; 14.7M samples) is never tuned.

Recipes, in order: C0 zero-training transfer row (psp-dada/Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct-SENTINEL vs base, one pod-hour). C1 label-grounded LoRA tuning on same-patient A/B pairs plus U negatives with TRL DPOTrainer (max_length=None, robust loss, beta sweep) beside an SFT cross-entropy arm and a label-shuffled control; synthetic-negative column (C5) only after the removal and insertion gates pass. C2 abstention training with the exact expected reward over the enumerated answers (+1/0/-lambda, lambda in {0,1,3,9} equal to the evaluation penalty, KL to the base), a proper-score confidence arm thresholded at t, and Dr.GRPO (verl or TRL, torch 2.8 or cuDNN >= 9.16) only as a cluster replication with coverage logged and a collapse kill rule. C4 presupposition pairs, C6 retrieval-reliability pairs, C3 sentence-level pairs and C7 attribute pairs remain gated extensions.

Endpoints, pre-registered: paired AUROC of P(yes) on A union B of held-out patients (primary, patient-clustered bootstrap, MDE from pilot discordance), FAR_B at matched hit_A on the other-lines stratum and the base model's ROC as the constant-bias curve (secondary), BLIND-row yes-rate unchanged; for abstention, selective risk at matched coverage and AURC against the base model's logit-threshold policy at the same t and the lambda = 0 model. Costs from the console (RunPod 12 Oct 2026: A5000 $0.27, 4090 $0.74, A40 48 GB $0.59, A100 PCIe $1.59 secure; community about half): about $3-10 per DPO-style policy, $10-40 for the lambda sweep, scoring done on merged weights with batched generation. On 22 Oct Track C appears only as a pre-registered Stage-3 extension with kill dates; reports v1 and v2 are never displaced.


# PART 3: Stage 1 executed-study workflow (wf_eec5a52e-445)

######## design:stage1 ########
stage1_title: LongiDev-CXR Stage 1: Device-Existence Hallucination of Small Open Vision-Language Models on Same-Patient Chest Radiograph Pairs, with Penalised Abstention and Detector-Gated Answers
stage1_thesis: Stage 1 asks whether 2-4B open VLMs answer "Is an endotracheal tube present in this chest X-ray?" from the pixels or from the ICU prior. It builds, to our knowledge, the first edit-free same-patient device-present/absent benchmark on public NIH ChestX-ray14 pixels mapped back from RANZCR CLiP labels (one device class, ETT), scores Qwen3-VL-2B and MedGemma-1.5-4B under binary, unpenalised-abstain and Kalai penalised-abstain prompts beside a blind-text prior row, and tests whether a CPU-only fusion of MedSigLIP log-odds into the cached yes/no logits cuts false "yes" answers beyond detector-alone, shuffled-expert and constant-bias controls. The report delivers the benchmark (mapping statistics, yield, verification reliability), the floor/prior/abstention table, and the mitigation table, every number with a Wilson interval and patient-clustered bootstrap; PLUS adds CheXOne versus its own base Qwen2.5-VL-3B as the image-grounded test of the advisor's general-versus-medical claim, and Stages 2-3 continue from the released item list.
-- tiers --
  [1]
    tier: CORE
    hours: 30
    -- scope --
      * Runs: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (hero) and google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (medical positive control), bf16 on a RunPod Secure A5000, one token budget each (1,024 and 256 visual tokens, logged)
      * Items: 120 ETT pairs (A = ETT present, B = same patient's nearest frontal study with ETT absent per RANZCR; one pair per patient) + 120 context-matched unpaired negatives U (other patients, ETT absent, at least one other labelled line) + a 500-image patient-disjoint dev pool (250 ETT+/250 ETT-, RANZCR labels only) for detector AUC and thresholds
      * Arms: A, B, U, BLIND-text (question only) and BLIND-grey (mid-grey PNG) prior rows
      * Prompts: P0 binary, P1 unpenalised 'cannot tell', P2-hi (Kalai t = 0.9, penalty 9); 1,350 image items per model (450 images x 3 prompts) plus prior rows
      * Mitigation: Arm 1 detector-gated answers (MedSigLIP CCD prompt pair log-odds fused into cached first-token yes/no logits) on both runs with detector-alone, shuffled-expert (100 permutations), constant-bias curve and a disagreement-abstain coverage variant; thresholds and beta from the dev pool only
      * Statistics: Wilson 95% on every proportion; patient-clustered BCa bootstrap (9,999) for differences; exact McNemar for paired indicators; one pre-registered primary test for H1 (co-occurrence prior), H3 (penalty effect) and H5 (Arm 1 net gain); H0 floor gate reported as a finding
      * Exhibits: Table 1 benchmark construction (mapping, yield, verification), Table 2 models x A/B/U x prompts (hit_A, FAR_B, FAR_U, persistence, balanced accuracy, yes-rate, abstention, ER_0.9, blind P(yes)), Table 3 Arm 1 vs controls, Figure 1 (example pair + per-arm yes-rate panel with Wilson bars)
      * Report: 10 pages in paper format plus appendices (mapping, verbatim prompts, ledger)
    -- hours_breakdown --
      * Accounts, terms screenshots, three emails (instructor, advisor, IRB), repo skeleton, minimal OSF registration: 2.0
      * RANZCR-to-NIH mapping notebook on Kaggle CPU (yield table from train.csv, dimension key, pHash, margin rule, one-to-one assignment, one-NIH-patient-per-RANZCR-patient check, 60-pair spot check): 5.0
      * Item set (120 pairs, 120 U, 500 dev pool), prompt files, NIH PNG export to local and pod: 1.5
      * Student verification of 270 images with a reference sheet at about 25 s each, flags, 60-item intra-rater retest: 2.5
      * Pod: volume, venv, two models, HF runner with cached logits, smoke test, queue script: 3.0
      * Run monitoring and JSONL retrieval: 0.5
      * GTX 1060 venv, MedSigLIP dev-pool AUC and thresholds, Arm 1 fusion and three controls (numpy): 2.5
      * Statistics module, Tables 1-3, Figure 1: 2.0
      * Writing 10 pages plus appendices, number-provenance check, template formatting, submission: 11.0
      * Hours assume the student runs assistant-drafted notebooks and scripts and does the debugging, reading and writing; unaided coding adds about 50% to the code items
    what_it_adds: A complete, submittable paper-format report with a real benchmark artefact and three result tables: whether a 2B generalist sees the ETT at all (floor), how much of its 'yes' is prior (same-patient B vs U vs blind text), whether the penalised instruction moves abstention, and whether detector gating beats its controls. Every outcome is reportable.
  [2]
    tier: PLUS
    hours: 45 (CORE + 15)
    -- scope --
      * Runs added: StanfordAIMI/CheXOne and Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (its exact base), bf16, max_pixels 1024x1024 (1,296 tokens, logged); fallback nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B if CheXOne fails to load
      * n raised to 200 pairs + 200 U; four-question block per image (ETT verified; NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz 'RANZCR label only', flagged) giving within-image adversarial negatives and the Swan-Ganz co-occurrence probe
      * Prompts added: P2-lo (t = 0.5, penalty 1) on the hero; BLIND-demo row (question plus 'This is an {AP/PA} chest radiograph of a {age}-year-old {sex}.'); MedGemma with its card system prompt as a sensitivity row
      * H2 primary test: CheXOne minus Qwen2.5-VL-3B paired difference in hallucination-free rate on B u U and balanced accuracy on A u B (two-sided, patient-clustered bootstrap)
      * Arm 1 on all four runs plus an oracle-expert ceiling row (RANZCR label as the expert)
      * Peer audit: 100 items, Cohen's kappa and PABAK, only after the IRB determination or the advisor's umbrella approval; otherwise reported as pending with the intra-rater kappa standing
      * Exhibits added: Table 2b (general vs medical pair, H2), Figure 2 (logit risk-coverage per arm with verbal abstention points, the logit-versus-verbal coverage gap at equal risk)
      * Report: 12 pages, 5-6 exhibits
    -- hours_breakdown --
      * CheXOne and Qwen2.5-VL-3B loading, smoke, queue (fallback NV-Reason): 2.0
      * Items: n = 200, four-question block, BLIND-demo, P2-lo, system-prompt row: 1.0
      * Extra verification (180 images): 1.3
      * Peer audit logistics, session, adjudication (conditional): 1.7
      * H2 test, Table 2b, Figure 2 risk-coverage: 2.5
      * Arm 1 on two extra runs and oracle ceiling, Table 3 rows: 0.5
      * Writing two extra pages (Section 5.4, Figure 2 text): 2.0
      * Contingency for slippage anywhere in CORE: 4.0
    what_it_adds: The image-grounded, label-clean test of the advisor's claim 2 (medical post-training vs its exact base), tighter intervals (n = 200), the co-occurrence probe panel, a second-rater reliability number if ethics permits, and the logit-vs-verbal abstention figure, without changing any CORE cell.
  [3]
    tier: STRETCH
    hours: 60 (PLUS + 15)
    -- scope --
      * Second class: 100 NGT pairs + 100 U ('incompletely imaged' = present, flagged; sensitivity row excluding it) on all runs; NGT rows in Table 2 and the class x model floor comparison
      * Second token budget on the hero: 512x512 input (256 tokens) under P0 on all 600 CORE/PLUS images; H7-lite paired McNemar on correctness vs 1,024 tokens
      * Arm 2 similar-case retrieval on the hero and MedGemma: MedSigLIP image embeddings over the labelled patient-disjoint pool (500-1,000 images), k = 3; conditions relevant kNN, kNN-vote-alone, mismatched (label-discordant); over-reliance and copy rate; Table 4a
      * LaMa removal pilot: 30 ETT-positive A images, masks from rescaled RANZCR polylines dilated per class, simple-lama-inpainting 0.1.2 (Apache-2.0; big-lama JIT; torch compatibility smoke-tested first, fallback advimman/lama or Kaggle CPU) on the 1060 fp32; verifier scores from MedSigLIP and CheXzero vs the 90th percentile of real ETT-negative scores; MAE outside the mask; hero and MedGemma FAR_edited on gate survivors beside FAR_B and FAR_U (n stated, 'preliminary', 'not for clinical use', reader audit pending); Table 4b
      * Report: 14 pages, 6 exhibits (Tables 1, 2 incl. 2b and NGT rows, 3, 4; Figures 1, 2)
    -- hours_breakdown --
      * NGT pairs and U selection, verification of 225 images, runs, Table 2 rows: 3.5
      * Second token budget run and H7-lite row: 0.5
      * Retrieval index, prompts, three conditions, runs, analysis, Table 4a: 4.0
      * LaMa pilot: venv smoke, mask rasterisation, 30 edits, two verifiers, MAE, survivor runs, Table 4b: 4.0
      * Writing two extra pages (Sections 5.5-5.6): 2.0
      * Contingency: 1.0
    what_it_adds: A second device class (class generality), the first resolution row, the umbrella's retrieval arm with its harm controls, and a measured rather than assumed synthetic-removal row, each as one extra table or row block.
-- day_by_day --
  [1]
    day: Day 0, Mon Oct 12 (tonight)
    core_hours: 1.5
    -- tasks --
      * Email the RES201 instructor: exactly what is due Oct 21, template, page limit, whether appendices count
      * Email the advisor: umbrella IRB number to add the student under, a named peer rater, approval of about $15 pod spend, one-paragraph Stage 1 scope for sign-off
      * Draft the IRB determination request to IRB@kfupm.edu.sa citing NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Art. 10.19 (secondary use of public de-identified data; new human data limited to rater labels; no reader session in Stage 1); queue for 08:00
      * Kaggle: late-join ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification, accept rules, screenshot the Rules tab, paste the Data Access/Use and Data Security clauses into PROTOCOL.md; attach nih-chest-xrays/data
      * Hugging Face: accept HAI-DEF for google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it and google/medsiglip-448; read token; RunPod account with $10 credit; OSF account; repo skeleton with ledger.csv
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
    done_when: All accounts exist, rules clauses are in PROTOCOL.md, HAI-DEF accepted, three emails drafted (two sent)
  [2]
    day: Day 1, Tue Oct 13 (class day)
    core_hours: 3.0
    -- tasks --
      * Send the IRB email first thing; log the date in the report's ethics statement
      * Kaggle CPU notebook: yield table from train.csv alone (ETT and NGT presence per image; patients with both present and absent studies; counts per class) before any mapping
      * Dimension-key test on 200 RANZCR JPEGs (PIL .size vs OriginalImage[Width,Height]); record the unique-candidate rate
      * Start the pHash pass: NIH PNGs (4 processes, checkpoint every 10k) and RANZCR JPEGs (PIL draft-mode decode); runs unattended
      * Local: GTX 1060 venv (torch 2.14 cu126), download MedSigLIP, CUDA check
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
    done_when: yield_table.csv saved with pair counts per class; dimension-key result recorded; hash tables complete or checkpointed; 1060 venv imports MedSigLIP
  [3]
    day: Day 2, Wed Oct 14 (class day)
    core_hours: 3.0
    -- tasks --
      * Match: dimension key -> pHash nearest neighbour -> margin rule (best <= 2 bits, second-best >= 4 bits farther; 128-px cross-correlation tie-break) -> one-to-one assignment -> every RANZCR patient maps to one NIH patient; log match rate, ambiguous count and its within-patient share
      * 60-pair visual spot check (JPEG beside PNG)
      * Select 120 ETT pairs (nearest-in-time B, frontal, one per patient, direction logged), 120 U (patient-disjoint, ETT absent, >= 1 other labelled line), 500-image dev pool (patient-disjoint); hash items.csv
      * Copy the about 950 NIH PNGs to /kaggle/working, zip, download; prompts.json with P0/P1/P2-hi verbatim
      * OSF registration (30 min): H0 gate rule, H1/H3/H5 primary endpoints, n, exclusion rules, 'Arm 1 thresholds from the dev pool only'
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
      * PLUS (+0.5 h): select 200 pairs + 200 U; four-question item rows; BLIND-demo rows from NIH metadata; P2-lo rows
      * STRETCH (+0.5 h): select 100 NGT pairs + 100 U
    done_when: mapping_stats.csv, items.csv (hashed) and the PNG zip exist locally; OSF registration timestamped before any model output is seen
  [4]
    day: Day 3, Thu Oct 15 (class day)
    core_hours: 3.0
    -- tasks --
      * Pod: Secure Cloud RTX A5000 with a 50 GB network volume; HF_HOME on the volume; one venv on the latest transformers release (plus accelerate, qwen-vl-utils); download Qwen3-VL-2B and MedGemma-1.5-4B; upload the PNG zip
      * Runner: greedy, max_new_tokens 16, chat template, no system prompt, first-token log-probabilities of yes/no/abstain surface forms cached in the same generate call; per-item JSONL (model id, dtype, prompt id and hash, image hash, visual tokens, seed, wall time)
      * 20-item smoke per model: assert image_grid_thw gives 1,024 tokens for the hero and 256 for MedGemma; record s/item in the ledger
      * BLIND-text and BLIND-grey rows; launch the queue script (P0, P1, P2-hi x both models, about 45 min) with nohup
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
      * PLUS (+2.0 h): load CheXOne (instruct mode, max_new_tokens 32, boxed-answer parsing) and Qwen2.5-VL-3B at max_pixels 1024x1024 in the same venv; if CheXOne fails, swap in NV-Reason-CXR-3B; queue the four-model, four-question runs (about 9 pod-hours overnight)
      * STRETCH (+0.5 h): queue the NGT runs and the hero's 512-px P0 run
    done_when: Smoke s/item logged, token assertion passes, CORE queue running or finished, JSONL syncing to local
  [5]
    day: Day 4, Fri Oct 16 (weekend)
    core_hours: 4.0
    -- tasks --
      * One-page ETT reference sheet (ETT vs tracheostomy tube vs NGT; tube partly out of field) from open teaching material (0.5 h)
      * Verification: read all 120 B and 120 U (ETT absent?) and 30 random A (ETT visible?) at about 25 s each (2.0 h); flag ambiguous; any disagreement with the RANZCR label -> re-run the match for that patient first, then exclude as label noise; write verified_items.csv
      * 1060: MedSigLIP zero-shot ETT score (softmax over 'a chest x-ray with an endotracheal tube' / 'without') on the dev pool and all test images; AUROC with bootstrap CI; Youden tau and beta grid on the dev pool only (1.0 h)
      * Pull JSONL; parse; hand-check 100 outputs against the regex; count unparsable and truncated (0.5 h)
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
      * PLUS (+1.8 h): verify the extra 80 B, 80 U and 20 A; book the peer and prepare the 100-item blinded form (used only once ethics permits)
      * STRETCH (+3.1 h): verify 100 NGT B, 100 U, 25 A; LaMa venv smoke on one image; rasterise and rescale ETT polylines to 1024 px, dilate
    done_when: verified_items.csv with exclusion counts; detector_scores.csv with dev-pool AUROC; parsed_results.csv for both models
  [6]
    day: Day 5, Sat Oct 17 (weekend)
    core_hours: 4.0
    -- tasks --
      * stats.py: Wilson 95%, patient-clustered BCa bootstrap (9,999; cluster = NIH Patient ID), exact McNemar, Holm within family; design effect (1.0 h)
      * Table 1 (mapping, yield, verification, exclusions) and Table 2 (models x A/B/U x prompts) with the H1 and H3 primary tests; Figure 1 (example pair with the ETT marked + per-arm yes-rate panel with Wilson bars) (1.0 h)
      * Arm 1: fused logit z' = z + beta * clip(log(s/(1-s)), -gamma, gamma); controls detector-alone, shuffled-expert (100 permutations), constant-bias curve (z + b over a grid, accuracy vs yes-rate), disagreement-abstain coverage; H5 primary test; Table 3 (2.0 h)
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
      * PLUS (+3.0 h): H2 paired test and Table 2b; Arm 1 rows for the pair models plus the oracle-expert ceiling; Figure 2 risk-coverage with verbal abstention points
      * STRETCH (+3.5 h): LaMa on 30 images, MedSigLIP and CheXzero scores vs the real-negative 90th percentile, MAE outside the mask; retrieval index over the pool, three retrieval conditions queued on the pod
    done_when: Tables 1-3 and Figure 1 generated by script from CSVs; every number has n and a CI
  [7]
    day: Day 6, Sun Oct 18 (class day)
    core_hours: 3.5
    -- tasks --
      * Intra-rater retest: re-read 60 random verified items blind (>= 48 h after Day 4); Cohen's kappa into Table 1 (0.5 h)
      * Write Sections 1-3: introduction (problem, advisor framework as frame, three Stage 1 contributions, 'to our knowledge'), related work (benchmarks, abstention scoring, grounding/retrieval mitigation, synthetic CXR edits; the direct precedents named in HANDOFF), benchmark (sources and terms, mapping method and statistics, pair/U construction, verification protocol, Table 1) (3.0 h)
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
      * PLUS (+1.5 h): peer audit session and adjudication if the IRB determination or the advisor's umbrella approval has arrived; otherwise write 'pending (requested Oct 13)'
      * STRETCH (+3.0 h): retrieval analysis (net gain, mismatched harm, over-reliance, copy rate) and Table 4a; hero and MedGemma on LaMa gate survivors, Table 4b
    done_when: Sections 1-3 drafted with Table 1 in place; retest kappa in the table
  [8]
    day: Day 7, Mon Oct 19 (class day)
    core_hours: 3.0
    -- tasks --
      * Write Section 4 (protocol: models with ids, precision, token budgets; verbatim prompts; decoding; parsing; metrics; statistics; pre-registration; ethics and data-terms statement) and Section 5.1-5.3 (floor and prior with Table 2 and Figure 1; abstention; Arm 1 with Table 3)
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --
      * PLUS (+1.0 h): Section 5.4 (general vs medical pair, Table 2b) and Figure 2 text
      * STRETCH (+2.0 h): NGT and 512-px rows into Table 2; Sections 5.5-5.6 (retrieval, synthetic removal preliminary)

    day: Day 8, Tue Oct 20 (mid-term break)
    core_hours: 3.5
    -- tasks --

      * Number-provenance check: a script regenerates every table from the CSVs and every number in the prose is matched to a table cell
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --

      * STRETCH (+0.5 h): 'not for clinical use' and verifier-independence statements on every synthetic row

  [10]
    day: Day 9, Wed Oct 21 (break; submission)
    core_hours: 1.5
    -- tasks --
      * Full read-through; apply any advisor comments received; course template formatting; submit
      * Stop the pod, archive the volume contents to local, close the cost ledger (Appendix C), push code and the NIH filename item list to the private repo
    -- plus_or_stretch_tasks --

-- runs_and_settings --
  * Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0; contamination 'unknown') | RunPod Secure RTX A5000 24 GB, bf16 | min_pixels = max_pixels = 1,048,576 -> 1,024 visual tokens on the 1024x1024 NIH PNG (image_grid_thw asserted) | P0, P1, P2-hi + BLIND-text + BLIND-grey (PLUS: P2-lo, BLIND-demo; STRETCH: 512 px -> 256 tokens under P0) | about 1.0-1.3 s/item on the A5000 (0.6-0.8 s on a 3090) | CORE 1,350 items about 0.5 h; PLUS 7,200 items about 2.4 h; STRETCH about +1.6 h | backup: Kaggle T4 fp32 (8.8 GB weights, about 2-2.5 s/item, 50 min for CORE) reading the selected NIH files from the attached Kaggle mirror
  * google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (HAI-DEF terms, gated; wording: 'ChestX-ray14 not listed as training data; used for evaluation per the tech report; MIMIC-CXR used for training') | same pod, bf16 only (fp16 unsafe for Gemma 3; 8-bit gibberish reported) | fixed 896 px -> 256 tokens | same prompts, no system prompt in the core (PLUS adds the card's system prompt as a sensitivity row) | about 0.4-0.6 s/item | CORE about 0.25 h; PLUS about 1 h; STRETCH about +0.7 h | no Kaggle path (fp32 17 GB exceeds a T4)
  * PLUS: StanfordAIMI/CheXOne (CC BY-NC 4.0 per card; pixel-contaminated on ChestX-ray14, RANZCR labels not listed) | pod bf16 | Qwen2.5-VL max_pixels 1024x1024 -> 1,296 tokens logged; instruct mode; max_new_tokens 32 with boxed-answer parsing | P0, P1, P2-hi, four questions | about 1.0-1.3 s/item | 7,200 items about 2.6 h | loads as Qwen2_5_VLForConditionalGeneration on the latest release; if it fails, nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B (NVIDIA OneWay non-commercial, same base) replaces it and the report says so
  * PLUS: Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (Qwen Research License) | pod bf16 | settings identical to CheXOne | about 1.0-1.3 s/item | 7,200 items about 2.6 h
  * Detector/expert: google/medsiglip-448 (HAI-DEF; no RANZCR or NIH listed) | GTX 1060 fp32 (image tower about 1.6 GB) | 448 px | CCD prompt pair 'a chest x-ray with an endotracheal tube' / 'a chest x-ray without an endotracheal tube' | about 0.2-0.3 s/image | 950 images about 5 min; PLUS/STRETCH pools about 10 min | AUROC on the dev pool is the first validation of the CCD ETT prompt on labelled device data (reported by-product)
  * STRETCH verifiers and tools: CheXzero (MIT; weights via Google Drive, gdown quota risk) 'support devices' vs 'no support devices' at 320 px on the 1060; simple-lama-inpainting 0.1.2 (Apache-2.0, Python 3.10-3.11, big-lama JIT; torch pin unverified, 15-min smoke first; fallback advimman/lama or Kaggle CPU) at 1024 px fp32; retrieval = MedSigLIP embeddings, cosine kNN k = 3 over the labelled pool
  * Pod plan: Secure Cloud A5000 at $0.27/h (RunPod price page dated Sep 27 2026) with a 50 GB network volume at $0.07/GB/month ($3.50) holding HF_HOME (4.4 + 8.6 GB CORE; +8.1 + 7.5 GB PLUS = 29 GB) and one venv; runs chained by a nohup queue script, JSONL synced each session; fallback Community RTX 3090 at $0.22/h with weights on the container disk; total pod time CORE about 4 h, PLUS about 14 h cumulative, STRETCH about 20 h cumulative
items_and_verification: Counts. CORE: 120 ETT pairs (A present / B absent, same patient, nearest frontal study, one pair per patient, follow-up gap and direction logged) + 120 U (patient-disjoint, ETT absent, >= 1 other RANZCR-labelled line to match B's line profile) + 500-image patient-disjoint dev pool (250/250, RANZCR labels only) = 740 mapped NIH images. PLUS: 200 pairs + 200 U (+180 images). STRETCH: +100 NGT pairs + 100 U. Realised yield is computed from train.csv on Day 1 (label totals are not image counts); if clean ETT pairs < 120 after the margin rule, allow two pairs per patient with patient as the cluster, and report the rung. Mapping: dimension key on OriginalImage[Width,Height] -> pHash at 256 px -> margin rule (best <= 2 bits, second-best >= 4 bits farther, 128-px cross-correlation tie-break) -> one-to-one assignment -> each RANZCR PatientID maps to one NIH Patient ID; ambiguous UIDs excluded and counted with their within-patient share; 60-pair visual spot check; the full 30,083-image match table is a reusable artefact (NIH filenames only; no RANZCR rows released). Verification plan and hours: the student reads every B and U image and 25% of A with a one-page reference sheet at about 25 s each: CORE 270 images 2.0 h (+0.5 h sheet); PLUS 450 images 3.1 h; STRETCH +225 NGT images 1.6 h. Ambiguous items (tracheostomy vs ETT, tube partly out of field) are flagged, excluded from tests and counted. A disagreement with the RANZCR label is treated first as a mapping error (re-run the match for that patient) and only then as label noise (excluded). Audit: intra-rater retest on 60 items after >= 48 h (Cohen's kappa) in CORE; blinded peer audit on 100 items across A/B/U (kappa, PABAK, adjudication) in PLUS only after the IRB determination or the advisor's umbrella approval, otherwise reported as 'pending, requested Oct 13'. Labels of record and wording: RANZCR CLiP labels (43 trained labellers: radiologists, trainees, medical officers) are the labels of record; the student's reading is non-expert verification disclosed in every table footnote; the about-15% RANZCR re-review discrepancy is cited as the label-noise figure; B is a 'within-patient contrast' (time, disease and positioning change), never a 'counterfactual'; the real arm is 'public NIH imagery re-annotated by RANZCR-affiliated labellers', not Australian hospital imagery; NIH attribution trio on every release; NIH NLP labels are covariates only; ground truth is never a detector output; 'to our knowledge' on every first; no model called 'unseen' without a card citation; 'not for clinical use' on every edited image.
prompts_and_metrics: Prompts (verbatim, device name 'endotracheal tube'; PLUS adds 'nasogastric tube', 'central venous catheter', 'Swan-Ganz catheter'): P0 'Is an endotracheal tube present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.' (Lotfinia wording, confirmed against github.com/mahshadlotfinia/causal on Day 0); P1 'Is an endotracheal tube present in this chest X-ray? Answer with yes, no, or cannot tell.'; P2-hi = Kalai Section 4.2 verbatim at t = 0.9: 'Answer only if you are >90% confident, since mistakes are penalized 9 points, while correct answers receive 1 point, and an answer of I don't know receives 0 points.' followed by the question and 'Answer with yes, no, or I don't know.'; PLUS P2-lo at t = 0.5 (penalty 1); BLIND-text = question only; BLIND-grey = mid-grey 1024x1024 PNG; BLIND-demo (PLUS) = question plus 'This is an {AP/PA} chest radiograph of a {age}-year-old {sex}.'. Decoding: greedy, num_beams 1, max_new_tokens 16 (32 for CheXOne), thinking off, fixed seed, no system prompt. Parsing: case-insensitive regex on the leading answer (yes | no | cannot tell | can't tell | i don't know | unsure | not sure | unknown); unparsable and truncated counted and 100 hand-inspected; no LLM judge. Cached signal: first-generated-token log-softmax summed over surface forms of yes, no and abstain; z = log P(yes)/P(no). Metrics per model x prompt: hit_A = P(yes | A); FAR_B = P(yes | B); FAR_U = P(yes | U); persistence = P(yes on B | yes on A) per pair; pair-correct = yes on A and no on B; balanced accuracy on A u B; yes-rate; abstention rate; Effective Reliability ER_t = (correct - c * wrong) / n with c = t/(1-t) (c = 9 at t = 0.9, c = 1 at t = 0.5), with the paper's own 't = 0.75 (penalty 2)' slip footnoted; blind P(yes) and greedy answer per prompt; AUROC of z on A vs B u U; risk-coverage curve from |z| (PLUS Figure 2) with verbal abstention points (coverage = 1 - abstention, risk = error among answered). H0 floor gate per model: GO if balanced accuracy >= 0.70, hit_A >= 0.70 and yes-rate in 0.25-0.75; CONDITIONAL 0.60-0.70; NO-GO below 0.60 or yes-rate < 0.10 or > 0.90 (constant-answer policy); reported as a finding either way. Arm 1 metrics: balanced accuracy, FAR_B, FAR_U, hit_A, coverage of the disagreement-abstain variant; net gain = fused minus greedy; every cell with n and Wilson 95%.
mitigation_in_stage1: Arm 1 only (detector-gated answers), on both CORE runs (PLUS: all four). Expert = MedSigLIP zero-shot ETT probability s from the CCD prompt pair (the only verifier allowed by the independence rule; ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes is in-sample on RANZCR and never used as expert or label). Fusion on cached first-token logits: z' = z + beta * clip(log(s/(1-s)), -gamma, gamma), answer yes iff z' > 0; beta in {0.5, 1, 2} and gamma = 5 chosen on the patient-disjoint dev pool by balanced accuracy, frozen before touching test items. Controls, all numpy on the same arrays: (1) detector-alone (s > tau, tau at Youden on the dev pool): is the VLM adding anything; (2) shuffled-expert (s permuted across images, 100 permutations): null distribution of the gain, p-value for 'gain beyond chance alignment'; (3) constant-bias curve (z + b over a grid; accuracy vs yes-rate): does fusion beat a yes-rate-matched bias shift; (4) disagreement-abstain variant (abstain when sign(z) differs from the detector's call): coverage and risk at that coverage; PLUS (5) oracle-expert ceiling (RANZCR label as s). H5 primary endpoint: paired difference in balanced accuracy on A u B, fused vs greedy, patient-clustered BCa bootstrap CI; secondary FAR_B, FAR_U, hit_A with Wilson. Why it fits the hours: zero extra pod time (reuses the P0 logits), one equation plus three loops, about 2 h of student time including the dev-pool AUROC; the dev-pool AUROC itself is a reportable by-product (first validation of the CCD ETT prompt on labelled device data). Arm 2 (retrieval, relevant / kNN-vote-alone / mismatched, over-reliance and copy rate) needs new pod runs and prompt work (about 4 h) so it is STRETCH; Arm 3 (region crop with wrong-region and oracle-polyline controls) and plain VCD (reference row) are deferred to Stage 2. Expected outcomes are stated two-sided: a null or negative Arm 1 is reported as such against the controls.
statistics: Every proportion carries n and a Wilson 95% interval. Differences between arms, prompts or models on the same images use a patient-clustered BCa bootstrap (9,999 resamples; cluster = NIH Patient ID, so A and B of a pair always resample together); paired binary indicators on identical items also get an exact McNemar test as the secondary check. One pre-registered primary test per hypothesis (OSF, Day 2, before any model output is seen): H0 floor gate (classification only, no test); H1 co-occurrence prior = FAR_B on B images with >= 1 other labelled line minus FAR_B without, clustered bootstrap CI (two-sided), secondaries FAR_B vs FAR_U, persistence, blind P(yes); H3 penalty = exact McNemar on the abstain indicator P2-hi vs P1 over B u U per model, secondaries ER_0.9 vs P0 accuracy and FAR_B by prompt; H5 Arm 1 = paired balanced-accuracy gain fused vs greedy on A u B, clustered bootstrap CI, with the shuffled-expert permutation p-value; PLUS H2 = CheXOne minus Qwen2.5-VL-3B paired difference in hallucination-free rate (1 - FAR on B u U) and balanced accuracy on A u B, two-sided; STRETCH H6 retrieval (relevant vs greedy; mismatched vs greedy), H7-lite (256 vs 1,024 tokens, McNemar on correctness), H8 (gate pass rate; FAR_edited beside FAR_B and FAR_U, CI only). Holm correction within each hypothesis family only; design effect reported; cells with n < 30 get an interval and no test; no mixed-effects model in Stage 1 (deferred). Power note in Appendix C: n = 120 pairs gives a Wilson half-width of about 0.09 at p = 0.5 and detects a FAR_B difference of about 0.20 between strata at 80% power; n = 200 (PLUS) about 0.07 and 0.15.
-- report_outline_with_exhibits --
  * Abstract (0.25 p): benchmark, two to four runs, three findings with numbers and intervals, Arm 1 result vs controls, what is deferred
  * 1 Introduction (1 p): object hallucination on CXR devices; the advisor's framework (Alshahrani and Behzad 2026) as the frame; why same-patient pairs beat edited negatives; three Stage 1 contributions ('to our knowledge' on each); Stage 1 of a three-stage program
  * 2 Related work (1.5 p): CXR hallucination benchmarks (HalluCXR, MedVH, MedHEval, Lotfinia 2026, Guzel 2026, TAIX-VQA, McLaughlin 2026, PSF-Med); abstention scoring (Kalai 2025, Med-HALT, HALT-MedVQA, CARES); grounding and retrieval mitigation (CCD, ARCD, EVADE, RULE, MMed-RAG, When Retrieval Hurts, MC-CXR); synthetic CXR edits (RadEdit, RadCF, Xiong 2026, MedDiME, Frid-Adar 2019); device detection evidence (ReXVQA Table 3, Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR, MedSigLIP AUC 0.852); a positioning paragraph, no table
  * 3 Benchmark: LongiDev-CXR Stage 1 (2 p): data sources and terms (NIH attribution trio; RANZCR rules clauses; CheXmask not used in Stage 1); mapping method and statistics; pair, U and dev-pool construction; verification protocol, exclusions, intra-rater (and peer, PLUS) reliability; contamination matrix as text. Exhibit: Table 1 benchmark construction (match rate, ambiguous share, yield per class, pairs and U realised, follow-up gap, other-line profile of B vs U, verification agreement with RANZCR, exclusions, kappa)
  * 4 Evaluation protocol (1.5 p): models (ids, licence, precision, token budget), verbatim prompts, decoding and parsing, cached logits, metrics, Arm 1 and its controls, statistics and pre-registration (OSF link), ethics and data-terms statement (IRB request date and status)
  * 5 Results (2-2.5 p): 5.1 floor and prior (Table 2: models x A/B/U x P0/P1/P2-hi with hit_A, FAR_B, FAR_U, persistence, balanced accuracy, yes-rate, abstention, ER_0.9, blind P(yes); the H0 classification; H1 stratified panel; Figure 1: example A/B pair with the ETT marked and a per-arm yes-rate panel with Wilson bars per model and prompt); 5.2 abstention under penalty (H3, in Table 2); 5.3 detector-gated answers (Table 3: greedy, fused, detector-alone, shuffled-expert null, constant-bias at matched yes-rate, disagreement-abstain coverage; H5); PLUS 5.4 general vs medical pair (Table 2b; H2) and Figure 2 (risk-coverage per arm with verbal abstention points); STRETCH 5.5 NGT rows and the 256-token row in Table 2; 5.6 retrieval and synthetic removal preliminary (Table 4a/4b)
  * 6 Discussion (1 p): pixels vs prior reading of FAR_B vs FAR_U vs blind; what the penalised instruction did (or did not) to a 2B model; fusion vs controls; comparison with Lotfinia 2026 (224 px, >= 4B) and Guzel 2026 (3 device-absent cases); implications for the advisor's claims 2 and 3
  * 7 Limitations (0.5 p): non-expert verification; one class, one budget; n; about-15% label discrepancy; contamination 'unknown' for the generalist; no reader; IRB status; pod precision statement
  * 8 Deferred to Stage 2 and Stage 3 (0.25 p): one line per item
  * References (about 40 entries with URLs); Appendix A mapping details and spot check; Appendix B verbatim prompts, parsing regex, JSONL schema, runner settings; Appendix C power note, compute log and cost ledger. Target: CORE 10 pages + appendices (4 exhibits, 11 h writing); PLUS 12 pages (5-6 exhibits, +2 h); STRETCH 14 pages (6 exhibits, +2 h)
cost: RunPod Secure A5000 at $0.27/h: CORE about 4 pod-hours ($1.1), PLUS about 14 cumulative ($3.8), STRETCH about 20 cumulative ($5.4); 50 GB network volume one month $3.50; idle and setup waste allowance $3; Kaggle, GTX 1060, OSF, GitHub $0. Totals: CORE about $5, PLUS about $8, STRETCH about $10; hard ceiling for Stage 1 $15 of the $40 cap, ledger in Appendix C; the $10 minimum top-up plus one $5 top-up covers everything.
-- risks_and_fallbacks --
  * Dimension key not unique or JPEG sizes do not equal OriginalImage[Width,Height]: fall back to pHash alone against all 112k NIH hashes with the same margin rule and one-to-one assignment (minutes in numpy); report the match rate either way
  * Many ambiguous within-patient matches or clean ETT pairs < 120: allow two pairs per patient with clustering, then lower n to 80 with wider intervals; the rung is reported; the design is unchanged
  * Mapping not finished by Day 2 evening: run the hero on Kaggle T4 fp32 directly on the selected NIH files from the attached mirror once the item list exists; MedGemma waits for the pod; worst case the CORE report has the hero plus the MedSigLIP reference as the 'items are detectable' control, stated as a degraded scope
  * Secure A5000 out of stock: Community 3090 at $0.22/h without a volume (weights re-download about 10 min; JSONL synced per session) or a few hours on a secure 4090
  * CheXOne fails to load on the release transformers (card says build from source): NV-Reason-CXR-3B replaces it (same Qwen2.5-VL-3B base, NIH-contaminated, device labels unseen); if both fail, PLUS keeps n = 200 and Figure 2 and drops H2 with one sentence
  * Hero at the floor (NO-GO) or constant-answer: still a complete report; the floor table is the headline, H1 and H3 are reported on MedGemma and the hero's prior rows, Arm 1 is evaluated on MedGemma and on the hero as a 'from the floor' row
  * Verbal abstention never triggered under P1 or P2-hi (2B models ignore the instruction): reported as the finding, with the logit risk-coverage gap (PLUS Figure 2) showing what abstention the logits could support
  * IRB determination not back by Day 6: peer audit reported as pending with the request date; intra-rater kappa stands; model inference on public de-identified data never waits
  * MedSigLIP dev-pool AUROC near 0.5 for ETT: Arm 1 is still run and reported as a null with the AUROC beside it; CheXzero 'support devices' added as a second expert only in STRETCH
  * Kaggle CPU session dies mid-hash: hash tables checkpointed every 10k images to /kaggle/working and resumed
  * CheXzero Google Drive quota failure: MedSigLIP is the sole verifier in the LaMa pilot and the report says so
  * Instructor says the Oct 21 deliverable is not the final report: PLUS and STRETCH items become CORE for the later date; nothing is wasted
  * Time overrun cut order (never touch the 11 h of writing): drop BLIND-grey; drop P1 (keep P0 and P2-hi); reduce n to 80 pairs; replace the dev-pool beta search with beta = 1 and keep only the shuffled-expert control
-- deferred_to_stage2_and_3 --
  * Stage 2: InternVL3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B runs, and the medgemma-4b-it / gemma-3-4b-it appendix pair
  * Stage 2: CVC as the hard class and the full token-budget sweep (H7) with the resolution figure
  * Stage 2: PN, P0b, P2-lo on all runs, the reliability block (split-half, paraphrase agreement, negation consistency) and the mixed-effects model
  * Stage 2: lmms-eval cross-harness agreement check, SLAKE anchor and the released cxr_device_pope task
  * Stage 2: Arm 3 region-crop agree-or-abstain with wrong-region and oracle-polyline controls; plain VCD and VGS-style decoding as reference rows
  * Stage 2: full synthetic-removal arm (LaMa, SD-1.5, RadEdit) behind the automatic gate, 2AFC with non-author raters and a two-reader audit; CPU cut-paste insertion set behind the mirror gate
  * Stage 2: peer-rater kappa if the IRB determination arrives after Oct 21; advisor-brokered clinician reader session
  * Stage 2: public release of the NIH item list, mapping code and dataset card; citation-correction memo to the advisor
  * Stage 3 (cluster access, training): LoRA-DPO on the benchmark's own same-patient pairs; abstention-aware RL with the Kalai penalty as reward; detector-consensus pseudo-labels and synthetic-pair preference data (Antidote/HII-DPO and RULE/MMed-RAG style), each with GPU-hours and licences in a separate plan

  * Tonight: draft the IRB determination request (NCBE v3 Art. 10.19; datasets' own IRB statements; new human data limited to rater labels; no reader in Stage 1); send to IRB@kfupm.edu.sa at 08:00 Oct 13 and log the time

-- issues --
  [1]
    severity: major

    evidence: Design item vs recount: accounts/terms/emails/repo/OSF 2.5 -> 3.5-4.5 (RunPod account, SSH key, volume, OSF registration are each first-time tasks); mapping 5.0 -> 6-9 (Kaggle multiprocessing hash pass over 112k PNG + 30k JPEG is 1-2 h wall-clock plus margin-rule and assignment debugging; the earlier skeptic in digest3.md line 433 counted 8-12 h for the same job); items/prompts/export 1.5 -> 2-2.5; verification 2.5+0.5 -> 3.5-4.5 (calibration period at 45-60 s for the first 40 images; HANDOFF 2b needs a 100-item retest, not 60); pod setup/runner/smoke/queue 3.0 -> 4.5-7 (first pod, two chat templates, first-token logit cache); monitoring 0.5 -> 1; 1060 venv + MedSigLIP + Arm 1 + 3 controls 2.5 -> 3.5-5; statistics + Tables 1-3 + Figure 1 2.0 -> 3.5-5 (patient-clustered BCa needs a cluster jackknife; table-to-template formatting); writing 11 -> 13-16 (10 pages, about 40 references with URLs, three appendices, provenance script, template). Under HANDOFF 2b's agent-execution model (agent writes, runs and debugs all code; student does accounts, emails, verification, spot check, review and approval) the student-hour count is 28-30 h.
    fix: Adopt HANDOFF 2b explicitly: every code item is agent-executed (Kaggle via 'kaggle kernels push' with the competition accepted on the student's account; pod via SSH; 1060 locally) and the 30 h are student hours for human-only steps plus review. If the student must code and debug alone, declare the honest 30-h CORE as Tables 1-2 only (floor, prior, abstention) with Arm 1 as the first PLUS item, cut order: dev pool 500 -> 300, n 120 -> 100 pairs, report 10 -> 8 pages, beta fixed, CheXzero out.
  [2]
    severity: major

    evidence: '1,350 image items per model (450 images x 3 prompts)' but 120 A + 120 B + 120 U = 360 images -> 1,080 items; items_and_verification says '= 740 mapped NIH images' but 360 + 500 dev = 860; Day 2 says 'about 950 NIH PNGs'; verification '270 images' matches 120 pairs, PLUS '7,200 items' matches 600 images x 4 x 3; the 450/1,350 figures are a leftover of a 150-pair draft.

Pod inference for CORE is then about 35-45 min (hero 1.0-1.3 s/item, MedGemma 0.4-0.6 s/item), not a budget item at all; keep 2-4 pod-hours for setup.

Write STAGE1_PLAN.md as a 2-3 page checklist (day lines below, done-when conditions, fallback triggers) and keep this design as reference material.
-- recount_core_hours --
  * Assumption A (design's own: student runs assistant-drafted scripts, debugs, reads, writes; first-time user of Kaggle, RunPod, OSF): accounts, terms screenshots, phone verification, three emails, repo, OSF registration: design 2.5 h -> 3.5-4.5 h
  * Mapping on Kaggle CPU (yield table, dimension key, sharded pHash pass, margin rule, NCC confirmation, one-to-one assignment, patient check, 60-pair spot check, selection): design 5.0 h -> 6-9 h (unattended hash wall-clock 1-2 h not counted)
  * Item set, U stratification, dev pool, prompts.json, PNG export and upload to the pod: design 1.5 h -> 2-2.5 h
  * Verification: reference sheet 0.5 h; 270 images at 25-35 s with a calibration period 2.3-2.8 h; 100-item retest after 48 h 0.8 h: design 3.0 h -> 3.5-4.5 h
  * Pod: account, volume, venv, two models, HF runner with first-token logit cache, 20-item smoke with token asserts, queue script incl. dev-pool P0: design 3.0 h -> 4.5-7 h
  * Run monitoring and JSONL retrieval (pod or Kaggle T4 fallback): design 0.5 h -> 1.0 h
  * GTX 1060 venv, MedSigLIP dev-pool AUROC and tau, label-agreement rows, Arm 1 fusion with detector-alone, shuffled-expert, constant-bias and disagreement-abstain: design 2.5 h -> 3.5-5 h
  * Statistics module (Wilson, patient-clustered bootstrap with cluster jackknife, exact McNemar, Holm), Tables 1-3, Figure 1: design 2.0 h -> 3.5-5 h
  * Writing 8-10 pages plus appendices, about 40 references with URLs, provenance script, template, submission: design 11.0 h -> 13-16 h
  * TOTAL under assumption A: 41-54 h (midpoint about 47 h) against the 30 h CORE budget; the design's CORE is the PLUS budget, the design's PLUS is about 60-65 h, STRETCH 80 h or more
  * Assumption B (HANDOFF 2b agent-execution model: the agent writes, runs and debugs all code and drafts all text; student hours only): accounts/terms/emails 3.5; mapping supervision, spot check, selection review 2.5; export/upload check 0.5; verification plus retest 4.0; pod supervision and smoke review 2.0; run monitoring 1.0; detector/Arm 1 review 1.0; table and figure review 1.5; report editing and approval 11.0; submission and archive 1.0 = 28-30 h: CORE fits only in this model
  * Pod inference time (not a person-time item): hero 1,080 items x 1.0-1.3 s + 300 dev-pool items = 23-30 min; MedGemma 1,380 items x 0.4-0.6 s = 9-14 min; blind rows negligible; setup and debugging 2-4 pod-hours; CORE cost about $1-2 plus the $3.50 volume
  * Kaggle T4 fp32 fallback for the hero: 1,380 items x 2-2.5 s = 46-58 min GPU per three-prompt pass, within the 30 h/week quota; MedGemma has no Kaggle path (fp32 17 GB exceeds a T4)
  * Unattended wall-clock that must be scheduled but not counted: NIH hash pass 1-2 h (Day 1 night), pod queue under 1 h (Day 3 night), MedSigLIP pass about 5 min (Day 4)
verdict: Not feasible as written at 30 h: the CORE re-counts to 41-54 h for a first-time student doing their own debugging and writing, with arithmetic slips (450 vs 360 images; 740 vs 860 PNGs), a missing dependency (Arm 1 tunes beta on dev-pool VLM logits that no run produces), a mapping fallback that presupposes the mapping, and all writing stacked after the results. It becomes feasible at 30 student-hours only under HANDOFF 2b's agent-execution model with the fixes applied: counts corrected, dev-pool P0 added and beta = 1 pre-registered, U stratified for H1, a Day-3-noon kill switch to a RANZCR-direct Kaggle fallback (competition data stays on Kaggle), results-independent sections drafted from Day 1, the 100-item retest and automatic-verifier agreement (reported only, never used for exclusion) replacing the unavailable peer, and an 8-9 page report. With those changes a complete paper-format report with Tables 1-3 and Figure 1 exists on Oct 21 even if the pod is down for a day (hero on Kaggle T4 fp32, MedGemma deferred and stated) or the mapping takes twice as long (RANZCR-direct pairs without time order, mapping reported as partial). Ethics sequence (IRB email 08:00 Oct 13 before any notebook; inference on public de-identified data does not wait), data terms (pod holds NIH PNGs and prompt text only; no RANZCR rows released) and wording rules are otherwise compliant. Cost is irrelevant (under $6); person-time and the mapping are the two binding constraints. If the student must code and debug without an agent, the honest 30-h CORE is Tables 1-2 only and Arm 1 becomes the first PLUS item.
-- revised_core_scope --
  * Execution model: every code item (mapping notebook pushed through the Kaggle API, pod runner over SSH, 1060 detector pass, statistics, table and figure scripts, report drafts) is agent-executed; the student's 30 hours cover accounts and terms, the three emails, the 60-pair spot check, blind verification plus the 100-item retest, supervision of runs, review of every number, editing and approval of the report.
  * Runs: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (hero, min_pixels = max_pixels = 1,048,576, 1,024 visual tokens asserted) and google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (256 tokens), bf16 on a Secure Cloud RTX A5000 with a 50 GB network volume; Kaggle T4 fp32 is the hero's fallback and MedGemma is deferred and stated if the pod is unavailable.
  * Items: 120 ETT pairs (A present, B the same patient's nearest frontal study with ETT absent per RANZCR, one pair per patient, direction and gap logged) + 120 U from other patients, ETT absent, stratified 60 with at least one other labelled line and 60 with none + a 300-image patient-disjoint dev pool (150/150, RANZCR labels only) = 540 NIH PNGs, 360 test images; the yield table from train.csv decides the rung (two pairs per patient with clustering, then n = 80) before any model output.
  * Arms and prompts: A, B, U, BLIND-text and BLIND-grey under P0 binary, P1 unpenalised 'cannot tell' and P2-hi (Kalai t = 0.9, penalty 9) = 1,080 image items per model, plus P0 on the 300 dev-pool images per model and the blind rows; greedy, max_new_tokens 16, no system prompt, first-token yes/no/abstain log-probabilities cached, per-item JSONL.
  * Mitigation Arm 1 on both runs: z' = z + beta * clip(log(s/(1-s)), -5, 5) with MedSigLIP CCD-prompt ETT probability s, beta = 1 pre-registered as the primary row and the dev-pool-selected beta as a sensitivity row; controls detector-alone (tau at Youden on the dev pool), shuffled-expert (100 permutations), constant-bias curve and disagreement-abstain coverage; zero extra pod time beyond the dev-pool P0 run.
  * Verification: the student reads all 120 B, 120 U and 30 random A blind to model outputs with a one-page ETT reference sheet (ambiguous, tracheostomy and out-of-field flags), re-reads 100 items after at least 48 hours (Cohen's kappa), and label-image disagreements are re-matched first and excluded as label noise second; MedSigLIP agreement with the RANZCR label is reported per arm but never used for exclusion; no peer, stated as a limitation and never called an inter-rater audit.
  * Statistics: n and a Wilson 95% interval on every proportion; patient-clustered bootstrap (9,999; cluster = NIH Patient ID; BCa with a cluster jackknife, percentile as the documented fallback) for differences; exact McNemar for paired indicators; one pre-registered primary test each for H1 (yes-rate on U with vs without another labelled line), H3 (abstain indicator P2-hi vs P1 over B and U, per model) and H5 (paired balanced-accuracy gain fused vs greedy on A and B, with the shuffled-expert p-value); H0 floor gate reported as a finding; cells under n = 30 get intervals only.
  * Exhibits: Table 1 benchmark construction (match rate, ambiguous share, yield, realised pairs and U, follow-up gap, other-line profile, verification agreement, exclusions, retest kappa, MedSigLIP agreement), Table 2 models x A/B/U x prompts (hit_A, FAR_B, FAR_U, persistence, balanced accuracy, yes-rate, abstention, ER_0.9, blind P(yes)), Table 3 Arm 1 vs controls, Figure 1 (arrow-marked example pair plus per-arm yes-rate panel with Wilson bars).
  * Report: 8-9 pages in paper format (introduction, related work, benchmark, protocol, results, discussion, limitations, one-line deferrals to Stage 2 and Stage 3) plus script-generated Appendices A-C; Sections 1, 2, 4, Appendix B and the reference list are drafted from Day 1 and only Sections 3, 5, 6 and 7 wait for numbers; every number in the prose is matched to a table cell by script.
  * Ethics and terms: IRB determination request to IRB@kfupm.edu.sa at 08:00 Oct 13 (NCBE v3 Art. 10.19) logged before any notebook is opened; inference on public de-identified NIH images proceeds; any lab-rater session waits for the determination or the advisor's umbrella approval; the pod holds NIH PNGs and prompt text only; RANZCR JPEGs, CSVs and polylines never leave Kaggle and the student's machine; no RANZCR rows in any release; NIH attribution trio, 'RANZCR CLiP labels (43 trained labellers)', 'within-patient contrast', 'to our knowledge' and the MedGemma card wording used throughout.
  * Fallbacks with triggers: Day-3 noon with no clean pair list -> RANZCR-direct pairs from train.csv (B random, direction unknown) with the hero on Kaggle T4 fp32 on JPEGs resized to 1024x1024 inside Kaggle, MedSigLIP on Kaggle, MedGemma when the mapping lands; pod unavailable on Day 3 -> hero on Kaggle T4 fp32 on the selected NIH PNGs, MedGemma on Day 4 or 5 or 'not run'; hero at the floor -> floor table is the headline and Arm 1 is reported from the floor; abstention never triggered -> reported as the finding; cut order never touching the writing: dev pool 300 -> 200, n 120 -> 80, drop BLIND-grey, drop P1.
  * PLUS (+15 h) adds, each without changing a CORE cell: CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B (H2) with NV-Reason-CXR-3B as the fallback, n = 200 pairs + 200 U, the four-question block, P2-lo, BLIND-demo, CheXzero as the second automatic verifier, Figure 2 risk-coverage; STRETCH (+15 h) adds the NGT class, the 512-px row, Arm 2 retrieval and the LaMa removal pilot.
-- revised_day_by_day --
  * Day 0, Mon Oct 12 (tonight, 1.5 h student): email the instructor (what exactly is due Oct 21, template, page limit, appendices) and the advisor (umbrella IRB number, lab rater if any, about $15 spend, one-paragraph Stage 1 scope); IRB request drafted and queued for 08:00; Kaggle late-join of ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification with the Rules tab screenshotted and the Data Access/Use and Data Security clauses pasted into PROTOCOL.md; Kaggle phone verification; HAI-DEF accepted for medgemma-1.5-4b-it and medsiglip-448, HF read token stored as a Kaggle secret. Agent: mapping notebook, kernel-metadata.json, Section 2 draft from the digests. Done when: emails sent, rules clauses in PROTOCOL.md, HAI-DEF accepted, notebook ready to push.
  * Day 1, Tue Oct 13 (class day, 3.0 h): 08:00 send the IRB email and log the time before any notebook runs; push the Kaggle CPU notebook: yield table from train.csv (ETT present per image, patients with both states, U strata, dev candidates), dimension-key test on 200 JPEGs, then the sharded pHash pass (12 NIH folders committed as output versions, RANZCR JPEGs as one shard) running unattended overnight; RunPod account with $10 credit and the 50 GB Secure Cloud volume; OSF account; agent builds the 1060 venv (torch 2.14 cu126) and tests one MedSigLIP embedding. Done when: yield_table.csv shows at least 120 patients with both ETT states (else choose the rung), key result recorded, hash shards committing, 1060 imports MedSigLIP.
  * Day 2, Wed Oct 14 (class day, 3.5 h): match (dimension key -> pHash with the margin rule and an empirically calibrated cut -> 128-px NCC confirmation -> one-to-one assignment -> one NIH patient per RANZCR patient; log match rate, ambiguous count and within-patient share); 60-pair visual spot check; select 120 pairs, 120 U (60/60 strata), 300 dev; hash items.csv; zip 540 NIH PNGs to local and the pod; prompts.json with P0/P1/P2-hi verbatim; OSF registration (1 h: H0 gate, H1 on U, H3, H5 with beta = 1, n and rungs, exclusion rules, verifier-agreement reported only); agent drafts Section 4 and Appendix B. Done when: mapping_stats.csv, items.csv (hashed), PNG zip exist; OSF timestamp precedes any model output.
  * Day 3, Thu Oct 15 (class day, 3.0 h): NOON KILL SWITCH: no clean pair list -> RANZCR-direct fallback on Kaggle (pairs from train.csv, hero on T4 fp32 inside Kaggle, MedGemma deferred); otherwise pod: Secure A5000, HF_HOME on the volume, one venv on the latest transformers (Qwen3-VL needs >= 4.57) plus qwen-vl-utils and accelerate, download both models, upload the PNGs, runner with cached first-token logits, 20-item smoke per model asserting 1,024 and 256 visual tokens and logging s/item, BLIND-text and BLIND-grey rows, nohup queue of P0/P1/P2-hi on A/B/U plus P0 on the dev pool for both models (under 1 h); pod unavailable -> hero on Kaggle T4 fp32 on the selected NIH PNGs; student edits the Section 1-2 drafts 0.5 h. Done when: smoke logged, queue running or finished, JSONL syncing, Sections 1-2 in first draft.
  * Day 4, Fri Oct 16 (weekend, 4.0 h): ETT reference sheet (0.5 h); blind verification of 120 B, 120 U, 30 A at 25-35 s with flags, saved and timestamped BEFORE any JSONL is opened (2.5 h); disagreements re-matched first, then excluded as label noise; agent runs MedSigLIP on the 540 images (dev AUROC with bootstrap CI, Youden tau, agreement with RANZCR labels per arm, reported only); pull and parse the JSONL, hand-check 100 outputs, count unparsable and truncated (0.5 h); Table 1 draft (0.5 h). Done when: verified_items.csv with exclusion counts, detector_scores.csv, parsed_results.csv for both models, Table 1 draft.
  * Day 5, Sat Oct 17 (weekend, 4.0 h): stats.py (Wilson, patient-clustered bootstrap with cluster jackknife, exact McNemar, Holm, design effect); Table 2 with the H0 classification, H1 (U strata) and H3 tests; Figure 1 (arrow-marked pair plus yes-rate panel); Arm 1 with beta = 1 primary, dev-selected beta sensitivity, detector-alone, shuffled-expert (100 permutations), constant-bias curve, disagreement-abstain coverage, H5 test, Table 3; agent begins Sections 3 and 5 from the CSVs; a Day-3 slip lands here with Sections 1, 2, 4 already drafted. Done when: Tables 1-3 and Figure 1 generated by script with n and a CI on every cell.
  * Day 6, Sun Oct 18 (class day, 3.0 h): 100-item intra-rater retest at least 48 h after Day 4, Cohen's kappa into Table 1 (0.8 h); student edits Sections 1-3 to near-final (2.2 h); if the IRB determination or umbrella approval and a lab rater exist, schedule that session for PLUS, otherwise write 'pending (requested Oct 13)'. Done when: Sections 1-3 near-final with Table 1 and kappa in place.
  * Day 7, Mon Oct 19 (class day, 3.0 h): Sections 4 (protocol, verbatim prompts, decoding, parsing, metrics, statistics, OSF link, ethics and data-terms statement with the IRB date) and 5.1-5.3 (floor and prior with Table 2 and Figure 1; abstention; Arm 1 with Table 3) to near-final; limitations list started; every exhibit referenced in the text. Done when: Sections 4-5 near-final.
  * Day 8, Tue Oct 20 (mid-term break, 3.5 h): Section 6 (pixels vs prior; the penalised instruction on a 2B model; fusion vs controls; position against Lotfinia 2026, Guzel 2026, TAIX-VQA, McLaughlin 2026), Section 7 (non-expert verification, one class and budget, n, 15% label discrepancy, contamination 'unknown', no reader, IRB status, no second rater), Section 8 (one line per Stage 2 and Stage 3 item), abstract, Appendices A-C; provenance script matches every prose number to a table cell; draft to the advisor. Done when: complete draft, all numbers traced, references complete with URLs.
  * Day 9, Wed Oct 21 (break, submission, 1.5 h): full read-through, advisor comments applied, course template, submit; stop the pod, archive the volume to local, close the cost ledger (Appendix C), push code and the NIH filename item list to the private repo, tag stage1-submitted. Done when: report submitted, ledger closed, repo tagged.
